# Decisions

Append-only log of decisions made while building Transcoda.

## 2026-10-16 M0 tooling

pnpm 12.6.x, Biome 2.5, Vitest 5, Vite 8. TypeScript version: see the Task 2 entry (7.0.2 is installed by the scaffold and may change in Task 2).

- Renovate: `renovate.json` is in the repo, but the Renovate GitHub App must be enabled for the org by the author (manual step, not part of this plan).
- Root script `ci` renamed to `check`: `pnpm ci` is a pnpm 12 built-in (clean install with frozen lockfile), so a script of that name would be shadowed. `pnpm check` runs typecheck + lint + check-deps + test. Later tasks and CI use `pnpm check`.
- Biome 2.5 deprecates `linter.rules.recommended: true`; `biome.json` uses `linter.rules.preset: "recommended"` (verified against the installed configuration schema). `files.includes` also excludes `.superpowers` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`.
- Root `tsconfig.json` is a solution file using `"include": []` plus `references`. TypeScript 7 rejects `"files": []` with TS18002; `"include": []` with no references yet makes `tsc -b` succeed.
- pnpm 12 blocks dependency build scripts by default (ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS). `esbuild`'s postinstall is denied (`allowBuilds: esbuild: false` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`); its platform binary arrives via optional dependencies, so it is not needed.
- Vitest 5: `test.projects: ['packages/*', 'apps/*']` works with no per-project configs yet; the placeholder packages become implicit projects and `passWithNoTests: true` gives exit code 0 ("No test files found, exiting with code 0").
- `@types/node` pinned to 24.19.2 (latest 24.x at install time).

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 2: TypeScript 7 vs 6

**Decision: TypeScript 7.0.2 (exact pin, root `devDependencies`). No fallback to 6.x needed.**

- Probe (`.local/ts-spike`, standalone package, `pnpm install --ignore-workspace`, since deleted) type-checked with `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` (tsc 7.0.2): exit 0, no errors. A deliberate type error was reported correctly, so the probe really type-checks the oRPC types.
- Config: the Task 1 base options (`strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `isolatedModules`, `skipLibCheck`, ES2024) plus `jsx: react-jsx`, `module: ESNext`, `moduleResolution: bundler`, `lib: [ES2024, DOM]`. No deprecated options used.
- Gotcha: with `moduleResolution: bundler`, `import './x.ts'` fails with TS5097 unless `allowImportingTsExtensions` is set; use `./x.js` (resolves to `x.ts`) as in NodeNext.
- Probe imports (to repeat the spike when Renovate proposes a new TS major): `oc`, `ContractRouterClient` (`@orpc/contract`); `createORPCClient` (`@orpc/client`); `RPCLink` (`@orpc/client/fetch`); `implement`, `os` (`@orpc/server`); `RPCHandler` (`@orpc/server/fetch`); `OpenAPIHandler` (`@orpc/openapi/fetch`); `OpenAPIGenerator` (`@orpc/openapi`); `ZodToJsonSchemaConverter` (`@orpc/zod/zod4`); `z` (`zod`); `ReactFlow`, `Node`, `Edge`, `useNodesState` (`@xyflow/react`); `Hono` (`hono`); `better-sqlite3` default import with `@types/better-sqlite3`; React JSX (`<ReactFlow nodes edges />`). Versions: @orpc/* 1.15.7, zod 4.6.7, @xyflow/react 12.12.0, hono 4.13.13, better-sqlite3 13.0.3 (types only; its install script stays blocked), react 19.3.1.
- Deferred: the brief's `tsc -b --dry` check of project references cannot run because no packages exist yet (root `tsconfig.json` has `references: []`). To be done in Task 5 when the first package with `composite` exists.
- No change to root `package.json` (already `typescript: 7.0.2`) or `pnpm-workspace.yaml`.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 3: password hashing in Node 24

**Decision: argon2id via the built-in `node:crypto`.**

- `node --version` = v24.21.0. `node -e "const c=require('node:crypto'); console.log(process.version, typeof c.argon2, typeof c.argon2Sync)"` printed `v24.21.0 function function`. No experimental flag is needed, so none is set (`node --help | grep -i argon` also lists nothing).
- Parameters: memory 64 MiB (`m=65536` KiB), passes 3, parallelism 1, 32-byte tag, 16-byte random salt. Stored as a PHC string `$argon2id$v=19$m=65536,t=3,p=1$<salt b64>$<hash b64>` (unpadded base64).
- API shape verified by running it: `crypto.argon2Sync('argon2id', { message, nonce: salt, parallelism: 1, tagLength: 32, memory: 65536, passes: 3 })` returns a 32-byte Buffer, is deterministic for the same inputs, and the callback form `crypto.argon2(...)` also works. One hash took about 110 ms on the dev machine. Note the salt is passed as `nonce`. Use the async `argon2` in the server so the event loop is not blocked.
- The scrypt fallback is not needed. Task 10 implements argon2id; the PHC prefix is self-describing so another verifier can be added later.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 4: oRPC wiring (verified against @orpc/* 1.15.7 and a running spike)

Spike run in a standalone package under `.local/` (since deleted) (hono `app.request`, contract + `implement` + `RPCHandler` + `OpenAPIHandler` + generator); everything below was observed, not assumed.

**Packages and exact imports** (all pinned 1.15.7; `@orpc/zod` has peer deps `@orpc/contract`, `@orpc/server`, `zod`):
- `import { oc, type ContractRouterClient } from '@orpc/contract'`
- `import { implement, os } from '@orpc/server'`
- `import { RPCHandler } from '@orpc/server/fetch'`
- `import { ResponseHeadersPlugin, type ResponseHeadersPluginContext, SimpleCsrfProtectionHandlerPlugin } from '@orpc/server/plugins'`
- `import { OpenAPIHandler } from '@orpc/openapi/fetch'`
- `import { OpenAPIGenerator } from '@orpc/openapi'`
- `import { ZodToJsonSchemaConverter } from '@orpc/zod/zod4'`
- `import { SmartCoercionPlugin } from '@orpc/json-schema'` (separate package, must be a direct dependency of whoever imports it)
- `import { createORPCClient, isDefinedError, ORPCError } from '@orpc/client'`; `import { RPCLink } from '@orpc/client/fetch'`

**Contract / implementer (Step 1, confirmed):** `oc.route({ method, path, tags, summary }).input(zod).output(zod).errors({ CODE: { status, message, data: zodSchema } })`; router is a plain nested object; `implement(contract).$context<Ctx>()`; `impl.proc.handler(fn)`; `impl.use(mw)`; `impl.router({...})` (nested routers are plain objects of handlers, `impl.auth.login.handler`). In handlers `errors.CODE({ data })` throws the typed error. `ContractRouterClient<typeof contract>` works.

**Handlers (Step 2, confirmed):** both `RPCHandler` and `OpenAPIHandler` have `.handle(request, { prefix, context })` returning `{ matched, response }` (response typed possibly undefined when unmatched; narrow on `matched`). `prefix` for RPC is `/rpc`, for REST `/api/v1`.

**Response headers (Set-Cookie):** `ResponseHeadersPlugin` exists in `@orpc/server/plugins`. Register it in the handler's `plugins: [new ResponseHeadersPlugin()]`; the context type must include `ResponseHeadersPluginContext` (`{ resHeaders?: Headers }`). The plugin injects a `Headers` into `context.resHeaders` and merges it into the response, so a procedure does `context.resHeaders?.append('set-cookie', '...')`. Verified: both `/api/v1/auth/login` (REST) and `/rpc/auth/login` (RPC) returned `set-cookie: tcd_session=abc; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax; Path=/` through the Hono middleware. The fallback (own Headers object in context) is not needed. Use `append` for multiple cookies.

**Hono adapter (~15 lines, verified):**
```ts
const rpc = new RPCHandler<Ctx>(router, { plugins: [new ResponseHeadersPlugin()] })
const rest = new OpenAPIHandler<Ctx>(router, {
  plugins: [new ResponseHeadersPlugin(), new SmartCoercionPlugin({ schemaConverters: [new ZodToJsonSchemaConverter()] })],
})
const app = new Hono()
app.use('/rpc/*', async (c, next) => {
  const { matched, response } = await rpc.handle(c.req.raw, { prefix: '/rpc', context: buildCtx(c) })
  return matched ? c.newResponse(response.body, response) : next()
})
app.use('/api/v1/*', async (c, next) => {
  const { matched, response } = await rest.handle(c.req.raw, { prefix: '/api/v1', context: buildCtx(c) })
  return matched ? c.newResponse(response.body, response) : next()
})
```

**OpenAPI generation (Step 3):** `new OpenAPIGenerator({ schemaConverters: [new ZodToJsonSchemaConverter()] })`, then `await generator.generate(contract, { info: { title, version }, servers: [{ url: '/api/v1' }], security: [{ bearerAuth: [] }], components: { securitySchemes: { bearerAuth: { type: 'http', scheme: 'bearer' } } } })`. The generate options are `Partial<Omit<OpenAPI.Document, 'openapi'>>` (plus `filter`, `commonSchemas`, `customErrorResponseBodySchema`), so `components.securitySchemes` and top-level `security` are passed straight into the document (verified in output). Per-operation overrides are possible with `oo.spec(...)` from `@orpc/openapi` (not needed; public routes like login/setup can set `security: []` that way). No plugin needed.
- Correction to the plan's assumption: `OpenAPIHandler` does NOT take schema converters. GET query/path params arrive as strings; to parse them, either use `z.coerce.*` in the contract or register `SmartCoercionPlugin({ schemaConverters: [new ZodToJsonSchemaConverter()] })` from `@orpc/json-schema` in the handler's `plugins`. Verified: `z.number().int()` on `GET /g?n=5` returns 400 "expected number, received string" without the plugin and 200 `{"n":5}` with it. Plan: use the plugin on the REST handler (`@orpc/zod/zod4` also exports a deprecated `experimental_ZodSmartCoercionPlugin`; do not use it).
- Spec for a GET with an `n` input has query parameter `n` (`in: query`). Server URL `/api/v1` appears in `servers`; paths in the document are relative to it (`/ping`, `/auth/login`).

**Client (Step 4):** `createORPCClient<ContractRouterClient<typeof contract>>(new RPCLink({ url, headers: () => ({...}), fetch? }))`; `fetch` option confirmed (used to route into `app.request`). Same-origin cookies need nothing extra. Errors: a contract-defined error is an `ORPCError` where `isDefinedError(e)` is true; `e.code` (`'TEAPOT'`), `e.status` (418), `e.data` (`{ why: 'unlucky' }`) observed on both RPC client and REST body (`{"defined":true,"code":"TEAPOT","status":418,"message":"I am a teapot","data":{...}}`).

**CSRF plugin (noted, not used):** `SimpleCsrfProtectionHandlerPlugin` in `@orpc/server/plugins`; defaults header `x-csrf-token` = `orpc` (options `headerName`, `headerValue`, `exclude`, `error`). Without the header it rejects with `CSRF_TOKEN_MISMATCH`, status 403 (verified). Transcoda uses its own session-bound CSRF token (Task 11) instead.

## Task 5: shared, sdk, dependency rule

- **Source-only packages (Decision):** `shared` and `sdk` export `./src/index.ts` directly (`"exports": { ".": "./src/index.ts" }`). No per-package build step in M0; consumers are tsx, Vite and the server bundler.
- **Dependency rule enforcement:** pnpm's non-hoisted layout plus `scripts/check-deps.mjs` enforce `shared <- sdk <- contract <- server/web` (allowlist per package; `sdk` may depend only on `@transcoda/shared` and has no third-party deps in M0; `sdk/src` may import only `@transcoda/shared`). TypeScript project references mirror it: each `tsconfig.json` `references` lists only its allowed deps. Verified by temporarily adding `@transcoda/contract` to sdk (exit 1, names sdk) and a bad import in `sdk/src` (exit 1).
- **TS 7 project references (deferred Task 2 check, confirmed):** `tsc -b` with project references works under TS 7.0.2. The root `tsconfig.json` is a solution file (`include: []`, `references` to shared and sdk). `noEmit: true` does NOT work for referenced composite projects (TS6310 "Referenced project may not disable emit"), so each package uses `composite: true`, `emitDeclarationOnly: true`, `outDir: dist`, `tsBuildInfoFile: dist/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (dist is gitignored). `allowImportingTsExtensions` is needed for `.ts` import specifiers (valid because emit is declarations only). Incremental `tsc -b --dry` reports projects up to date on re-run.
- **Test config:** no per-package Vitest config needed; the root `test.projects: ['packages/*', 'apps/*']` picks up directories without config. The sdk `test` script uses `--passWithNoTests` since it has no tests in M0.
- **Aggregate script:** `pnpm check` (pnpm 12 reserves `ci`).

## Task 6: packages/contract

- **Source-only package, project references:** same shape as shared/sdk; `references` to shared and sdk, added to root `tsconfig.json`. Dependencies pinned exactly: `@orpc/contract` 1.15.7, `zod` 4.6.7 (latest on npm at the time).
- **Error codes defined once, attached per procedure (Decision):** `src/errors.ts` holds a single `errorDefs` map; procedures compose the subset they can raise via `pickErrors(...)` and the shared sets `authedErrors` (UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN) and `mutationErrors` (+ CSRF_FAILED). Mapping:
  - `system.health`: none (public). `system.capabilities`: UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN.
  - `auth.setup`: SETUP_ALREADY_DONE. `auth.login`: INVALID_CREDENTIALS, RATE_LIMITED. HOST_NOT_ALLOWED stays in `errorDefs` but is raised by the Hono host-check middleware (plain 403 JSON), not by procedures. `auth.logout`: UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, CSRF_FAILED. `auth.me`: none (public).
  - `tokens.create`: UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, CSRF_FAILED. `tokens.list`: UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN. `tokens.revoke`: UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, CSRF_FAILED, NOT_FOUND.
  - RATE_LIMITED carries data `{ retryAfterSeconds: number }`.
- **Outputs chosen where the plan was silent:** `auth.setup` and `auth.login` return `{ user }` (`AuthUserOutputSchema`); `auth.logout` returns `{ ok: true }`. Timestamps use `z.iso.datetime()` (UTC `Z` form only).
- **12-character password minimum** (per plan) lives in `CredentialsSchema`.

## Task 8: SQLite open, migrations, backups

- **better-sqlite3 13.0.3 needs no install script (Decision):** the package ships prebuilt binaries in `prebuilds/` (incl. darwin-arm64, linux-x64/arm64, musl). With `allowBuilds: { better-sqlite3: true }` pnpm 12 runs its implicit `node-gyp rebuild` (because of `binding.gyp`) and fails without node-gyp. So `pnpm-workspace.yaml` sets `better-sqlite3: false`; the prebuilt binary loads and works (verified).
- **`@types/better-sqlite3` is 9.6.0 (latest on npm) while the runtime is 13.0.3:** types cover every API used here (`prepare`, `exec`, `pragma`, `transaction`); `backup()` is typed but unused (we use `VACUUM INTO` via `exec`). No mismatch hit.
- **Backups (Decision):** file `transcoda-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>-v<fromVersion>.sqlite` in local time; made only when upgrading an existing schema (`current > 0`); pruned to `keepBackups` (default 5) right after each backup. A second backup within the same second would fail (`VACUUM INTO` refuses existing files); acceptable since startup runs once. *(Superseded 2026-10-21: a counter suffix avoids the clash.)*
- **Schema-too-new:** `SchemaTooNewError` message names the newest `transcoda-*.sqlite` path in `backupDir` (or "no backup found"). The check runs before any backup or migration.
- **Validation:** migrations must be contiguous 1..N with no duplicates, checked before the DB is touched (not even `schema_migrations` is created).

## Task 7: apps/server skeleton

- **Context shape (Decision):** `ServerContext.resHeaders` is optional (`resHeaders?: Headers`) so it is compatible with `ResponseHeadersPluginContext`; the plugin injects it per request, `buildCtx` does not set it. `AppDeps` is `{ config, db, version }` for now; single definition in `src/rpc/context.ts`. `TokenScope` is imported from `@transcoda/shared` (contract does not re-export it).
- **Unimplemented procedures** throw `ORPCError('NOT_IMPLEMENTED')` (501) until their task.
- **Dependencies:** `@orpc/json-schema` and `@orpc/client` (tests) added as exact-pinned direct deps; `@hono/node-server` 2.1.5, `hono` 4.13.13. `serveStatic` takes `root` relative to cwd, so the web dir is `path.resolve`d; SPA fallback uses `serveStatic({ root, path: 'index.html' })` for GETs outside `/api` and `/rpc`.
- **tsx works without esbuild's install script** (`esbuild: false` in `allowBuilds` unchanged; dev server verified).
- **OpenAPI JSON key order:** the document begins `{"info":...`; `openapi: "3.1.x"` is present but not the first key.
- **Version:** `VERSION` is `process.env.TRANSCODA_VERSION ?? '0.0.0-dev'` for now (no bundler define yet).

## Task 9: Migration 0001 and repositories

- **IDs (Decision):** random UUID v4 via `crypto.randomUUID()` (brief's "v7" wording was a typo).
- **Timestamps:** ISO-8601 UTC from `Date#toISOString()` (satisfies `z.iso.datetime()`); `deleteExpiredSessions` removes rows with `expires_at <= now`.
- **`createTestDb()`** uses `openDatabase(':memory:')` so `foreign_keys = ON` and cascades are real.
- **`revokeToken`** returns false for unknown, foreign, or already-revoked tokens (keeps the original `revoked_at`).
- **Extra index** `api_tokens_user_id` added for `listTokens`. `getSetting` throws if the stored JSON fails the schema.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 10: secret key file and password hashing

- PHC strings use unpadded standard base64 (`+` `/`, no `=`) for salt and hash, per the PHC convention. `PASSWORD_ALGO = 'argon2id'` (see Task 3). `verifyPassword` also verifies `$scrypt$N=<n>,r=<r>,p=<p>$<salt>$<hash>` (hash length taken from the stored hash) and never throws: garbage, unknown algorithms, malformed or out-of-range params (argon2 m up to 2^21 KiB, t up to 20, p up to 16; scrypt N a power of two up to 2^20) return `false`.
- `needsRehash` is true for scrypt, unknown or unparseable strings, and argon2id with m/t/p or tag length different from the current ones.
- `loadOrCreateSecretKey(configDir, fromEnvHex, warn = no-op)`: env or file content must be exactly 64 hex chars after trim, else a `ConfigError` (message never contains the key material). A file mode broader than 0600 (`mode & 0o077`) calls `warn` with the path only and does not fail. If the `wx` create loses a race (EEXIST) the existing file is read.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 11: sessions, cookies and CSRF tokens

- **Cookies:** hand-written `sessionCookie`/`clearSessionCookie`/`readCookie` (about 30 lines) instead of `hono/cookie`; its `serialize`/`parse` are in `hono/utils/cookie` and enforce prefix/Max-Age rules we do not need, and a plain-string module keeps auth independent of the framework. `Expires` uses `Date#toUTCString()`; the clear cookie also sets `Expires` to the epoch alongside `Max-Age=0`.
- **Sessions:** `verify` rejects ids not matching `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$` without querying the DB; expiry is compared as ISO strings; sliding update happens only when `now - last_seen_at > 1 h`. Added repo function `deleteSessionsForUser` for `revokeAllForUser`.
- **CSRF (Decision):** stateless session-bound HMAC-SHA256 token (base64url over `'csrf:' + sessionIdHash`) sent in `X-CSRF-Token` on every non-GET request; required for cookie-authenticated mutating requests on `/rpc` and `/api/v1`; bearer-token requests are exempt. Comparison uses `timingSafeEqual` on equal-length buffers only.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 12: host checks and trusted proxies

- **Hosts (Decision):** hostnames are compared without ports; allowed = settings `allowed_hosts` ∪ `config.allowedHosts` ∪ `ALWAYS_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, which `checkHost`/`checkOrigin` also accept on their own). A corrupt `allowed_hosts` setting is treated as empty. The check applies only under `/api` and `/rpc` and is skipped while no users exist (first visit reaches setup).
- **`clientIp`:** walks `X-Forwarded-For` right to left from a trusted `remoteAddr` and returns the first untrusted hop; a non-IP hop makes it fall back to `remoteAddr`. `isHttps` honours the first `X-Forwarded-Proto` value only from a trusted proxy.
- **Errors:** `app.onError` returns `{ error: 'INTERNAL' }` (500) for `/api` and `/rpc`, logging only the error message.

## Task 14: login rate limiter
- **Decision:** in-memory only (single process, no persistence). `check` returns the longest remaining lockout among blocked keys (ceil, >= 1 ms). Backoff level (`consecutiveLockouts`) resets on `recordSuccess` and when a key has been idle (no failures, unlocked) for longer than `maxLockoutMs`; `prune()` drops keys with an empty window and expired lockout, retaining backoff memory until that idle time has passed.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 15: auth middleware and auth.* procedures
- **Deps wiring (Decision):** `src/deps.ts` `createAppDeps({ config, db, secretKey, version, now? })` builds `AppDeps` (adds `secretKey`, `sessions`, `tokens`, `limiter`, `setupRequired`, and `now`, a clock shared by the services so tests can advance time). Used by `main.ts` and `src/test-app.ts` (`createTestApp`). The host-check middleware now uses `deps.setupRequired()`.
- **Auth resolution:** `resolveAuth` runs once per `/rpc` and `/api/v1` request before the oRPC handler. Only an `Authorization` header with the `Bearer` scheme counts as a bearer attempt (other schemes fall through to the cookie); an invalid bearer yields `none` with no cookie fallback. A present-but-invalid session cookie is cleared by `app.ts` unless the response already sets `tcd_session` (login/setup), so a fresh cookie is never followed by a clearing one.
- **Middlewares:** `os` (the contract implementer) lives in `rpc/middleware.ts`. Mutation = contract route method other than GET (missing method counts as POST). Middleware `errors` is typed as the union of every procedure's map, so middlewares build contract errors from `errorDefs` with `defined: true` (identical to what the `errors.X()` proxy produces); handlers use `errors.X()`.
- **CSRF for cookie mutations:** Origin/Referer must be an allowed host, or absent with `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin|none`; plus the session-bound `X-CSRF-Token`. Requests without Origin/Referer and without Sec-Fetch-Site (e.g. curl with a cookie) are rejected; scripts should use API tokens.
- **Login:** limiter keys `ip:<clientIp>` and `user:<lowercased username>`; `retryAfterSeconds = ceil(retryAfterMs/1000)`. Unknown users are verified against a lazily computed, cached argon2id hash of a random password (warmed at startup in `main.ts`). `needsRehash` triggers `updatePasswordHash` (new repo function). An existing session on the request is deleted before the new one is issued.
- **Setup:** password hashed before the transaction; the transaction re-checks `countUsers` and also records the Host hostname in `allowed_hosts` (dedup, always-allowed hosts skipped, corrupt setting treated as empty); a UNIQUE violation also maps to `SETUP_ALREADY_DONE`.
- **system.capabilities** already enforces `requireScope('read')` (401/403), body still NOT_IMPLEMENTED until Task 17.
- **onError:** `HTTPException` responses pass through before the JSON 500 branch.
- **main.ts (placeholder until Task 20):** opens `<configDir>/transcoda.db`, runs migrations (backups to `<configDir>/backups`), loads/creates the secret key.

- **Task 14 fix round 1 (rate limiter):** `recordFailure` is a no-op for a key while locked; idleness is measured from the latest of last failure and lockout end; `prune()` trims stale failures before deciding, so keys with stale failures no longer leak.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 17: system.capabilities
- **Capabilities wiring (Decision):** `createAppDeps` accepts an injected `capabilities?: Capabilities` (the startup probe result, to be passed by `main.ts` in Task 20) and exposes it as `AppDeps.capabilities()`. Until then it defaults to `{ available: false, path: 'ffmpeg', error: 'ffmpeg has not been probed' }`. `createTestApp({ capabilities })` injects a fixed value.
- **Probe:** `probeFfmpeg(path, exec?)` runs `-hide_banner -version|-encoders|-hwaccels` (10 s timeout, 4 MiB buffer) and never throws; any failure or unparseable output becomes `available:false` with the error message. Fixtures come from Homebrew ffmpeg 9.0.2; the version fixture omits the `configuration:` line (contains local paths).

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 15 fix round 1: login burst, same-origin setup/login, sliding cookie
- **Login attempt reservation (Decision):** `login` calls `limiter.recordFailure(keys)` right after a passing `check` (no await in between), before `verifyPassword`, and `recordSuccess(keys)` on success. A parallel burst therefore cannot all pass `check`; at most 5 attempts per window reach the verify. Failures are not recorded a second time.
- **Same-origin guard on setup and login (Decision):** new `requireSameOrigin` middleware (`rpc/middleware.ts`). Rejects with `CSRF_FAILED` (403) when `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, or when Origin (else Referer) is present and its hostname is neither the request Host's hostname nor an allowed host (an unparseable value such as `Origin: null` is rejected). Requests with neither Origin nor Referer (curl, scripts) pass. Reason: oRPC's OpenAPI handler accepts `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, so a cross-site form could claim a fresh instance or force a login without a preflight. Contract change: `CSRF_FAILED` added to the `.errors()` of `auth.setup` and `auth.login` in `packages/contract`. Hostnames are compared without ports (same as the host check).
- **Sliding cookie refresh (Decision):** `SessionService.verify` returns `renewedExpiresAt` when it slid the session (used after the 1 h touch interval). `resolveAuth` passes it up as `renewedSession { id, expiresAt }` and `app.ts` re-sends `tcd_session` with the new Expires (Secure via `isHttps`) unless the response already sets or clears `tcd_session` (setup, login, logout).
- **Minor:** `main.ts` opens `<configDir>/transcoda.sqlite` (the name Task 20 uses). The cached dummy hash promise is reset when it rejects so the next login retries; the startup warm-up catches the rejection and logs a warning. Set-Cookie writes in auth procedures throw if `context.resHeaders` is missing instead of dropping the cookie silently.

## 2026-10-16 M0 Task 16: tokens.* procedures
- **Session-only (Decision):** `tokens.create` / `list` / `revoke` use `requireSession`; token management is never a token scope. `revoke` returns `NOT_FOUND` when `TokenService.revoke` returns false (unknown, foreign or already revoked id).
- **Past expiry (Decision):** `InvalidTokenExpiryError` from `TokenService.create` maps to oRPC's built-in `BAD_REQUEST` (400); the contract's `tokens.create` error list has no suitable code, so none was added.

## Task 18: apps/web scaffold

- **Versions (pinned):** react/react-dom 19.3.1, vite 8.3.4, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.2, wouter 3.13.0, @testing-library/react 16.3.3 (+ @testing-library/dom 10.4.2, its peer), jsdom 30.1.3, @types/react(-dom) 19.3.0, @orpc/* 1.15.7, vitest 5.0.5.
- **Project reference:** `apps/web/tsconfig.json` is composite + `emitDeclarationOnly`, but with `outDir: dist-types` (gitignored) so it does not collide with Vite's `dist` build output; added to the root solution file. Plus `module ESNext`, `moduleResolution Bundler`, `jsx react-jsx`, `lib DOM`.
- **Test env:** the jsdom environment is set in `apps/web/vite.config.ts` (`test.environment`), so server tests keep the node environment under the root `projects` setup.
- **oRPC error handling in pages:** `isDefinedError(e)` on an `unknown` catch variable narrows to `never` (it is `Extract<T, ORPCError>`). Pages use `const [err] = await safe(api.auth.x(...))`, which types `err` as the contract's defined errors, so `err.code` and `err.data.retryAfterSeconds` are typed. `safe` is re-exported from `api/client.ts`.
- **Dev proxy:** `/rpc` and `/api` go to `http://localhost:8080` (override with `TRANSCODA_API_URL`); no `changeOrigin`, so Origin and Host both stay `localhost:5173` and the server's same-origin check passes.
- **Auth bootstrap failure:** if `auth.me` itself fails (server down), status falls back to `anonymous`.

## Task 19: app shell, canvas, tokens page

- `@xyflow/react` pinned 12.12.0; canvas uses `colorMode="dark"` to match the token-based dark theme (restyle comes later).
- Token expiry input is a date; it is sent as end of that local day (`T23:59:59` local → ISO). A past date yields a server 400, shown as a generic error.
- Token create/revoke failures show generic messages; the plaintext token lives only in component state, cleared on Dismiss or navigation.
- Login/setup/logout catch errors from `refresh()`/`logout()` and show a generic message (no unhandled rejections).
- `biome.json` now ignores `**/dist-types` (generated declaration output from apps/web).

## Task 20: server entrypoint, production bundle, graceful shutdown

- **Bundler (Decision):** `tsdown` 0.23.1 (rolldown). It runs without any install script, so `allowBuilds` is unchanged. Config in `apps/server/tsdown.config.ts`: ESM, node24, `deps.alwaysBundle: [/^@transcoda\//]` (tsdown deprecated `noExternal`), `clean: false` because `dist` also holds `tsc -b` declaration output, `dts: false`. `TRANSCODA_VERSION` is defined from `apps/server/package.json`. Every runtime import left external in `dist/main.js` (hono, @hono/node-server, @orpc/{server,contract,openapi,json-schema,zod}, better-sqlite3, zod) is a declared dependency of apps/server, so `pnpm deploy --prod` resolves them.
- **Root build:** `pnpm build` runs the web build, then the server bundle.
- **bootstrap.ts:** `createDeps(config, log)` wraps `createAppDeps` (services are wired once). It probes ffmpeg once (warning only), warms the dummy hash, and runs `sessions.purgeExpired()` + `limiter.prune()` every 10 min on an unref'd timer cleared by `close()`. It throws `SchemaTooNewError` (closing the db first); `main.ts` maps it to exit 3, `ConfigError` to exit 2, anything else to exit 1.
- **Logger:** `logger.ts`; JSON lines (`time`, `level`, `msg`, ...) when `NODE_ENV=production`, plain text otherwise. Callers never pass secrets.
- **Shutdown:** SIGTERM/SIGINT stop accepting, close idle keep-alive connections, `server.close()` then `deps.close()` and exit 0; a 10 s unref'd timer forces exit 1.
- **Not changed:** the server listens on `config.host` (default 0.0.0.0) as before.

- **Web test timezone (Task 19 fix):** `apps/web/vite.config.ts` sets `process.env.TZ = 'Asia/Kolkata'` (UTC+5:30, no DST) before vitest forks workers, so every web test runs in a fixed non-UTC zone. This makes the token-expiry local-time conversion test non-tautological on UTC CI machines; the test also asserts the zone offset is non-zero. Chosen over per-file setup because the config-level env is inherited by all workers and needs no extra file.
- **Token form:** expiry is end of local day (`T23:59:59.999`); selected scopes are always sent in `TOKEN_SCOPES` order; whitespace-only names are rejected client-side (`pattern` plus a trim guard).

## Task 21: Docker image, entrypoint, compose example

- **Base image (pinned):** `node:24.22.1-bookworm-slim` (Node 24.22.1; exact patch tag since 2026-10-26, see "Node 24.22.1" below). Set as `ARG NODE_IMAGE` and used by all three stages.
- **ffmpeg (pinned):** `jellyfin-ffmpeg8=8.1.3-1-bookworm` from `https://repo.jellyfin.org/debian` (suite `bookworm`, component `main`, key `jellyfin_team.gpg.key` dearmored to `/usr/share/keyrings/jellyfin.gpg`); newest 8.1.x on 2026-10-16. Overridable with `--build-arg JELLYFIN_FFMPEG_VERSION`. Startup log reports `ffmpeg: 8.1.3-Jellyfin`.
- **Image size:** `transcoda:dev` (linux/arm64) is 426 MB uncompressed (base image 249 MB, ffmpeg + libraries ~165 MB, app 13 MB). Target < 450 MB met for arm64. **linux/amd64 is 552 MB (over target):** jellyfin-ffmpeg8 on amd64 installs 215 MB (vs ~92 MB on arm64) because it bundles the VAAPI/QSV/Vulkan stack (iHD, i965, radeonsi drivers 68 MB, libmfx*, Vulkan ICDs); plus libicu72 (36 MB, pulled by libxml2). These are what make Intel/AMD hardware transcoding work, so they are kept. Compressed (`docker save | gzip -6`): arm64 147 MB, amd64 184 MB. The amd64 build was verified locally under emulation (starts, `ffmpeg: 8.1.3-Jellyfin`, health ok, node runs as `transcoda`, stop 0.2 s).
- **pnpm deploy (Decision):** pnpm 12.6.0 runs `pnpm deploy --prod --filter @transcoda/server /deploy` without `--legacy` and without `inject-workspace-packages`; the deps stage copies only the manifests and the lockfile. The workspace packages end up as (manifest-only) injected copies; they are unused at runtime because the bundle inlines them.
- **better-sqlite3 in the image:** the prebuilt `prebuilds/linux-<arch>.node` loads on bookworm; no compiler and no install script (allowBuilds stays false). The deps stage fails the build if `new Database(':memory:')` does not work, and prunes `deps/`, `src/` and the other platforms' prebuilds (13 MB of node_modules instead of ~57 MB).
- **Runtime layout:** `/app/package.json` (the deployed server manifest, so `/app/server/main.js` is ESM by `type: module`), `/app/node_modules`, `/app/server/main.js`, `/app/web`.
- **Runtime extras (Decision):** `procps` is installed (~1 MB) so `ps`/`pgrep` work in `docker exec`. `curl`/`gnupg` are purged after adding the key. npm, corepack and yarn are deleted from the runtime stage (less attack surface; no size gain because they live in the base layers).
- **`node` user removed (Decision):** the base image's `node` user/group (uid/gid 1000) is deleted so the default `PUID=1000` gets a `transcoda` user.
- **Entrypoint groups (Decision):** setpriv is given `--groups=<gid list>` (or `--clear-groups`) instead of `--init-groups`. `--init-groups` calls `initgroups()` for the passwd entry of the uid, so with a duplicated uid (`useradd -o`, e.g. `PUID=33`) it picks the other account (`www-data`) and loses the device groups (verified: only gid 33 with `--init-groups`, gid 105 with `--groups`). Device nodes owned by gid 0 are skipped with a log line (never grant group root). Only character devices are considered.
- **Entrypoint details:** `PUID`/`PGID` must be numeric (exit 2 otherwise). `useradd -K UID_MIN=0 -K UID_MAX=2147483647` silences the "uid outside UID_MIN..UID_MAX" warning for macOS-style uids such as 501. Failing `chown` of `/config`/`/transcode` (e.g. NFS) logs a warning instead of aborting. The user is created only when no `transcoda` user exists (a restarted container keeps it; a changed PUID needs a recreated container, which compose does).
- **Healthcheck:** uses `process.env.TRANSCODA_PORT || 8080`, so a changed port keeps the check working.
- **.dockerignore:** the plan's entries plus `**/node_modules`, `**/.local` (apps/server/.local holds a dev secret key), `**/dist-types`, `**/*.tsbuildinfo`, `**/coverage`, `.superpowers`, `.claude`, `docker/docker-compose.yml`.
- **Bind mounts on Docker Desktop (macOS):** ownership inside the container follows the container user, files on the host belong to the host user. Once, a container started right after `rm -rf` + `mkdir` of the bind-mounted directory failed with `ENOENT` opening `/config/secret.key`; an identical rerun worked (stale file-sharing cache, not reproducible).

## Task 22: GitHub Actions

- **Workflows:** `ci.yml` (every push and PR; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`; 15 min timeout; cancels superseded runs), `docker.yml` (main, `v*` tags, manual; amd64+arm64 via QEMU; tags `main` and semver only, no sha tags; 45 min; no cancel), `ghcr-cleanup.yml`. The brief's `pnpm ci` is `pnpm check` (pnpm 12 reserves `ci`). Actions are pinned to the current major tags (checkout v7, setup-node v7, pnpm/action-setup v6, docker/* setup-qemu v4, setup-buildx v4, login v4, metadata v6, build-push v7); Renovate keeps them current. No dependabot.
- **Cleanup action (Decision):** `dataaxiom/ghcr-cleanup-action@v1` (v1.2.2). Its README says it handles multi-arch images and its algorithm first removes child images (platform manifests, referrers, cosign) from the working set, then deletes untagged images together with their own children, so the children of tagged `main`/`vX.Y.Z` indexes are kept. Inputs used: `owner`, `package`, `delete-untagged`, `dry-run`, `validate`. Real deletion is therefore enabled on `workflow_run` (after a successful Docker run) and weekly `schedule`; `workflow_dispatch` defaults to dry-run via an input (`dry-run`, default true). Not yet verified against a real package: Step 1b (dry run after the first two docker runs, then pull both platforms) is the author's call, since it needs a push. The package must grant the repo Admin under Package settings, Manage Actions access. Shared concurrency group `ghcr-cleanup` (the action is not parallel-safe).
- **Minutes and storage:** CI about 3-5 min per push; image build about 15-25 min (arm64 `pnpm deploy` under QEMU), only on main and tags. Compressed image size (`docker save | gzip -6`, Task 21): arm64 about 147 MB + amd64 about 184 MB = about 331 MB per tag. GHCR private storage on GitHub Free is 500 MB, so `main` plus one release (about 662 MB, less with shared layers) can exceed it. Open for the author: public package, or slimmer amd64 image (the VAAPI/QSV stack in jellyfin-ffmpeg is 215 MB uncompressed). Tagged versions are never deleted automatically. Re-measure after the first push.

Password hashing is Node's built-in argon2id (no dependency, no flag).

## Task 24: Patchbay UI design on the M0 screens

- **Design adopted:** the author's "Patchbay" handoff (Claude Design) is the visual source of truth for the web UI. The handoff package (README, `tokens.css`, brand, screens 01-08, editor specs, explorations) is kept outside the repo; only `tokens.css` is vendored (below). M0 implements `screens/01-setup-and-login`, `02-app-shell-empty-canvas`, `03-api-tokens`; later screens are reference for later milestones.
- **Tokens:** `apps/web/src/styles/tokens.css` is a byte-identical copy of the handoff `tokens.css` and is excluded from Biome (`biome.json`) so it stays diffable against future handoffs (Biome would lowercase the hex values and rejects the handoff's `!important` in the reduced-motion rule). `src/styles.css` is rewritten against the tokens; the few colours the screen files use that are not tokens (`#FF9A8C`, `#5C2A24`, `#E2FF9E`, `#34333A`, `#4A4850`, `#23271B`) are named variables at the top of `styles.css`. Dark theme only; the light tokens are present but there is no theme switch (out of scope).
- **Fonts:** `@fontsource/bricolage-grotesque` 5.3.0 (400/500/700/800) and `@fontsource/jetbrains-mono` 5.3.0 (400/600), exact-pinned, both OFL-1.1, imported in `main.tsx` so Vite bundles the woff2/woff files (no CDN; the packages' CSS has no remote URLs). The static Bricolage files have one optical size, while the design canvas loads the variable font with `opsz 12..96` from Google Fonts, so large headings are slightly less condensed than on the canvas.
- **Logo and favicon:** `components/Logo.tsx` has the L1d mark as an inline SVG (`Mark`, tone `lime` on dark or `ink` on lime, always decorative next to the wordmark) and the 800-weight `transcoda` wordmark. Favicons are the sheet's simplified 32 and 16 px marks as SVG (`public/favicon.svg`, `public/favicon-16.svg`); the sheet has no PNGs, so there is no PNG fallback.
- **React Flow theming:** only through props and React Flow's CSS variables/classes: `Background` dots (gap 22, `size` 2.4 = 1.2 px radius, `--canvas-dot` on `--surface`), `Controls` horizontal bottom-right without the lock button, restyled to 40 px buttons. Attribution stays visible (moved bottom-left, muted) although the design does not draw it. The empty state is an overlay; the canvas has no nodes.
- **Hit targets:** the design draws some controls smaller than 44 px (Log out and Revoke 36 px, canvas zoom controls 40 px). They keep the drawn size; `.btn-sm` (Log out, Revoke) gets an invisible `::before` that extends the hit area to 44 px tall. The React Flow controls stay 40 px (not extended, to avoid styling React Flow internals beyond its classes); open point for the author.
- **Behaviour unchanged:** API calls, error mapping, CSRF, token logic and auth flow are as before. Test changes are selector/copy only (button and link names, `within(row)` for scope chips, the reveal's new copy), plus new LoginPage tests for the wrong-password and rate-limited states. The auth pages now also call the public `system.health` for the version shown on the brand panel (test mocks extended accordingly).
- **Copy changes (design wins for visible text):** "Log in"/"Log in to Transcoda" → "Sign in" with "to Transcoda on <hostname>"; setup button "Create account and sign in"; "Wrong username or password."; "Too many attempts. Try again in N s."; nav "Tokens" → "API tokens"; reveal "Copy it now. It won't be shown again." with "I've saved it" instead of "Dismiss".
- **Deviations from the screen files (brief wins for behaviour/scope):**
  - Setup has no "Confirm password" field: adding it would change the setup flow; the live "N of 12 characters minimum" meter is implemented (display only, `minLength` still enforces).
  - The "Already set up" screen is not built: on `SETUP_ALREADY_DONE` the app still refreshes and redirects to `/login` (frozen behaviour); the gate never shows `/setup` once an account exists.
  - Rate limited: the design's locked state is implemented as a client-side countdown from `retryAfterSeconds` (fields and button disabled, button reads "Sign in · wait N s"); it is not persisted, so a reload unlocks the form (the server still enforces the limit).
  - Nav: Jobs (M2), Libraries (M3.5), Plugins (M3) and Settings (M2) are shown as in the M0 design but as plain, non-focusable items with the milestone tag (and "coming in" for screen readers), not links, so nothing routes to a 404.
  - Empty canvas: "Add a trigger" and "Start from 'TV cleanup' example" are shown dimmed and disabled, and the "press / to search nodes" hint is replaced by "The node editor arrives in M2." (no node editor in M0).
  - Token expiry stays a date input (end of local day, as before) instead of the design's preset select (90 days / 30 days / 1 year / Never); empty means never. "Cancel" clears the form. The create form is hidden while a new token is revealed, as in the design.
  - Token dates use the browser locale (`Intl.DateTimeFormat`, day + short month + year); created and last used show "just now", "N min ago", "N h ago" within a day; full timestamp in the `title`. The design's samples are en-GB.
  - Phone width (< 900 px): the auth brand panel stacks above the form with a 40 px tagline; the design only draws 1280 px.

## M0 final fix wave

Fixes from the whole-branch review.

- **Effective host behind a proxy (Decision):** `requestHost(req, remoteAddr, trusted)` (`http/request-info.ts`) returns the first `X-Forwarded-Host` value when the peer is in `TRANSCODA_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, else the `Host` header. The host check (`app.ts`), the setup/login same-origin guard (`sameOriginOk`, which now takes the effective host as a parameter) and setup's host recording all use it, so a proxy that rewrites `Host` (nginx's default) works once it is trusted and sends `X-Forwarded-Host`. From an untrusted peer the header is ignored. The session CSRF origin check (`checkOrigin`) is unchanged: it compares against the allowed hosts, which include the recorded public host.
- **HOST_NOT_ALLOWED body (Decision):** the 403 is encoded exactly as oRPC encodes a defined error, built with `ORPCError#toJSON()`: `{ defined: true, code: 'HOST_NOT_ALLOWED', status: 403, message: 'Host not allowed', data: { host } }` for `/api/*`, and the same wrapped as `{ json: <error>, meta: [] }` for `/rpc/*` (the RPC link deserialises `body.json`; verified in `@orpc/client` 1.15.7's `StandardRPCLinkCodec.decode`). `data.host` is the normalised rejected hostname. The contract's `HOST_NOT_ALLOWED` def now declares that `data` schema. It is not added to the procedures' error maps (the check runs before routing).
- **Web host-not-allowed screen (Decision):** an RPCLink interceptor (`api/host-guard.ts`) reports HOST_NOT_ALLOWED from any call to subscribers; `AuthProvider` subscribes and also checks the initial `auth.me` failure, and switches to a `host-not-allowed` status. `App` then shows `HostNotAllowedPage` (auth layout, danger alert "This host name isn't allowed: <host>. Add it to TRANSCODA_ALLOWED_HOSTS.") on every route.
- **Loading state:** `.app-loading` (full height, centred, muted mono text) with `role="status"`; the removed `.auth-page` class is no longer referenced. The token reveal resets `copied` when dismissed.
- **OpenAPI security (Decision):** the document keeps `security: [{ bearerAuth: [] }]`; `system.health`, `auth.setup`, `auth.login` and `auth.me` override it with `security: []` through the route's `spec` callback (`publicSpec` in `packages/contract/src/openapi.ts`, a function so oRPC still generates the rest of the operation). A test checks the generated spec.
- **INVALID_EXPIRY (Decision):** new contract error `INVALID_EXPIRY` (400, "Token expiry must be a valid time in the future") on `tokens.create`, replacing oRPC's built-in `BAD_REQUEST` for a past or invalid `expiresAt`. Schema-level input errors stay `BAD_REQUEST`.
- **Logger:** `createApp` takes `log` (`AppOptions.log`, required); `app.onError` logs through it instead of `console.error`.
- **SPA fallback:** a path whose last segment has a file extension (`/assets/x.js`, `/favicon.ico`) that does not exist gets the JSON 404 instead of `index.html`; dots in earlier segments do not count.
- **Healthcheck (Decision):** the image's healthcheck probes `TRANSCODA_HOST` unless it is `0.0.0.0`/`::` (then `127.0.0.1`), on `TRANSCODA_PORT`. It uses `node:http` with an explicit `Host: localhost` header, since the host check (active after setup) only always-allows `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1`, and a specific bind address is usually not an allowed host.
- **Entrypoint:** exports `HOME="$CONFIG_DIR"` before `setpriv` (setpriv keeps root's `HOME=/root`). With `--user` only `UMASK` applies (documented in `docs/env.md`, together with "recreate the container after changing PUID/PGID").
- **.dockerignore:** adds `.env*`, `**/*.sqlite*`, `**/*.pem`, `**/secret.key`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `docs/plans`, `docs/design` (some are already covered by `docs` and `*.md`; listed explicitly so a later edit of those lines cannot let them in).
- **GHCR cleanup grace:** `older-than: 1 day` (input name checked in `dataaxiom/ghcr-cleanup-action` `action.yml` at `v1`), so the untagged manifests of an in-flight multi-arch push are never pruned. Before the first run, the package must grant this repository the Admin role under Package settings, Manage Actions access; otherwise the action cannot delete versions.
- **Minor:** `@orpc/client` is a dev dependency of the server (tests only); the password hashing timing bound is 5 s (slow CI runners); the web test TZ override (`Asia/Kolkata`) is set only when `VITEST` is set, so `vite` dev and build keep the real zone.
- **Docs:** `docs/env.md` explains the shared per-IP login limit without `TRANSCODA_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, adds a "Reverse proxies" section (nginx headers, Caddy/Traefik/NPM, the `TRANSCODA_ALLOWED_HOSTS` fallback) and states that `TRANSCODA_VERSION` is build-time.

## M1 Task 9: node migrations and sub-workflow pin comparison

- **Migration path strictness (Decision):** `migrationPath` (`apps/server/src/workflows/upgrade.ts`) returns `null` (node broken, nothing rewritten) not only for a missing link but also when two migrations share a `from` (ambiguous), a step does not move forward (`to <= from`), or a step overshoots the target version. A stored `typeVersion` above the registered version is left alone by upgrades (not downgraded, not broken).
- **Whole-graph atomicity:** `planNodeUpgrades` checks every outdated node's path before running any `migrate`; if any node is broken the plan is `broken` with all broken node ids and no migration runs. Each `migrate` receives a deep copy of the settings, so the input graph is never mutated.
- **Bypass in pin comparison (Decision, deviates from the task brief):** the brief listed `bypassed` among ignored fields, but the design ("any difference other than positions/labels/groups … counts as behaviour") and the controller ruling ("anything not proven safe counts as behaviour") win: toggling `bypassed` changes what happens to files, so it is `behaviour`. `undefined` and `false` are treated as equal.
- **Pin comparison details:** a changed graph kind, duplicate node ids, a migration that throws while being checked, an unknown node type with a version change, or a lowered `typeVersion` are all `behaviour`. Edge ids and edge order are ignored; edges compare as a multiset of `(from.node, from.port, to.node, to.port)` (sorted keys, duplicates kept), so adding or removing a duplicate parallel edge is `behaviour`. Settings compare by key-sorted JSON. Non-integer versions never form a migration path.
- **Pin comparison and overrides (controller ruling):** `comparePinChange(oldChild, newChild, overrides, registry)` takes the calling Run node's `overrides` (keys `<nodeId>.<settingKey>`). It is `behaviour` if any override key is malformed or targets a node missing in either child, a node whose type or `typeVersion` changed, or a setting key the new node's settings schema no longer has (so a safe migration that renames an overridden setting is `behaviour`). The Task 12 save handler additionally treats a changed `subworkflowId` and any dropped overrides as `behaviour`.
- **applyOverrides registry required:** `applyOverrides(graph, overrides, registry)` no longer defaults to the core registry, so callers always pass the live registry (plugin nodes).

## M1 Task 10: workflow validation

- **Order and shape:** `validateWorkflow` (`apps/server/src/workflows/validate.ts`) emits issues in check order (node type/version and settings, edges and inputs, graph cycle, kind rules and reachability, sub-workflow references, availability), nodes in graph order within each check.
- **Root-level settings errors (Decision):** a settings issue at the root (non-object settings such as `5`, `null` or an array) is `INVALID_SETTINGS` without `settingPath`; nested issues carry the dotted path.
- **UNKNOWN_PORT (Decision):** one issue per edge, naming every bad end ("X has no output file; Y has no input pass"). A port that exists only on the other side (an input used as `from`, an output used as `to`) is unknown. Type mismatch is checked only when both ports exist.
- **Input counting (Decision):** an edge counts towards its target input whenever both endpoint nodes exist, the target's type is known and the target port is one of its inputs, even if the source is an unknown-type node, the source port is unknown or the types mismatch. A wrong edge is so reported once (UNKNOWN_PORT / PORT_TYPE_MISMATCH / UNKNOWN_NODE_TYPE) and not again as INPUT_UNCONNECTED; edges touching unknown-type nodes otherwise get only the existence check (controller ruling).
- **GRAPH_CYCLE:** one issue for the first cycle found by DFS over all existing edges; message lists the node ids (`a → b → a`), `nodeId` is the first node on it.
- **NO_FINISH_PATH (Decision):** reported when the main workflow has no finish node at all (whatever the trigger count), or when it has exactly one trigger and no finish node is reachable from it. With zero or several triggers and some finish node present, only TRIGGER_COUNT is reported. Reachability warnings need exactly one root.
- **Reachability:** forward closure from the root over all edges, then data nodes feeding a reachable node via a `data:*` output, repeated to a fixed point (controller ruling). Unknown-type nodes reached by an edge count as reachable.
- **Sub-workflow checks (Decision):** only for Run sub-workflow nodes at the current type version with valid settings. A null resolve (unknown, soft-deleted, kind `workflow`, missing/corrupt version; controller ruling) is SUBWORKFLOW_MISSING and skips the rest. Override issues carry `settingPath: overrides.<key>`.
- **Reference cycles (Decision):** compared by sub-workflow id across versions (design: "across references and versions"). DFS through resolved children's valid run nodes with a stack of ids; reaching `ctx.self.id` or a stacked id reports SUBWORKFLOW_REFERENCE_CYCLE once on the top-level node. Explored refs are memoised with the set of ids they reach, so diamonds are not re-walked and a memoised ref still closes a cycle when one of its reached ids is on the current stack.
- **Availability:** checked for every node with a registry entry (also outdated/newer ones). Bypassed nodes are validated like any other.

### M1 Task 10 fix round

- **Graph size caps (controller ruling):** `WorkflowGraphSchema` caps `nodes` at 500, `edges` at 2000 and `groups` at 100 (`MAX_GRAPH_NODES` / `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` / `MAX_GRAPH_GROUPS` in `packages/shared/src/workflow/graph.ts`), with messages "A workflow can have at most N nodes|edges|groups".
- **Linear graph walks:** validation builds one successor map over existing edges; the cycle search is an iterative DFS (explicit path and cursor stacks, no recursion) and reachability is a worklist closure over adjacency maps. Data-feeder reachability is a closure over reversed `data:*` edges from the forward-reachable set, replacing the fixed-point loop (same result).
- **SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN (Decision, controller ruling):** new error code. For each top-level Run sub-workflow node whose pinned version resolves, the resolved child is walked recursively through nested Run nodes (each `id@version` walked once per top-level node, so diamonds report once and reference cycles terminate). Each unknown node type, newer node version, outdated node with no migration path, unavailable node, or missing nested sub-workflow is one SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN error on the top-level node; the message names the chain (`sub-workflow A version 1 → B version 2: <problem>`). Outdated nodes with a migration path inside a pinned child are not reported (pins are intentional). Settings validity inside children is not checked (it was validated when that version was saved).
- **Throwing settings schemas (Decision):** if `parseSettings` throws (e.g. a plugin refinement), the node gets one root-level INVALID_SETTINGS "Settings could not be checked: <message>" without `settingPath`.

## M1 Task 12: workflow service and startup upgrade

- **WorkflowService** (`apps/server/src/workflows/service.ts`) is the one place the procedures and startup use; `AppDeps` gains `nodes` (the `NodeRegistry`) and `workflows`. `createAppDeps` takes `nodes` (the complete list, core included, default: core registry) and a required `log`. New workflow ids are `randomUUID()`.
- **Sub-workflow resolution:** validation, stale-override dropping and pin comparison share one resolver: a live (not soft-deleted) workflow of kind `subworkflow` and a stored, parsable version; anything else is "missing" (null).
- **Pin changes on save (controller ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: pin changes no longer flag anything.)* for each Run node present (by id) in both the base and the new graph: a changed `subworkflowId` (or settings that no longer parse on one side) is behaviour; a changed `version` is `comparePinChange(oldChild, newChild, overrides of the new Run node, registry)`, an unresolvable child is behaviour; Run nodes new to the graph are not pin changes. Any override removed by `dropStaleOverrides` is also behaviour. Behaviour sets `needsReview` with the note `<change>[; <change>…]; check the workflow before enabling it` (e.g. `Updated sub-workflow Sub A 3→4`, `Switched sub-workflow A 2→B 2`, `Dropped overrides that no longer exist: kl.nope`) and switches an enabled workflow to dry run. A save without behaviour keeps the existing `needsReview` flag. If the base version is corrupt there is nothing to compare, so no pin change is reported (Decision; superseded in Task 13: the save itself counts as behaviour).
- **needsReview:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: review state is derived, not stored.)* only `setMode('enabled')` (which requires zero errors) clears it; `setMode('dry-run')` keeps it. Enabling a workflow whose current version is corrupt returns `invalid` with one `CORRUPT_GRAPH` issue.
- **Summaries (Decision):** `get(id, version)` computes `valid`/`errorCount`/`usesOutdatedSubworkflow` from the requested version's graph (matching its `issues`); `currentVersion`, mode, review flags *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: mode and review flags are gone; the summary carries the active version)* and `usedBy` describe the workflow as it is now. `usedBy` counts each parent once, ignores a sub-workflow's reference to itself, and is empty for main workflows. A corrupt current version summarises as `valid: false, errorCount: 1`, `usesOutdatedSubworkflow: false`.
- **upgradeAll (controller ruling):** each workflow is handled in its own `try`: corrupt graph, no migration path (whole graph left untouched) and a throwing migration or failed write are each one `log.warn` and count as broken; nothing aborts startup. One `log.info` summary per run. `bootstrap.createDeps` calls it once after wiring the services; log `error` fields are message strings.

## M1 Task 13: nodes and workflows procedures

- **Auth (controller ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: setMode is gone; activation is session-only.)* `nodes.list`, `workflows.list`, `get`, `versions` and `validate` (stores nothing) need scope `read`; `create`, `save`, `setMode`, `rename`, `delete` need `workflows:write` (or a session, CSRF-checked). `requireScope` now narrows `context.auth` to the signed-in caller (session or token), so `createdBy` is `context.auth.user.id` for both.
- **Kind mismatch on save (controller ruling):** `WorkflowService.save` throws `KindMismatchError` when `graph.kind` differs from the stored workflow's kind (checked before the version-conflict check); the procedure maps it to oRPC's generic `BAD_REQUEST` (400, `defined: false`) with the error message, like a schema failure. No contract error code was added. Nothing is stored.
- **Corrupt base version on save (controller ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: a save never flags the workflow.)* the save counts as behaviour: `needsReview` with note `Previous version N could not be read; check the workflow before enabling it`, and an enabled workflow switches to dry run.
- **CorruptGraphError** is not mapped by the procedures; oRPC turns it into a 500 `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` response itself (it does not reach `app.onError`, so it is not logged).
- **REST test helpers** live in `apps/server/src/test-http.ts` (`send` accepts GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE).

### M1 Task 12 fix round

- **Upgrade output is validated (controller ruling):** `upgradeAll` parses each upgraded graph with `WorkflowGraphSchema` before `appendVersion` and writes the parsed data. A graph that does not parse (e.g. a migration that mutates its input and returns nothing) counts as broken with one `log.warn` (`workflow upgrade failed` + the parse message); nothing is written and the mode is unchanged.
- **upgradeAll never aborts startup:** if listing the workflows fails (a database error), it logs one `log.error` (`workflow upgrades failed: could not list workflows`) and returns the empty summary, without the info line.
- **Missing current version row (Decision):** `listCurrentVersions` left-joins the versions, so a live workflow whose `current_version` row is missing is returned as an unreadable version (`error: Stored version N is missing`, `raw: null`) instead of being skipped. `upgradeAll` counts it as broken (so `upgraded + broken + unchanged` = live workflows always holds), `list` shows it invalid with `errorCount: 1`, and `get(id)` without a version throws `CorruptGraphError` (500) like a corrupt graph; `get(id, n)` for a missing explicit version stays `null` (404). Unreadable stored versions carry their raw text (`raw`).
- **Review notes accumulate (controller ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: review notes no longer exist.)* when a behaviour change (save or upgrade) hits a workflow whose `needsReview` is already true, the new note is appended to the existing `reviewNote` with `; ` instead of replacing it.
- **Delete and corrupt parents (controller ruling, Decision):** `delete` of a sub-workflow also refuses with `in-use` when a live parent's current version cannot be parsed but its stored text contains the sub-workflow id (a substring scan; ids are UUIDs, so false positives are negligible and err on the safe side). A parent with a missing version row has nothing to scan and does not block.
- **Unexpected procedure errors are logged (controller ruling):** both oRPC handlers (`RPCHandler`, `OpenAPIHandler`) get a client interceptor (`onError` from `@orpc/server`) that logs once via the app logger (`log.error({ msg: 'rpc procedure failed', procedure, error: '<name>: <message>' })`) when the error is not an `ORPCError`, or is an undefined `ORPCError` with a 5xx status (e.g. output validation). Defined errors and 4xx errors are not logged; input, context and request bodies never are. Each handler gets its own interceptor array, because oRPC plugins push their own interceptors into the options arrays. This supersedes the Task 13 note that a `CorruptGraphError` is not logged.

## 2026-10-16 M1 workflow contract step

Per-task rulings are recorded in the M1 Task 9–13 sections above (bypass toggle and pin comparison with overrides, graph size caps, `SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN`, kind mismatch 400, corrupt base version, review-note appending, unexpected-error logging, delete with corrupt parents); this entry summarises the rest.

- **Dependencies:** zod is a dependency of `shared` as well as `sdk`; the `THIRD_PARTY` allowlist applies to package.json dependencies and has a `node:test` script test; `@transcoda/sdk` is added to the server.
- **Core nodes:** definitions live in `apps/server/src/nodes/core/` (the sdk must not depend on contract; the plugin track reuses `@transcoda/sdk` only). No hooks in this step. Icons are lucide names, `shortId` is 10 characters, duplicate ids are a structural 400, settings schemas are always `z.object` (so override keys are top-level) and every setting has a `.describe()` label.
- **Save stores `typeVersion`s as-is:** validation reports `NODE_OUTDATED`; the upgrader rewrites on the next start.
- **Spec deviations (controller rulings):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: setMode removed; activate replaces it.)* only `setMode('enabled', confirm)` clears `needsReview`; a missing or false `confirm` is a plain 400 from the input schema (not `WORKFLOW_INVALID`); "dry runs require a valid workflow" applies to running them (engine), not to the resting mode every new workflow starts in; a pin change is a changed `subworkflowId` (always behaviour) or a changed `version` on a run node present in both versions.
- **`upgradeAll`:** summary `{ upgraded, flagged, broken, unchanged }`, logged once by the service; whole-graph atomicity (one node without a path means nothing is rewritten); corrupt graphs and throwing migrations are logged once and counted broken.
- **Validation:** data nodes are reachable when they feed a reachable node; edges touching an unknown-type node get only the existence check.
- **Status codes:** `WORKFLOW_INVALID` 422, `VERSION_CONFLICT` and `IN_USE` 409. `usedBy` is computed in JS from current versions (no reference table). A corrupt stored graph lists as invalid and `get` returns 500.
- **Node requirements:** `core:encode-video` has no static `requires` (the encoder is a setting; revisit in the engine track); `core:encode-audio` requires `aac` and `pan`. Settings marked "revisit": `input-library.libraryId`, `input-webhook`, `encode-video.encoder`. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21, except the export/import remap.)*
- **Upgrade notes:** format `Updated <Title> a→b` (plus ` (<migration note>)` for behaviour steps); review notes as in Tasks 12 and 13.
- **Task 14:** the end-to-end scenario (`apps/server/src/workflows/e2e.test.ts`, fixtures in `fixtures/tigger-pooh.ts`) passed without changes to earlier tasks' files.

## M1 final review

Rulings from the whole-branch review (`final-review.md`); they supersede the earlier entries where they differ.

- **C1 — nested pins are not overridable (ruling):** `core:run-subworkflow` nodes are excluded from a sub-workflow's settings and override keys, like the start/end nodes (no deep override paths, per the design). A parent override such as `inner.version`, `inner.subworkflowId` or `inner.overrides` is unknown everywhere: an `OVERRIDE_DROPPED` warning in validation, dropped (and counted as behaviour) on save, ignored by `applyOverrides`, and `behaviour` in pin comparison.
- **I1 — behaviour migrations in pinned children (ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: behaviour migrations no longer flag parents.)* a node in a pinned sub-workflow version whose migration path contains a `behaviour` step makes the parent's Run node `SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN` ("<node> needs a review; re-pin to a version upgraded on this server"); a migration that throws does too ("<node> could not be upgraded: …"). All-safe paths stay allowed (the Task 10 ruling is kept). **The engine applies only safe migrations when loading a pinned child, never behaviour ones.** When `upgradeAll` upgrades a sub-workflow with a behaviour change, every live workflow whose current version pins an older version of it is flagged after the upgrade pass: `needsReview`, enabled → dry run, note `Sub-workflow <name> was upgraded with a behaviour change (version N); check the workflow before enabling it` (appended per the review-note rule). No new parent version is written; a failure is logged per workflow and never aborts startup. Only direct parents are flagged. *(Superseded by M1 engine Task 1, N3/N5: transitive and unupgradable-child flagging, reviews that stick.)*
- **I2 — pinned children are validated whole (ruling):** supersedes "Settings validity inside children is not checked" (Task 10 fix round). For each top-level Run node whose pin resolves, the child version is loaded as the engine would (all-safe migrations applied first, then the parent's valid overrides — overrides are keyed against the current node schemas, so applying them before a renaming safe migration would silently lose them; re-review N1) and run through `validateWorkflow` with `self` = the child; every child *error* becomes one `SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN` on the top-level Run node, its message naming the chain (`<Run node>: sub-workflow A version 1 → B version 2: <problem>`; settings issues are prefixed with the node title and setting path). Warnings and the child's own reference-cycle errors are not repeated (the cycle is reported on the top-level node as before); invalid overrides are reported as `INVALID_OVERRIDE` only and left out of the child check. Results are memoised per `id@version` + overrides in a `ValidationCache` (per call; `list()` shares one across all workflows), and an `id@version` already on the recursion stack is skipped. *(Superseded in part by M1 engine Task 1, N2: the cache also memoises sub-workflow lookups and the reference-cycle search, no longer per call.)*
- **I3 — saving an enabled workflow with errors (ruling):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: a save with errors keeps the active version.)* if the workflow is enabled and the saved version has at least one error, the save switches it to dry run with `needsReview` and the note `Saved with N error(s)` (combined with any pin-change notes, appended per the review-note rule). Warnings alone do not. **The engine still revalidates the current version, with its children, before each job and refuses on errors; `mode` alone is not sufficient.** The save's returned issues are now computed before the version is written (identical except for a Run node pinning the workflow's own not-yet-written version).
- **KIND_MISMATCH (ruling):** supersedes the Task 13 kind-mismatch note: a defined contract error `KIND_MISMATCH` (400) in `errorDefs` and in `workflows.save`'s errors (so it appears in the OpenAPI spec); the procedure throws it instead of the generic `BAD_REQUEST`.
- **Body limit (ruling):** Hono `bodyLimit` of 2 MB (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`) on `/api/*` and `/rpc/*`; larger bodies get 413 as an undefined oRPC error `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (wrapped as `{ json, meta }` on `/rpc`).
- **GROUP_MISSING (ruling):** new warning when a node's `groupId` names no group in `groups`.
- **Scopes are exact:** `workflows:write` does not imply `read`; an API client that writes needs both scopes.
- **Unknown graph keys are stripped on save:** the server is the authority on the graph format; the editor must not keep state in the graph that the server does not know.
- **Plugin settings schemas must be non-recursive:** per-setting JSON Schemas are sliced from the node's schema (`properties.<key>`) and `$defs` are dropped, so a recursive/`$ref` schema would yield dangling references.
- **Revisit markers** are now in code too: `core:input-library.libraryId` (a row id inside the graph; export/import must remap it) and `core:input-webhook`. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21, except the export/import remap.)*

## M2 Task 1: web test setup, node-catalogue fixture, data hooks

- **jsdom node measurement (builder's call):** React Flow 12 measures nodes with `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, not with the ResizeObserver entry, and jsdom has no layout (always 0), so the planned polyfills alone made nodes visible (via `initialWidth`/`initialHeight`) but never stored handle bounds, and no edge rendered. `apps/web/src/test/setup.ts` therefore also gives elements with the class `react-flow__node` an `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` of their inline style size (groups) or the card size 210 × 120; every other element keeps jsdom's 0. `apps/web/src/test/flow.test.tsx` pins that a node and an edge render.
- **Fixture JSON in the web project:** `apps/web/tsconfig.json` includes `src/test/fixtures/*.json`, because a composite project must list every file it imports (`import raw from './nodes.json'`, TS6307 otherwise).
- **`useCatalog` cache:** one module-level `nodes.list` promise shared by every consumer, failures included; `reload()` drops it and notifies every mounted consumer, which refetch once together.
- **`describeError` fallbacks:** an error that is neither an `ORPCError` nor a `TypeError` maps to `{ code: null, status: null }` with its own message (or "Something went wrong"); an undefined 400 keeps its oRPC code with the message "The server rejected the request".

## M2 Task 2: dialog, toast, chip primitives and shared time formatting

- **Undrawn icons (builder's call):** `alert`, `chevron-down`, `trash` and `play` are copied from the handoff screens; `info`, `check-circle`, `x-circle`, `subworkflow`, `chevron-right`, `history`, `search`, `pencil` and `pause` are not drawn there and follow the same 24-unit stroke style. `workflow` reuses the `workflows` nav glyph.
- **Toast regions:** the polite `role="status"` region is always mounted (so info toasts are announced); the `role="alert"` region is mounted only while an error toast exists, since an alert is announced when it is inserted. Using an error toast's action also dismisses it.
- **Dialog close path:** Escape (keydown) and the browser's own `cancel` request both call `onClose` and are otherwise prevented, so the parent's `open` prop stays the only source of truth.
- **`fmtAgoLong` past a day:** "saved " plus day and short month in the browser's locale (`Intl.DateTimeFormat(undefined, …)`, "12 Oct" in en-GB, "Oct 12" in en-US), like the other dates in the app. `fmtDayMonth` (the tokens page's "REVOKED 12 OCT" formatter) moved to `lib/time.ts` with `fmtDate`/`fmtAgo`.
- **New non-token colour:** `--backdrop` (`rgb(0 0 0 / 0.6)`) for the dialog backdrop, at the top of `styles.css`.

## M2 Task 3: workflow list, create, rename and delete pages

- **Errors inside dialogs are inline; danger dialogs focus Cancel (controller ruling):** toasts are inert while a modal `<dialog>` is open, so a failure from an action taken inside a dialog is shown in the dialog body (`role="alert"` with the `describeError` text and "Try again"), never as a toast. `Dialog` gives initial focus to the child marked `data-autofocus` (an override); otherwise a `tone="danger"` dialog focuses its footer button labelled "Cancel" (or the dialog itself, never the destructive action) and other dialogs the first focusable child. (Fix round: danger dialogs no longer need the marker.)
- **Dialog focus return (fix-round ruling):** `Dialog` takes `returnFocusTo` (an element or a ref). On close focus goes there when it is still in the document, else to the element focused at open. A dialog opened from a menu item passes the menu button, because the item has unmounted by then; the delete dialog returns focus to the row's "Actions for <name>" button. When the deleted row itself is gone, focus is left to the browser.
- **List page layout (builder's call, undrawn):** *(Superseded 2026-10-18 by Jan: the "New sub-workflow" button is gone; "New workflow" leads to the page where the kind is chosen.)* `.page` with a `.page-head` row (h1 "Workflows", "New workflow" primary, "New sub-workflow" secondary) and a rounded `.wf-table` panel in the tokens-table style; the empty state (screen 02's copy and chain) sits in a bordered panel below the head instead of over a canvas. A sub-workflow with no users shows "Used by 0" (the brief's rule, no special case).
- **"Add a trigger" before Task 16:** creates a workflow named "Untitled workflow" (screen 02's page title) and opens it; the example button is disabled with the title "arrives in Task 16".
- **Row menu and inline rename:** a menu button ("Actions for <name>", three-dot icon `more`, drawn in the same stroke style) opens a `role="menu"` with "Rename" / "Delete"; Escape or a click outside closes it. Rename: Enter saves (trimmed), Escape cancels, blur also cancels (nothing is saved without Enter); an empty or unchanged name just closes the editor; a failure is an error toast (the rename is not inside a dialog), the editor stays open and gets focus back once it is enabled again. An edit ended by Enter or Escape returns focus to the row's actions button; one ended by blur leaves focus where the user moved it.
- **Delete:** the `IN_USE` replacement text is `role="alert"` so it is announced; the Delete button stays disabled (retrying would fail the same way); success closes the dialog, reloads and shows the info toast "Deleted <name>". The destructive button is a new `.btn-danger` (filled `--st-failed` on `--st-failed-ink`).
- **New page:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: the note says "until you activate a version", not "enable".)* h1 follows the chosen kind ("New workflow" / "New sub-workflow"), with screen 02's note "New workflows never touch files until you enable them."; an empty name shows the alert "Enter a name", a failed create "Could not create the workflow, try again" and keeps the form.
- **Table width:** the list table's minimum width (720 px) fits the 1024 px desktop beside the nav, so the row menu is never clipped there; below 1024 px the table scrolls horizontally inside its panel. **Known limitation (desktop-first):** there the scroll container can clip the open row menu; a portal/popover menu would fix it if the list ever needs a narrow layout. Button-styled links (`a.btn`) keep their button colours and drop the link underline.
- **Status fallback (fix round):** a workflow with `valid: false` and `errorCount: 0` shows an error chip "Invalid", so an invalid row never shows no status. A load error is shown whenever `useWorkflows` reports one (with "Try again").
- **Settings nav tag:** "later" instead of "M2" (Settings is not in M2's scope); the screen-reader text reads "Settings, coming later" ("…, coming in M2" for dated items).

## M2 Task 4: editor model (graph operations, connection rules)

- **Undrawn refusal copy (builder's call):** besides the plan's refusals, operations on a node or group that is gone refuse with "That node no longer exists" / "That group no longer exists"; a port id the node's definition does not have refuses with "That port does not exist"; `createGroup` with no existing node refuses with "Select at least one node to group"; `setPinnedVersion`/`setOverride` on a node that is not `core:run-subworkflow` refuse with "That node does not run a sub-workflow". The edge and group caps reuse the server's wording ("A workflow can have at most 2000 edges" / "… 100 groups").
- **`compatibleTargets` and sub-workflow start/end (builder's call):** in a sub-workflow trigger and finish nodes are excluded and `core:subworkflow-end` stays (it has a file input, and the palette offers "End"); in a main workflow the `subworkflow-io` nodes are excluded. Unavailable nodes are kept so the palette can list them disabled with the reason.
- **Connection check order:** self-loop, then the output and input exist, then the port types match, then the input is free; the edge cap is checked by `connect` after the rules. Ports are looked up on the right side only (`from` among outputs, `to` among inputs).
- **Run-node settings that are not an object** (e.g. `null`) are treated as `{}` by `setPinnedVersion`/`setOverride`, so those operations always leave an object with an `overrides` object.
- **Fixture names:** `tiggerPoohSub()` in `apps/web/src/test/fixtures/graphs.ts` is the server's `languageCleanupSub()` written out literally; `tiggerPoohMain` omits the server fixture's `extraNodes` option.
- **Placement rules (review fix):** `placementRefusal(def, graph, catalog)` is shared by `addNode` and `duplicateNode` and mirrors the server's TRIGGER_COUNT / SUBWORKFLOW_IO_COUNT / NODE_NOT_ALLOWED_HERE: "A workflow has exactly one trigger" (a second trigger in a workflow), "A sub-workflow has exactly one start" (a second start), "<title> is not allowed in a workflow" (Start/End in a workflow) and "<title> is not allowed in a sub-workflow" (trigger or finish in a sub-workflow), the last two in the server's wording; a second End is allowed. The graph's own `kind` is the workflow kind and the catalogue gives the kinds of the nodes already placed, so the signature is `(def, graph, catalog)` rather than the review's `(kind, def, graph)`. `duplicateNode` therefore takes the catalogue (`duplicateNode(g, id, catalog)`) and refuses a node of an unknown type with "Unknown node type <type>".
- **Other review-fix refusals:** `addNode` with an `id` already in the graph: "A node with the id <id> already exists"; `setLabel` past 120 characters (`MAX_LABEL_LENGTH`, the schema's `max(120)`): "A label can be at most 120 characters"; `setPinnedVersion` with a non-integer or a value below 1: "The pinned version must be a whole number of at least 1".

## M2 Task 5: editor state, issue mapping, graph equality, undo history

- **Node state precedence (builder's call):** among a node's issues the one with the strongest border wins the badge and word (red > amber > dashed), errors before warnings at the same level, and the first in server order on a tie; issues that draw nothing (`NODE_UNREACHABLE`, `OVERRIDE_DROPPED`, `NO_WRITE_PATH`, a warning `NODE_OUTDATED`) never take the word. `dim` is the strongest dim of any issue (0.6 over 0.7), independent of the winning border, so an unavailable node with a settings problem is amber and dimmed. `SUBWORKFLOW_OUTDATED` sets the badge `outdated-pin` only when nothing with a border is present.
- **Unknown codes and known codes outside the table:** an error is `generic-error` "error" with a red border, a warning is `generic-warning` "warning" with an amber border (the word keeps colour from carrying meaning alone). Codes the server sends with a `nodeId` but the N6 table does not list (`GRAPH_CYCLE` on the cycle's first node, `INPUT_MULTIPLE_EDGES` on the target node, `GROUP_MISSING`) render the same way.
- **`fieldIssues`** holds only `INVALID_SETTINGS` issues with a `settingPath` (Run-node `INVALID_OVERRIDE` issues stay in `issues` for Task 15).
