- **Broken edges:** `edgeStateFor(edgeId, issues, intoBroken?)` takes the set from `edgesIntoBroken(graph, states)` (edge ids whose target node has a red border, `states` being the per-node `NodeState` map), so the caller computes node states once.
- **Equality:** `stableStringify` is the server's copy except that it skips `undefined` object values (in-memory drafts may carry them; the wire drops them). `isLayoutOnlyChange` compares `bypassed ?? false`, ignores edge ids (endpoint multiset, as the server's `edgeKeys`) and node/edge order, and is true for two equal graphs.
- **Reducer details:** `EditorState` gains `coalesceKey` (`<coalesce>:<key>` of the last apply); a run of same-key applies shares one undo step and any select, undo, redo or save ends it. An apply whose graph equals the draft is a no-op (no empty undo step). `apply`, `undo` and `redo` do nothing while `readOnly`. Undo/redo drop selected ids that no longer exist. `saved` also updates `workflow.currentVersion`, `valid` and `errorCount` from the response issues; `rebase` updates `currentVersion`.

## M2 Task 6: React Flow adapter (`toFlow` / `fromFlow`)

- **Collapsed groups:** a member of a collapsed group is `hidden`; an edge with one end hidden is re-targeted to the group node with the handle id `'group'` (`GROUP_HANDLE`); an edge with both ends inside the same collapsed group is `hidden`. Edge ids never change. A node whose `groupId` names no group (`GROUP_MISSING`) has no parent and keeps its absolute position. The `trace` parameter is accepted but unused until Task 23.
- **Position mapping (builder's call):** only `position` changes with `dragging !== true` become graph moves — a finished drag and an arrow-key nudge (`dragging: false`) and the resizer's own position changes (no `dragging` key); positions while a drag is in progress map to nothing, so the canvas (Task 10) shows the live drag itself. Each batch yields one move op coalesced as `move` (the page supplies the key).
- **Leaving a group:** a child dropped (`dragging: false`) with its card centre (measured size, else `NODE_SIZE`) outside its group's box is ungrouped at its absolute position. Resizing never ungroups, even when the box shrinks past a member. Nothing groups a node by dropping it into a group (YAGNI; grouping is the explicit "Group" action).
- **Group drag and resize:** a dragged group moves its members by the same delta (the graph stores them absolute); a resize (`dimensions` with a boolean `resizing`) sets position and size via `resizeGroup`, and the members keep their absolute positions because React Flow compensates their relative positions in the same batch. Measurement-only `dimensions` changes (no `resizing`) map to nothing.
- **Removal:** removed groups are ungrouped (members stay) and removed nodes go through `removeNodes` in one op, so a refusal (a sub-workflow's start or end) is returned as a refusal. React Flow's own delete also cascades to a removed group's children and their edges; since Task 11 React Flow never deletes (no `deleteKeyCode`, no `deleteElements` call, no `onBeforeDelete`) — deletion is `removeSelection` from the keyboard and `removeNodes` from the toolbar.
- **Selection:** a `select` result is the complete next selection of the kind that changed — `fromNodeChanges` gives `nodes` and `group` (one group at a time: the one just selected, else the first still selected) with `edges: []`, `fromEdgeChanges` gives `edges` with `nodes: []`. React Flow reports both kinds in two calls of the same tick, so the page merges each result by kind against the latest state. `fromEdgeChanges` takes an optional second argument, the flow edges from `toFlow`, so an added edge keeps the ones already selected (the plan's signature had only `changes`).
- **Result shape:** results are aggregated per batch (geometry op, removal op, selection) and ignored changes are omitted rather than returned as `null`.
- **Review fixes:** the `saved` action carries the graph that was sent (`graph`); `saved` becomes that graph and `dirty` is `!graphEquals(draft, graph)`, so an edit made while the save was in flight stays unsaved. `editorReducer(s: EditorState | null, a)` returns a null state unchanged for every action except `loaded`, so it goes straight into `useReducer(editorReducer, initialEditorState())`. A `validating` with a seq lower than the latest is ignored, like a stale `validated`. On the drag path an apply that returns the draft itself is a no-op before any comparison, and `graphEquals` memoises each graph's serialisation in a `WeakMap` by reference (drafts are immutable values), so an apply serialises only the new graph once for both the no-op check and the dirty flag.

## M2 Task 7: node, handle and edge components

- **Port rows (screen 05):** row *i* pairs the *i*-th input (left edge) with the *i*-th output (right edge), as screen 05 draws "file … file" on one row; the rows sit under the 50 px header and the 40 px on-node row slot comes last (screen 05 order), so a one-in/one-out card is 50 + 30 + 40 = `NODE_SIZE.height`. Data rows are in `--kind-data`.
- **Badge (builder's call):** a pill with icon + word at the card's top-right whenever `NodeState.word` is set: plug-off for "plugin missing"/"unknown node", lock for "not on this server", "!" (screen 05's glyph) for the amber states, the workflow icon for "sub-workflow problem", alert for "not allowed here", "error" and a word without a badge kind ("update pending"). Its colour follows the border (red, amber, grey for dashed). `outdated-pin` draws nothing here (Task 15's pill). The card's `aria-label` appends the state word and, for a bypassed node, "skipped here".
- **Unknown node:** no definition → eyebrow "UNKNOWN", the plug-off icon (there is no kind to take an icon from), title = the type, and one handle per port that existing edges use (labelled with the raw port id), so the edges into it still draw red dashed (N6) instead of disappearing. A known node whose catalogue icon this build does not draw shows its kind icon.
- **Handle compatibility (N5):** `is-compatible` / `is-incompatible` come from React Flow's own connection state (`useConnection`) in `TcHandle` — opposite direction, another node, same port type — rather than a data attribute the canvas would have to set; the canvas (Task 10) needs to add nothing.
- **"+" button (N4):** a 44 px target around the 24 px lime dot, outside the card past the output handle; shown only on a free output and only when the canvas is editable (`readOnly` false in `EditorActionsContext`); it calls `startConnectFrom(nodeId, port)`. Its accessible name uses the catalogue label ("Connect from File", "Connect from Else").
- **Selection and focus:** a selected card gets a 2.5 px lime outline offset 3 px (so the state border stays visible); a keyboard-focused card gets the focus ring.
- **Edge arrowheads:** each arrowed edge (`edge-file`, `edge-path`) carries its own `<marker>` (id `tc-arrow-<edge id>`), filled by class, since SVG markers do not take the edge's stroke colour. The path-taken animation stops under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **Icons:** the node and kind icons are lucide's path data (ISC) inlined in `Icon.tsx`; `plug-off` is the handoff's plugin icon with a slash (screen 05). `Icon` now sets `data-icon` on the svg and exports `isIconName`.

## M2 Task 8: groups, minimap, zoom detail levels, node toolbar

- **Collapse action:** `EditorActions` gains `setGroupCollapsed(groupId, collapsed)` (no-op by default); the plan's interface had no way for `TcGroup`'s collapse button to reach the page. The page wires it to `setGroupCollapsed` (Task 4) in Task 10/14. On a read-only canvas (past version, narrow window, job trace) the button stays and collapse/expand is view-only local state: the page keeps a map group id → collapsed and draws `toFlow(withViewCollapse(graph, map), …)`; nothing is written to the graph, so an old version's collapsed group can be expanded.
- **Collapsed size:** `toFlow` gives a collapsed group the tile size 210 x 60 (`COLLAPSED_GROUP_SIZE`) as `style` and `initialWidth/Height`; the stored `size` is kept for expanding. No resizer on a collapsed tile.
- **Group header placement:** as screen 05 draws it, inside the dashed box at the bottom, in the 24 px group padding: name and "GROUP · N NODES" at bottom 4 px, left 14 px; the 26 px collapse chip at bottom 0, right 8 px, inside a 44 px target. Collapsed, they sit inside the tile.
- **Toolbar (N8):** buttons are 44 px wide (min-width) and drawn 34 px tall (editor spec 01) with a `::before` extending the hit area to 44 px tall, like `.btn-sm`, so every target is 44 x 44. Accessible names are the brief's words ("Duplicate", "Bypass"/"Un-bypass", "Delete"; screen 05 says "Bypass node"/"Delete node"); the toolbar is `role="toolbar"` "Node actions" (spec 01). "Try this node" is disabled with `title="needs the engine"` while `EditorActions.tryNode` is null. New icons `bypass` and `chevron-up` from screen 05.
- **Zoom levels (N9):** `compact` is the full card without the port labels and without the on-node row (rows and handles stay). This deviates from spec N9's 50 % drawing (a small icon + name pill): the card keeps its size so port rows, handles and group boxes do not jump. Task 14's on-node controls render nothing below `full` (compact shows only the row's summary text, see Task 14); `icon` is a 48 px tile in the kind colour that keeps one handle per port (all centred on their side) so edges still draw, no "+" buttons, the badge as its glyph only, and `aria-label` = title plus the state words. The canvas (Task 10) must set `minZoom` below 0.4 (React Flow's default is 0.5) or the icon level is never reached.
- **Minimap (N11):** classes come from `nodeClassName` (`miniNodeClass`: `tc-mini kind-…` plus `border-red|amber|dashed`; groups `tc-mini-group`, transparent and dashed) and `MiniNode` draws a rounded rect; the strokes use `vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke`. The minimap reads the user node's `initialWidth/Height` (toFlow always sets them).

## M2 Task 9: palette and node search

- **Disabled items:** palette items and search options that can't be added are `aria-disabled="true"` (as screen 05 draws "Generate subtitles"), not native `disabled`, so they stay focusable and their reason line is reachable; clicks and drags on them do nothing (`draggable="false"`). The reason is the item's accessible description (`aria-describedby`), the name stays the title. Precedence: read-only disables everything; an unavailable node shows its `availability.reason`; in a sub-workflow, trigger and finish nodes show "main workflow only".
- **Plugin mark:** plugin nodes show the plug icon after the title (screen 05) and their accessible name is "<title>, plugin".
- **Row height:** screen 05 draws 36 px palette rows with a 4 px gap; rows are 44 px with no gap (targets ≥ 44 px). New colour `--disabled-text` (#6a6770, screen 05's disabled item).
- **Sections:** a section is hidden when the search leaves it empty; "Sub-workflows" holds, in a sub-workflow, the End node first, then every other sub-workflow by name (never the one being edited; the search matches names). Start is never offered (a sub-workflow always has exactly one). Nothing left → "No nodes match “<query>”" (undrawn copy).
- **Node search heading:** the parent passes the plain title ("Add a node", "Drop on a file input"); with a port filter the overlay appends " · N matches" ("1 match"), N being the rows listed after the typed text. Without a filter it lists every node the graph kind allows (no start/end in a workflow; no trigger, finish or Start in a sub-workflow). It is a combobox (text input with `data-autofocus`) over a listbox: arrows move between available rows without wrapping, Enter or a click picks, Escape closes; the parent closes it after `onPick`. Each row shows icon, title, plug mark, kind word and the reason when unavailable.
- **Drag payload:** `PALETTE_DRAG_TYPE` = `application/x-transcoda-node`, data `JSON.stringify(item)`, `effectAllowed = 'copy'`. `searchRef` is typed `RefObject<HTMLInputElement | null>` (what React 19's `useRef(null)` returns); the "/" shortcut that focuses it is Task 12's.

## M2 Task 10: editor page, live validation, save and conflicts

- **Page shape:** `/workflows/:id` renders inside the Shell (side nav stays) as `.editor`: the header row, then palette 240 px | canvas | inspector column 340 px ("Select a node" until Task 13). The Shell is held to the window height while the editor is mounted (`.shell:has(.editor)`), so the canvas fills the rest. `EditorPage` keys a fresh `ReactFlowProvider` + editor by `id@version`, so another workflow or version starts with a fresh state, history and viewport.
- **Editable:** the canvas is editable when the workflow is the current version (no `?version=N`) and the node catalogue has loaded; otherwise palette, toolbar, connections, deletion and Save are off (`EditorActions.readOnly`). `?version=N` loads that version read-only with no banner yet (Task 18). Group collapse on a read-only canvas is view-only (`withViewCollapse` over a local map); on an editable one it is a graph op.
- **Live validation:** `useValidation` sends `workflows.validate({ graph, id })` 400 ms after the last edit, one request in flight; an edit made meanwhile is sent when it returns. A response for a draft that has since changed is dropped; a failed call keeps the issues (new reducer action `validateFailed` clears `validating`). The draft that arrives with the load is not validated (its issues came with it).
- **Save:** exactly `{ id, baseVersion, graph }` (the note waits for Task 18's contract change; the header's "Save with a note…" menu exists behind `noteMenu`, off). Success → `saved` with the sent graph, `updatedAt` set to now for the pill, toast "Saved vN". `VERSION_CONFLICT` → `ConflictDialog` (Escape closes it and keeps the draft; "Reload the latest version" re-runs the load; "Save over it" → `rebase` then save with `baseVersion = currentVersion`). Any other failure → error toast `describeError(e).message` with "Try again"; draft and dirty flag untouched. `ConflictDialog` takes an extra `onClose` (Dialog needs one).
- **Header:** pill "vN · saved 2 min ago" / "vN · unsaved changes" (re-rendered every 30 s); it is a button for the versions panel (Task 18). The name is a button (title "Rename") that becomes an inline input (Enter/blur saves through `workflows.rename`, Escape cancels; disabled when read-only). "Try on a file" disabled with title "needs the engine". Save reads "Save v<savedVersion + 1>", primary when dirty and editable. The header buttons are drawn 40 px (screen 05) and the pill 32 px, each with a `::before` hit area of 44 px.
- **Adding nodes:** palette click adds at the canvas centre, a drop at the drop point (card header under the pointer); a spot already taken moves the new card right by card width + 70 px until it is free. A sub-workflow item adds a Run node pinned to that sub-workflow's current version with empty overrides (the plan puts this in Task 15; added here so the item is never a silent no-op). "+" on an output opens the node search ("Drop on a file input" / "Drop on a data input"); the picked node is placed right of the origin and connected to its first input of that port type in one undo step. Every refusal (placement, caps, start/end rules) is an error toast.
- **Connections:** `isValidConnection` is `checkConnection`; a connection released on (or clicked onto) a port that does not fit shows the reason as a toast via `onConnectEnd` / `onClickConnectEnd`. During a drag, `TcHandle` marks ports `is-compatible` / `is-incompatible` from React Flow's `useConnection` (checked in the browser).
- **Canvas state:** React Flow nodes are derived from the draft; a drag moves a local overlay (`applyNodeChanges`) and only the drop dispatches the move (coalesced per drag). Measurements carry over to newly derived nodes so cards are not re-measured on every edit. Dropped positions and resized group sizes are rounded to whole pixels (`fromFlow`). Selection results are merged by kind against the latest sent selection. Deleting a group ungroups it (its unselected members and their edges stay); a deletion the graph refuses (last Start/End of a sub-workflow) changes nothing and shows a toast. (Superseded in Task 11: Delete/Backspace go through `useEditorKeys` → `removeSelection`; React Flow's delete and `onBeforeDelete` are gone.)
- **Viewport:** `minZoom` 0.1 so the icon zoom level is reachable; a non-empty graph is fitted on load at most at 100 %; an empty one starts at 100 %. Controls sit bottom-right with the minimap stacked above them.
- **Toolbar keyboard path (N8):** Enter or "." on a focused node selects it and moves focus to its toolbar's first enabled button; Escape in the toolbar returns focus to the node (since Task 11 the node stays selected: the toolbar is `nokey` and stops Escape). `TcToolbar` carries `data-node-id`.
- **Groups from a selection:** `createGroup` takes optional measured card sizes (React Flow's `measured`), falling back to `NODE_SIZE`, so tall cards do not cover the group header.

## M2 Task 11: issues panel, canvas banner, keyboard path, viewport, narrow and catalogue-less states

- **Issues panel:** a `<details>` (open by default) at the bottom of the inspector column; summary "N errors · M warnings", "1 error", "No issues". Entries are errors first, then warnings (server order within each); each is a button: icon + a visually hidden "Error"/"Warning", the node title (label, else catalogue title, else the type; an unknown id shows the id), "Edge" or "Workflow", and the server's message. Clicking selects and centres: a node (or its collapsed group) is centred at most at 100 % with 40 % padding, an edge by its two ends, a workflow entry fits the whole graph; with `settingPath` it goes through `EditorActions.focusField` (Task 13 focuses the field). The centring computes the viewport directly (`getNodesBounds` + `getViewportForBounds` + `setViewport`) because React Flow 12's `fitView` is queued until the next node update.
- **Canvas banners:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: no "Can't enable" or review banner; the can't-run banner and the info banner of the save's changes replace them.)* a stack at the top of the canvas (`role="status"` each): the narrow notice, the catalogue notice, then `CanvasBanner`'s "Can't enable: <first error message>. Dry runs still work." (one trailing full stop of the message is dropped so the sentence reads once) and the review banner "<reviewNote> — Review, then enable to clear this." ("This workflow changed" when the server sends no note; undrawn copy). The "Install plugin" link waits for M3.
- **Keyboard (`useEditorKeys`, on `window`):** Tab / Shift+Tab move the selection to the next / previous node in graph order (x, then y; nodes inside a collapsed group are skipped) and focus it (React Flow pans to a focused node) — only while a node is selected and focus is on the canvas, never in the header, palette, inspector or node toolbar. No wrap (fix round): Tab past the last node and Shift+Tab before the first fall through to the browser, so the canvas is never a Tab trap. The order is the graph's x-then-y reading order, not the DOM order React Flow renders (insertion order); the DOM order still applies before a node is selected and once Tab leaves the canvas. "c" with exactly one node selected opens the node search filtered to that node's first free output (toast "This node has no free output"; ignored while a connection is being dragged). "/" opens the node search. Delete/Backspace remove the whole selection (edges, a selected group is ungrouped, nodes) as one undo step. Mod+Z undo, Shift+Mod+Z and Mod+Y redo, Mod+S save (only when dirty and editable; default always prevented, also from a text field, so the browser's save dialog never opens). Every key is ignored in fields, contenteditable and dialogs, and while a dialog of the page (search, conflict) is open; Delete, Backspace and "c" are also ignored on buttons, links, `summary` and elements with an interactive role (button, switch, slider, combobox, textbox, radio, checkbox, menuitem, tab, option). React Flow's `deleteKeyCode` is off, so Delete has exactly one binding and it is undoable. Editing keys do nothing on a read-only canvas.
- **Toolbar keys:** the node toolbar carries React Flow's `nokey` class and stops Escape and arrow keys, so they no longer reach the node's own handler: Escape returns focus to the node and the node stays selected; arrows do not nudge it. Enter or "." on a node prevents its default, so the same key press no longer activates the toolbar button that just took focus (found in the browser: Enter duplicated the node).
- **Colour key:** a `<details>` "Key" bottom-left of the canvas (screen 05 draws a "?" button with a dialog; a disclosure needs no focus handling), with screen 05's rows LINES, HANDLES, STATES, ICONS, KEYS verbatim. React Flow's attribution moves to bottom-centre to make room.
- **Viewport memory:** `localStorage` `tc.viewport.<workflowId>` = `{x,y,zoom}`, written on every move end and read once per editor mount (also for `?version=N`); anything that is not three finite numbers with zoom > 0, or blocked storage, falls back to fitting the graph.
- **Narrow window:** below 1024 px (`matchMedia('(max-width: 1023px)')`, live) the editor is read-only (palette, toolbar, connections, keys that edit, Save) with the notice "The editor needs a wider window (1024 px). You can look, not edit."; Save's title says "Needs a wider window". The side columns shrink to 180 / 240 px there so the canvas keeps some room.
- **Catalogue unavailable:** when `nodes.list` fails, the banner "Node catalogue unavailable — reload" with a "Reload" button (`useCatalog().reload()`); the canvas is read-only, every node is drawn without a definition ("UNKNOWN", plug icon), nothing is validated or saved, Save's title is "Node catalogue unavailable".
- **New-node placement:** palette clicks, drops on an occupied spot and search picks go to the free spot nearest the target that lies wholly inside the visible canvas (a grid through the target, 40 px steps, coarser when zoomed out; vertical distance weighted 1.5 so cards prefer the right). Cards keep 40 px / 60 px clearance (cards with several ports are taller than 120 px). A full view cascades +40/+40 from the target, kept inside the view. Without a measured canvas the old step-right rule applies (`editor/model/placement.ts`).
- **Handle hit area:** each handle gets a 26 px invisible circle (`::after`, inset −8 px) around its 14 px dot, and React Flow's `connectionRadius` is 30 px.
- **Fix round (review):** the conflict dialog stays open while "Reload the latest version" or "Save over it" runs (both buttons disabled, `aria-busy`, a status line) and shows a failure inside the dialog (role=alert, `describeError` message, "Try again" repeats the failed action); a second conflict during save-over re-targets the dialog at the newer version. `saved` takes the response's issues only when the draft still equals the sent graph (otherwise the queued validation owns them). The pill reads `EditorState.savedAt` (the workflow's `updatedAt` at load — the API has no per-version time on `get` — and the client clock after a save), so a rename no longer reads as "saved just now". A save is skipped when the draft is clean (unless saving over a newer version) and after the page is gone, so a stale "Try again" toast never makes an empty version. A refused move or resize puts the cards back at their graph positions. The issues panel drops a leading "<node title>: " (the server's message format) from a message shown under that node.

## M2 Task 12: JSON Schema fields, field controls, language picker


- **Default settings live in schema-fields.** `defaultSettings(schema)` (top-level `default`s, deep-copied) replaces graph-ops' `defaultSettingsFromSchema`; `addNode` uses it.
- **"Original (from data)" is the unset value.** `LanguageField` with `originalOption` shows "Original (from data)" instead of "not set" when the value is undefined; picking it clears the setting (the picker emits the sentinel `ORIGINAL_FROM_DATA = 'original'`, which never matches the language pattern, and the field maps it to `undefined`). The schema offers no other valid way to say "from data". Engine semantics of an unset `has-stream.language` with a connected `data:language` input are the engine track's to confirm.
- **Language table.** 180 ISO 639-1 codes: all except the extinct Avestan (ae), Church Slavic (cu) and Pali (pi); constructed/classical codes media files carry (eo, ia, ie, io, vo, la, sa) are kept. English names are CLDR's (generated once with `Intl.DisplayNames`, then static) with four overrides to the ISO names: bn Bengali, tw Twi (CLDR says Akan), tl Tagalog, ny Chichewa. Sorted by name; "Untagged" (`unknown`) last. Three-letter codes are valid settings and display as the code.
- **Picker search order:** exact code, then name prefix, then code prefix, then name substring; Enter takes the first (Original first when offered and matching).
- **Client checks are `minimum`/`maximum`, `enum` (also each entry of an enum list) and `required` only.** `exclusiveMinimum` (video-is heights/bitrate) and `minLength` stay server-side; empty input already means "not set".
- **"not set" and Clear:** optional + undefined → "not set"; optional + value + editable → "Clear" (sets undefined, so the default shows as placeholder). Required fields have neither; their "Required" comes from `clientIssue` through the message slot.
- **Slider when `max − min ≤ 100` inclusive** (size-gate 0–100 and quality 0–63 get one; bitrate 32–1024 does not). Integers step 1, numbers step "any"; `coerce` drops non-finite input; out-of-range values are passed through with the live issue (the server decides).
- **Segmented enum = buttons with `role="radio"`** in a `role="radiogroup"` with a roving tab stop and arrow keys, as the brief says (Biome's `useSemanticElements` suppressed with a reason). Chip lists are `<fieldset aria-labelledby>`.
- **Password fields never read back:** the input starts empty with "Saved; type to replace" when a value exists; only non-empty typing is sent; Clear removes it.
- **Raw JSON fields are labelled "<label> (raw JSON)"** so the raw fallback is identifiable (Task 13's `/raw/i` query). Unparseable text shows "Not valid JSON" and is not sent; empty text means not set; outside changes (undo) replace the text, own edits are not reformatted.
- **`SettingField`** (extra file `fields/SettingField.tsx`) dispatches kind → control; **`LanguagePicker` has an optional `title`** (default "Pick a language"; the chip list uses "Add language").
- **`onNodeControlKeys`:** an `onNodeSettings` key missing from the schema is a summary.


### Task 12 fix round (supersedes any "Original = unset" wording above)

- **Unset language means "any language"** (core:has-stream matches any language when `language` is unset and, in M1, ignores its `data:language` input). The UI reads it "Any language". "Original (from data)" is not offered and is never an alias for unset; `originalOption` is kept, off by default, until a settings value can express "from data".
- **Chip × hit area 36 × 36 instead of 44 px:** screen 05 draws 30 px chips with 22 px × buttons packed side by side; a 44 px area would overlap the neighbouring chip. All other field targets (add/toggle chips, segmented buttons, chip text input, switches, inputs) are ≥ 44 px.
- **Long descriptions split** at the first "; " or " — " into label + hint (hint shown under the label and in the control's description).
- **Enum display text:** multi-word ids sentence-cased, single tokens as stored; values never change.
- **Password fields:** never read back, `autocomplete="new-password"`, empty input clears. A secret typed into a node setting is stored in the workflow graph and therefore in every saved workflow version (real secret handling is M3 plugin settings).
- **Unset slider:** shows the default (or minimum) muted with a "default" cue and `aria-valuetext "<n> (default)"`.

The earlier decision "'Original (from data)' is the unset value" is withdrawn; concern 4 is resolved; concern 3 is replaced by the 36 × 36 decision above.

### Task 11 fix round

- **One delete path:** `removeSelection(g, { nodes, edges, group? })` in `graph-ops` (edges disconnected, a selected group ungrouped, nodes removed with their edges, refusals passed through) is the keyboard's delete; Canvas's `onBeforeDelete` is removed (nothing calls React Flow's `deleteElements`; `deleteKeyCode` is null).
- **Editor shell (deviation from the design doc, in favour of screen 05):** the design doc keeps the side nav in the editor; screen 05 draws the editor without the app's top bar and nav. In the editor the top bar is dropped (the editor header's breadcrumb "Workflows / name" is the way back) and the side nav collapses to a 56 px icon rail: the mark (link "Transcoda home"), the nav items as 44 px icons whose names stay in the accessible name (visually hidden) and in a `title` tooltip ("Jobs, coming in M2"), and a log-out icon button ("Log out <user>") at the end. `Shell rail` is used only on `/workflows/:id`. Read-only and narrow keep the palette visible but disabled. "Workflows" stays the current nav item inside a workflow. Between 1024 and 1279 px the editor's side columns are 200 / 280 px (screen 05's 240 / 340 left the canvas under 400 px at 1024).
- **Issues panel:** the list scrolls by itself (max 40 vh). An issue of a node deleted since the last validation reads "Removed node" until validation returns.
- **Banner text:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: the review-note hint and "Review, then enable to clear this" no longer exist; review notes are gone.)* a node's error is led by the node's name ("Replace original: Input file is not connected") unless the message starts with it or quotes it; the error banner has `aria-live="off"` (it changes with every edit; the issues panel is the place to read them), the notices keep `role="status"`. The server's review notes end with "; check the workflow before enabling it"; the banner drops that hint and adds its own "— Review, then enable to clear this.".
- **Overlays:** new-node placement and issue centring use the canvas area below the banners and above the bottom row (key, zoom controls: 72 px); the minimap's column on the right is not excluded.

## M2 Task 13: inspector


- **Inspector props:** `dispatch` is the page's guarded `apply(op, opts)` (refusals → toast, read-only no-op), not the reducer dispatch; added `readOnly` (page's `!editable`: read-only version, narrow window, catalogue unavailable) and `catalog: Catalog | null` (null → every node shown as unknown, raw, read-only).
- **Undo granularity:** settings edits coalesce per node and field (`settings:<nodeId>:<key>`); typing a name coalesces as `settings:label:<nodeId>` (no change to Canvas's `ApplyOptions`). The whole-value raw field uses key `''`.
- **Server message first:** a field shows the server's `INVALID_SETTINGS` message for its path (exact, then nested `key.n`), else the client's (`clientIssue`). So a missing required URL reads the server's "Needs a setting" once validated (client "Required" only before the first validation). Path-less settings messages show above the form, or in the whole-value raw field when settings is not an object.
- **State line** = card's glyph + sentence-cased word; unavailable reads "Not on this server: <availability.reason>" (falls back to the issue message). The node's other non-settings messages are listed under it with the "<title>: " prefix stripped (IssuesPanel's `messageUnder`).
- **Group select grows the group box** around the joined node's card (NODE_SIZE + 24 px padding) in the same undo step, so membership never draws a node outside its box. Leaving a group keeps the box.
- **"On the canvas" open state** persists in `localStorage` key `tc.inspector.on-the-canvas` (`open`/`closed`), guarded by try/catch.
- **Emptying the whole-value raw field** sets settings to `{}`.
- **Focus requests** from the issues panel live only while their node is the single selection; otherwise dropped.

## M2 Task 14: on-node row, groups UI, bypass

- **On-node row (N12):** one row per card, for the first key of `onNodeControlKeys(def)`. The label is the schema description with its unit put back ("Quality (CRF)", "Minimum saving (%)"). A missing value shows the schema default (input placeholder; summary text); "needs a setting" replaces the row when the value is missing and required, or the server flags that key with "Needs a setting". Settings that are not an object draw no row (the inspector's raw field handles them).
- **Number control:** 28 px input (`nodrag nopan nowheel`); text that does not coerce (empty, not a number) is not applied and the old value stays; an out-of-range value is applied like the inspector does (the server decides) and the bound shows as the input's `title` with `aria-invalid`. Card edits coalesce per node (page key `settings:<nodeId>`), separate from the inspector's per-field undo steps.
- **Summary:** a real button over the row (aria-label "<label>: <value>. Edit in the inspector"); lists are up to 4 mono chips plus "+N"; language codes stay codes (`unknown` reads "untagged", full name as `title`); a language list with boolean `keepOriginal`/`keepUntagged` siblings adds "original"/"untagged" (Keep languages: a view over three fields). Scalars read "label · value". Click selects the node and focuses the inspector field.
- **Compact zoom (controller ruling):** no controls and no chips; only the summary text ("Quality (CRF) · 24", "en · de · original · untagged") in a 40 px line (`.tc-node-summary`), so the card keeps its full-zoom height. Icon zoom: nothing.
- **Selection toolbar:** with ≥ 2 cards selected, the first selected card renders one `NodeToolbar` over the whole selection: "Group N nodes" and "Delete N nodes" (read-only canvases: none). Only selected cards subscribe to the selection list.
- **Shift-click multi-select:** React Flow's `multiSelectionKeyCode` is `['Meta', 'Control', 'Shift']` (Canvas), so Shift-click adds a card as the UI check expects; Shift-drag on the pane still box-selects.
- **New group:** `createGroup('Group', selection, measured card sizes)` (controller note: sized from measured nodes so the bottom header clears them); the group becomes the selection and the group inspector focuses and selects its name. The page's focus request now targets a node or a group (`targetId`).
- **Group inspector:** "Group name" (≤ 120 chars, typing coalesces as `settings:group:<id>`; emptied → "Group" on blur), "N nodes", "Collapsed" switch (`setGroupCollapsed`), "Ungroup" (members stay). Read-only: all disabled (the canvas toggle stays view-only there).
- **Collapsed tile:** Task 8's `.tc-group.is-collapsed` already is the tile (name + "GROUP · N NODES"); no separate `.tc-group-tile` class was added.
- **Bypass:** unchanged from Tasks 7/13 (toolbar and inspector switch both `toggleBypass`); pinned by a page test.


## M2 Task 16: TV cleanup example and empty-state actions


- **M2 Task 16 — TV cleanup example:**
  - The example graphs are written out literally in `apps/web/src/examples/tv-cleanup.ts`, because the web app never imports server code. They have the same nodes, settings and edges as `apps/server/src/workflows/fixtures/tigger-pooh.ts`.
  - Main layout: two rows 240 px apart with a 300 px step. The HEVC branch is on top. The else branch (encode video) starts under "Video is", so both Run nodes share one column and the "Cleanup" group (id `cleanup`) can surround them. Its box is computed with `NODE_SIZE` + `GROUP_PADDING` like `createGroup`.
  - `tvCleanupMain(subId, subVersion)` always takes the real pin; no exported placeholder graph is needed. The brief's `'__sub__'`/0 placeholder is therefore not a separate export.
- **Pinned version:** `createTvCleanupExample` pins the Run nodes to the version the sub-workflow save returned, which is 2 for a fresh sub-workflow, instead of a hard-coded 2. It uses the created workflow's `version` as `baseVersion`, which is 1.
- **Clean-up on failure:**
  - A failure after the first create deletes the main workflow first (it may reference the sub-workflow), then the sub-workflow.
  - Delete failures are swallowed and the original error is rethrown.
  - The list reloads after a failed example so any leftover shows up.
- **Empty-state actions:**
  - "Add a trigger" and the example share one busy flag. Both empty-state buttons and the header "Examples" button are disabled while either is pending, and the action row has `aria-busy`.
  - Error toasts read "Could not create the workflow: …" and "Could not create the example: …".
- **Header "Examples" menu:** a secondary button "Examples" with a chevron, a `role="menu"` list labelled "Examples", and the single item "Start from “TV cleanup” example". It reuses the row-menu styling and behaviour: focus goes to the first item, Escape returns focus to the button, and an outside click closes it. It is shown only when the list is non-empty; the empty state already offers both actions.
- **`?focus=palette`:** focuses the palette search once per editor mount, once the workflow and catalogue are loaded. It scrolls the TRIGGER section with `block: 'nearest'`. The query parameter stays in the URL, which does no harm because the focus is one-shot per mount.


## M2 Task 18: versions panel, past versions, restore, save notes


- `workflows.save` takes an optional `note` (`z.string().trim().max(120).optional()`), stored in `workflow_versions.note`; additive, no behaviour change. The server stores an empty (all-blank) note as `null` (`input.note || null`, not `??`), so the history never shows an empty note.
- "Layout only" is computed client-side (`saveNoteFor`) from `isLayoutOnlyChange`, the server's safe-change list (positions, labels, groups); a user's note from "Save with a note…" wins; any other change saves without a note.
- Restore = a normal save of the past version's graph on top of the current version with note "Restored from vN"; a new version, history untouched; the server's pin/review rules apply as for any save. A restore conflict uses the Task 10 dialog: "Reload the latest version" opens the current version (drops the restore), "Save over it" restores on top of the newer one.
- Versions panel row: `vN · <note> · <relative time> · <author>`; note falls back to "saved by you" (createdBy = current user id) or "saved" (someone else); author = the current user's username for own versions, "automatic update" for `system:upgrade`, else the stored createdBy id (the API has no user lookup).
- The panel is a non-modal `role="dialog"` under the pill (not the modal `Dialog` component), so the canvas stays usable; Escape, outside press or a row click closes it.
- `?version=N` equal to the current version (only reachable by URL; the panel links the current version to the plain route) shows "Viewing vN — the current version" with a link "Edit it" instead of a restore.
- The pill's time is the shown version's own `createdAt` from `workflows.versions` (controller note on Task 10).


## M2 Task 15: sub-workflow authoring, Run node inspector, overrides

1. The pin banner in the singular reads "1 workflow pins an older version; it keeps running the version it pinned until updated." The plural is the design's text exactly.
2. The overrides editor drops start, end and nested Run settings on the client as well. The server already excludes them, so this is defense in depth (controller note). Overrides on such keys show as "will be dropped on save".
3. A row label comes from `fieldsFor` over the entry's schema: the description head with its unit and hint split off, or a sentence-cased setting key. The brief says "description ?? settingKey"; the result is the same for core nodes and reads better for keys without a description.
4. The override control is marked required, so it has no Clear button; the Override switch is how you remove a value. An emptied control keeps the row open (and the key unset) until you switch it off.
5. Each row shows the child's value as "Default <text>". Arrays are joined with ", " and keep their codes ("en, de"). Booleans are "yes"/"no", empty arrays "none", null "not set", objects JSON.
6. The picker sorts by name with the current choice first. It shows "Choose a sub-workflow" (disabled) when no id is set, and leaves out the workflow being edited.
7. "(no longer exists)" appears only when the server says so (NOT_FOUND from `get` or a SUBWORKFLOW_MISSING issue). It never appears just because the list has not loaded yet. "Open sub-workflow" is hidden for a missing sub-workflow.
8. "Update to vN" uses the larger of the SUBWORKFLOW_OUTDATED issue's "latest is N" and the list summary's `currentVersion`.
9. Any other failure to load the child shows "Could not load its settings: <message>" with "Try again", inline in the inspector.
10. Override edits are undo-coalesced per key (`settings` / `<nodeId>:overrides.<key>`). Re-picking and Update are single undo steps.


### M2 Task 13 fix round (supersedes the Task 13 wording above where they differ)

- **Settings messages, client first:** the inspector shows the client's own check (required → "Required", bounds, enum) first. The server's `INVALID_SETTINGS` message shows only when the client has none, and only while the setting still holds the value the server validated (`EditorState.issuesGraph`). The state line follows the same effective issues. A path-less server message counts only while no setting has changed. The canvas card still shows the server's last answer.
- **Inspector layout (screen 05):** fixed header, a scrolling body, a pinned footer, and the issues panel as its own region below.
- **Footer buttons** are btn-sm, drawn at 36 px with a 44 px hit area. Accepted.
- **Group join** grows the box around the card's measured size (falling back to NODE_SIZE) plus 24 px padding.
- **Group names are never empty** (graph-ops refuses them); the inspector restores "Group" when the field is left blank.
- **Run nodes:** the header does not repeat what the Run inspector shows inline. An override that is switched on shows its label once; the control's own label is for screen readers only (`labelHidden`).
- **On-node number:** the input shows what is typed; a value that is not a number is never sent, and blur or undo shows the stored value again.


## M2 Task 19: leave guard, undo/redo controls, save shortcut

1. **No history blocker; browser back is covered by beforeunload only.** wouter 3.13 has no navigation blocker. The guard covers in-app links and `useGuardedNavigate` only. Browser back/forward, reload and closing the tab rely on the `beforeunload` prompt that is armed while dirty. Back/forward between SPA routes does not fire `beforeunload` at all, so pressing back with a dirty draft loses it silently. This is the recorded deviation.
2. **`apps/web/src/lib/navigation.tsx`, not `.ts`.** It exports the `GuardedLink` component (JSX).
3. **LeaveDialog has an optional `error` prop** (an addition to the brief's interface). A failed "Save and leave" shows inline in the dialog and keeps the draft dirty. A VERSION_CONFLICT closes the leave dialog and opens the conflict dialog instead.
4. **Footer order Stay / Leave / Save and leave**, primary on the right as in the other dialogs, with the initial focus on "Stay" (the safe choice). The dialog uses the default tone: there is no "Cancel" button for the danger tone to focus, so `data-autofocus` on Stay does that job.
5. **A guarded link to the route already shown is not leaving** (for example the versions-panel row for the current version while on it).
6. **Links routed through the guard** beyond the brief's list: the rail home mark, the top-bar brand and PastVersionBanner "Edit it". Programmatic navigations after a restore or a conflict reload stay raw; they only run on a non-dirty past-version view.
7. **Undo/Redo icons** are lucide undo-2/redo-2 (ISC), 44 px wide. They take the editor header's existing 40 px button height, like Save and the save menu button.
8. **Undo history survives a save** and is cleared only on load (initial load, version switch, conflict reload), as the design says ("cleared on load").


## M2 Task 17: dry-run/enabled switch and enable confirmation

*(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: the ActivationControl and Activate dialog replace the switch.)*

- The mode switch is two buttons with `role="radio"` (Biome's useSemanticElements is suppressed with a reason). There is one tab stop on the checked radio. Arrow keys move focus only and never change the mode; Space, Enter or a click changes it. This deviates from the APG radio pattern on purpose, because checking ENABLED has to go through a confirmation.
- A blocked and unchecked ENABLED shows a lock icon instead of the check, as screen 05 draws it.
- In the editor, the switch is read-only whenever the editor is (past version, narrow window, catalogue unavailable).
- The dialog footer follows the app's convention (secondary "Keep in dry run" left, primary "Enable" right) instead of screen 06's Enable-first row. In Phase A, "Shows" is not bold because `libraryLine` is a plain string.
- A failed switch to dry run is an error toast with "Try again" (there is no dialog for it to be inline in). Failures inside the enable dialog stay inline.
- The refused state lists only error-severity issues, with the server's messages unprefixed.
- `enableBlocked` and `LIBRARY_LINE` live in ModeSwitch.tsx so the editor and the list share them.


## M2 Phase A: editor

Summary of Phase A (Tasks 1–20) and the Task 20 gate. The task sections above hold the detail; this section is the one place to look first.

- **File layout:** `apps/web/src/editor/model` holds pure, React-free logic (graph operations, connection rules, issue → state mapping, equality and layout-only diff, undo history, the editor reducer, schema → field mapping, placement, save notes); `editor/flow` holds the React Flow adapter (`toFlow` / `fromFlow`) and the canvas components (`TcNode`, `TcHandle`, `TcEdge`, `TcGroup`, `TcMiniMap`, `TcToolbar`, zoom levels, the actions context); `editor/ui` holds everything around the canvas (palette, node search, inspector and fields, Run sub-workflow inspector and overrides, issues panel, banners, header, mode switch and dialogs, versions panel, keys, viewport memory, leave guard). Pages compose them; `editor/model` never imports from `flow` or `ui`.
- **Positions:** the graph stores absolute canvas coordinates for every node, grouped or not. The adapter converts to React Flow's parent-relative positions for group members and back on drop, rounding to whole pixels. React Flow state (`selected`, `measured`, `dragging`, viewport) is never written into the graph; the save payload is exactly `{ id, baseVersion, graph, note? }`.
- **NODE_OUTDATED** is drawn by the server's severity: today the server reports it as an error, so the card is red with the word "update pending" and enabling is blocked; a warning would draw nothing on the card (inspector note only).
- **Start/End colours:** the `subworkflow-io` nodes use the trigger colour (Start) and the finish colour (End); their kind word is "Sub-workflow".
- **Keyboard path:** Tab / Shift+Tab walk the nodes in x-then-y order without trapping focus; Enter or "." moves into the selected node's toolbar, Escape back; "/" opens the node search, "c" connects from the selected node's first free output; Delete/Backspace remove the selection as one undo step; ⌘Z, ⇧⌘Z / ⌘Y, ⌘S. Details in Task 11.
- **Undrawn copy** (screens do not draw these states): conflict "Someone saved vN while you were editing" with "Reload the latest version" / "Save over it"; leave "Leave without saving?" / "Your unsaved changes will be lost." with Stay / Leave / Save and leave; delete "Delete <name>?" (an in-use sub-workflow lists its users instead); sub-workflow banners "1 workflow pins an older version; it keeps running the version it pinned until updated." while a used sub-workflow has unsaved changes, and the review banner "<changes> — Review, then enable to clear this."
- **localStorage keys:** `tc.viewport.<workflowId>` (`{x,y,zoom}`) and `tc.inspector.<section>` (`open`/`closed`); every access is in try/catch and the page works without storage.
- **Constants:** validate debounce 400 ms; refetch coalescing ≤ 1/s per resource; undo depth 100; toast 4 s (errors stay until dismissed); duplicate offset +40/+40 px; group padding 24 px; node card width 210 px.
- **Leave guard limitation:** wouter has no history blocker. In-app links, guarded navigations and (since Task 20) Log out ask first; browser back/forward between app routes does not, and drops a dirty draft silently. Reload and closing the tab are covered by `beforeunload`.
- **Settings nav tag** reads "later" instead of screen 05's "M2" (Jan's answer 2: Settings is not in M2).
- **28 px on-node inputs** are the one exception to the ≥ 44 px target rule, as N12 draws them; the inspector keeps the full-size control for the same setting.
- **Biome** skips the generated `apps/web/src/test/fixtures/nodes.json` (`biome.json` `!apps/web/src/test/fixtures/nodes.json`); a server test keeps it equal to the live catalogue.
- **Production bundle (Task 20 Step 1):** `pnpm build` gives one JS chunk of 683 kB (212 kB gzip) and one CSS file of 95 kB (27 kB gzip) that includes React Flow's stylesheet; Vite warns about the > 500 kB chunk. Code splitting is left for later (one self-hosted page, loaded once).

### Task 20 gate: walkthrough findings

Built by hand in a real browser from an empty instance (throwaway config dir, 127.0.0.1 behind a `.localhost` host name so cookies stay separate): sub-workflow "Language cleanup" (Keep languages en/de/original/untagged → Set default track audio en → Reorder tracks en, de) and workflow "TV cleanup" (Manual input → Video is hevc → pass: Run "Language cleanup" with Languages to keep = en, de, tr → Cover and attachments → Size gate 0 → Replace original; else: Encode video → Run (same override) → Replace original), the two Run nodes grouped as "Cleanup", saved, enabled through the dialog; then `ja` added in the sub-workflow → the parent listed "Outdated sub-workflow"; both pins updated, saved → review banner and dry run; enabled again. Fixed in Task 20 (with tests):

- **Two issues of one node read the same.** An empty Run node gets two `INVALID_SETTINGS` ("Needs a setting") for `subworkflowId` and `version`; the issues panel listed "Run sub-workflow / Needs a setting" twice. A settings issue now names its setting with the inspector's label ("Sub-workflow: Needs a setting", "Pinned version: Needs a setting"), unless the message already starts with it.
- **The review note repeated a change.** One sub-workflow pinned by two Run nodes gave "Updated sub-workflow Language cleanup 2→3; Updated sub-workflow Language cleanup 2→3". The banner now says each change once (the server is unchanged; it still lists one change per Run node).
- **Banner text wrapped under its icon** when long (`flex-wrap`); the text now shrinks beside the icon and only an action button wraps.

Controller additions folded into Task 20 (from the Tasks 17 and 19 reviews):

- **Log out asks the leave guard.** The guard holds a continuation, `{ to }` or `{ run }` (`lib/navigation.tsx` `guardedRun`); Leave and Save and leave continue it, Stay drops it. Both the rail and the top-bar Log out go through it.
- **"Open sub-workflow"** in the Run inspector is a `GuardedLink`.
- **Save and leave stays** when the draft changed while the save ran ("It changed while saving. Save again to keep the new changes."), so edits made during the save are never dropped by leaving.
- **Raw `navigate()`** after a restore and on a restore conflict's reload are commented: both run on a read-only past version, which is never dirty.
- **Sub-workflows have no mode (controller ruling).** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: a sub-workflow still has no mode and no switch, but there are no review chips or banners and `setMode` is gone; `activate` refuses a sub-workflow with `KIND_MISMATCH`.)* A sub-workflow runs in the mode of the workflow that runs it, so the list and the editor show no dry-run/enabled switch for it (the list's mode cell shows "—" with that explanation as a tooltip), and no "Needs review" chip or "enable to clear this" banner, since enabling is what clears a review. Its "Used by N" stays in the list column and the editor header. The server still accepts `setMode` on a sub-workflow; the UI just never calls it.
- **GuardedLink** has tests for modified and non-primary clicks: wouter's `Link` already leaves those to the browser before our handler runs, so the guard is not asked and nothing is held.

Found and left for later (not fixed in Task 20):

- **Enable dialog promises a recycle copy (superseded).** "Each original moves to the recycle folder and is kept for 14 days." shows even when Replace original has no recycle folder set ("Empty means no recycle copy"). The fixed copy from screen 06 should follow the Replace original nodes' settings (or the library's) once libraries exist (Phase B). Superseded: the engine always recycles (a library's recycle folder defaults to `.transcoda-recycle`, there is no off-switch) and keeps originals 7 days, so the dialog says "up to 7 days" (see the Task 27 entry below); the "14 days" and "Empty means no recycle copy" wording is stale.
- **"Video is" with no codecs is valid.** The server accepts `codecs: []` without a warning (a new Video is node starts that way), so a workflow can be enabled with a condition that matches nothing or everything. Engine/catalogue track.
- **Saving a behaviour change keeps an enabled workflow enabled** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: saves never touch the active version.)* (only pin changes and dropped overrides switch it to dry run, per the M1 rules). Adding `hevc` to Video is on an enabled workflow saved as enabled with no prompt. Matches the M1 contract; worth a product decision.
- **Group boxes do not follow their members.** Moving a member leaves the box where it was (it can end up covering non-members); the resize handles did not respond to a drag in the walkthrough. Ungroup + group again gives a tight box.
- **Fit view right after a drag** once fitted the pre-drag bounds (a node ended up outside the canvas); a second click fitted correctly. Not reproduced reliably.
- **The minimap is oversized on a narrow canvas** (the < 1024 px read-only view) and covers cards; fit view and new-node placement still ignore it (already on the final-wave list).
- **Issue messages repeat the node title when it is not a prefix** ("Run sub-workflow / Run sub-workflow uses version 2; the latest is 3"): only a leading "<title>: " is dropped.
- **Versions rows say the author twice** ("v4 · saved by you · just now · devadmin") when a version has no note.
- **No Group action in the multi-select inspector** ("2 nodes selected" offers only Delete); grouping is on the canvas toolbar only (already on the final-wave list).
- **Dev proxy:** Vite's proxy forwards to `TRANSCODA_API_URL` with the target's host, so a UI opened on a host name other than the API's fails setup/login with CSRF_FAILED unless that name is in `TRANSCODA_ALLOWED_HOSTS`. Dev-only; the documented localhost:5173 → localhost:8080 setup is unaffected.
## M1 engine Task 1: deferred cleanups

Closes N2, N3, N5 and M4 from the contract-track reviews, and the spec's `verify` removal.

- **N2 — validation cache shape:** `ValidationCache` is `{ results, active, nested, resolved, clean }`. `resolved` (keyed `id@version`) memoises `ctx.resolveSubworkflow`, so each pinned version is looked up once per cache across the reference checks, the cycle search and child validation. `clean` is the `reachesCycle` memo, previously per call; the ids a ref reaches do not depend on `self` or the stack, so it is shared across validations (as `list()` shares one cache).
- **N3 — transitive stale parents (ruling):** supersedes "Only direct parents are flagged" (M1 final review, I1). After the upgrade pass, every sub-workflow that pins (transitively, computed to a fixpoint, so pin cycles and diamonds terminate and reasons from every depth are collected) a behaviour-upgraded child at an older version or an unupgradable child needs a review, and flags every live parent pinning **any** version of it — including a sub-workflow that was itself upgraded in this pass, whose fresh version still pins the stale child. Such a parent's note is `Sub-workflow <M> needs a review because <reasons joined with ' and '>`, the reasons carrying the originating text forward (`sub-workflow C was upgraded with a behaviour change (version N)` / `sub-workflow C could not be upgraded (<detail>)`). The middle sub-workflow gets no new version. Each workflow is written once per pass, with all its new notes.
- **N5 — unupgradable children (ruling):** a sub-workflow counted broken by `upgradeAll` flags every live parent pinning any version of it: note `Sub-workflow <name> could not be upgraded (<detail>); check the workflow before enabling it`, detail one of `corrupt graph`, `no migration path`, `invalid migration output`, `migration failed`.
- **Reviews stick (controller ruling):** a broken child stays broken on every start, so a parent is flagged for a reason only when that reason is new. The settings row `workflow_review_acks` (no migration) maps each workflow id to the pins it had (`id@version` list) and the reason keys flagged on it in the last pass; a reason already there with the same pins is not flagged again, so reviewing and enabling the parent sticks across restarts. The map is replaced every pass: a reason that went away and comes back, a different break of the child (another detail), or a change of the parent's pins flags it again. A failed flag write keeps the old entry so it is retried.
- **M4 — `listNodes` cache:** node metadata (including the settings JSON Schema) is built once per `WorkflowService` (the registry is fixed for its lifetime); availability is recomputed on every call from the current capabilities. The cached metadata is a deep-frozen copy (callers share it; the definition's own arrays are not frozen).
- **`core:replace-original` drops `verify`:** verification before a replace is never optional. No stored workflows used the setting (an unknown key is stripped on parse), so the node stays at version 1 with no migration. The `recycleFolder` description is now `Recycle folder for the original; unset means the library default`.

## M1 engine Task 2: shared engine vocabulary

- **`normalizeProfile` rule (ruling):** the brief's "spaces removed" contradicts its own example `Constrained Baseline` → `constrained-baseline`. The rule that satisfies all three examples: lowercase, remove `:` and `.`, remove spaces next to a digit, turn any other space (between two words) into `-`. So `Main 10` → `main10`, `High 4:4:4 Predictive` → `high444predictive`, `Main Still Picture` → `main-still-picture`, VP9's `Profile 0` → `profile0`.
- **Language table:** `LANGUAGES` holds the 183 current ISO 639-1 languages plus the withdrawn `bh` (Bihari, `bih`) and `sh` (Serbo-Croatian, canonical `hbs` from 639-3, as it has no 639-2 code), 185 rows. `b` is set only for the 20 languages whose 639-2/B code differs. Legacy 639-1 aliases (`iw`, `in`, `ji`, `mo`) are not mapped; they pass through unchanged like any unknown code.
- **Device profiles, verified against the sources (2026-10-16):**
  - `constrained-baseline` is listed with `baseline` for h264 on both devices (a subset of Baseline; ffprobe reports it for many files).
  - android-tv: AAC lowered to 6 channels (Android: "mono/stereo/5.0/5.1"); FLAC lowered to 2 channels (Android: "Mono/Stereo (no multichannel)"). HLG kept from Android's HDR table (HEVC 10-bit). PGS and mov_text confirmed by media3's `pgs` and `tx3g` parsers. AC-3/E-AC-3 from the Shield spec (Dolby Digital / Digital Plus). h264 level 51 and hevc level 153 are generous ceilings, not limits any source states (Android's compatibility definition only states a minimum, HEVC Main Level 4.1); a file above them is treated as unplayable there.
  - chrome: no hevc (Chromium supports it only with hardware decoding, so it is not assumed). VP9 is `profile0` and `profile2` (8 and 10 bit), level 62 (the highest VP9 level, i.e. no limit; MDN lists no level constraints), HDR `hdr10` and `hlg` (MDN: "HDR10+, HLG, and PQ"). Audio channel counts are not given by the sources; the plan's values are kept.
  - **chrome subtitles (ruling):** `<video>` renders only in-band WebVTT, but Jellyfin's web player extracts text subtitles server-side, so `subrip`, `ass`, `ssa` and `mov_text` count as playable alongside `webvtt`. Image subtitles (PGS) do not.

## M1 engine Task 4: engine config, probe and fingerprint

- **Config:** `ffprobePath` (`TRANSCODA_FFPROBE_PATH`, default `ffprobe` beside `ffmpegPath` when that contains a `/`, else bare `ffprobe`), `outputRoots` (`TRANSCODA_OUTPUT_ROOTS`, comma list of absolute paths, a relative entry is a ConfigError), `puid`/`pgid` (`PUID`/`PGID`, empty or unset → null; anything but a non-negative integer is a ConfigError rather than being ignored).
- **ffprobe exec:** `probeFile` uses its own default exec with a 60 s timeout and 32 MB output buffer (the capability probe's 10 s / 4 MB is too tight for a file on a sleeping disk or network share, or one with hundreds of chapters).
- **Lenient stream types:** a `codec_type` other than video/audio/subtitle/attachment/data (ffprobe prints `unknown` for unnamed media types), or a missing one, maps to `data` instead of rejecting the whole file; a missing `codec_name` becomes `unknown`. Shape errors (no `format`, no `format_name`, `streams` not an array, a stream that is not an object or has no integer `index`, stdout that is not JSON) throw `unrecognised ffprobe output`.
- **Probe details the plan leaves open:** `title` from `tags.title`; `width`/`height`/`channels`/`sampleRate` only when positive integers; `hdr` is null for non-video streams and `none` for video without HDR signalling; `bitDepth` from `bits_per_raw_sample` for any stream (FLAC reports 24), else for video from `pix_fmt` (`…p10le`/`p010le` → 10, common 8-bit formats → 8, unknown → null); `durationSeconds`/`sizeBytes` fall back to 0 when the format omits them; `chapters` is the chapter count.
- **Fixture count:** the captured tigger-pooh output has 43 streams (indexes 0–42: HEVC, 20 AAC, 21 mov_text, PNG cover), matching the fixture manifest; the plan's "42" was an off-by-one.

## M1 engine Task 5: migration 0003 and engine repositories

- **Frozen state list:** `0003_engine` spells out the 15 job states literally instead of importing `JOB_STATES`, so the migration's SQL never changes after it shipped; a new state needs a new migration.
- **Fingerprint columns:** `files.fingerprint` and `job_results.fingerprint` hold `fingerprintKey` (`dev:ino:size:mtime`); `nlink` is not stored. `FileRow` exposes the identity columns plus that key. `upsertFile` keys on `path` and keeps the row id.
- **Final-at-insert jobs:** `insertJob` sets `finished_at` when the job is inserted in a final state (`skipped`, `already_processed`).
- **Library lookups:** `findLibrary` returns soft-deleted libraries (jobs keep pointing at them); `listLibraries`, `libraryForPath` and `updateLibrary` see live ones only (`updateLibrary` on a deleted library returns null). `insertLibrary` stores the path without a trailing slash; `libraryForPath` ignores one on either side.
- **Live-path uniqueness (review ruling):** `libraries.path` is unique only among live libraries (partial unique index `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`), so a deleted library's path can be added again.
- **Constraints (review ruling):** `job_results.outcome` is CHECKed against the 15 states; every boolean column (`process_hardlinked`, `dry_run`, `force`, `paused`) is CHECKed to 0/1; a job in a final state must have `finished_at` (it is the list cursor key). `updateJob` stamps `finished_at = now` when a job moves from a non-final into a final state, unless the patch sets it; updating an already-final job keeps its time. A test inserts one job and one result per `JOB_STATES` value to catch drift from the frozen list.
- **Indexes (review ruling):** `jobs(library_id)`, `jobs(finished_at)`, `files(library_id)`, `job_results(workflow_id, outcome, created_at)` (ratios), `job_results(created_at)` (stats).
- **`listJobs` paging (review ruling, supersedes the brief's `updated_at` cursor):** two queries, active jobs (`priority DESC, queued_at, rowid`) then final jobs (`finished_at DESC, rowid DESC`, walked on `jobs_finished_at`), default page size 200 (`JOB_LIST_LIMIT`). `before` is the id of the last job of the previous page; the next page continues after it by keyset (`(-priority, queued_at, rowid)` for an active cursor, `(finished_at, rowid)` for a final one), so equal timestamps never skip or repeat rows. The cursor job's current state decides which segment it is in; an unknown id gives an empty page. `states: []` gives an empty page.
- **`jobStats`:** `files` counts `done` results since `since`, `savedBytes` sums their `before − after`, `failed` counts `failed` results; `queued` is jobs in `queued` (paused ones included), `running` is jobs in `RUNNING_JOB_STATES`.
- **Orderings:** `listRecycled` is oldest first (retention order); `recentRatios` is newest first across all versions of the workflow.

## M1 engine Task 6: build model and ffmpeg command rendering

- **Identity model:** `modelFromProbe` keeps every stream in copy mode with its language, title, flags and dispositions; encoder, quality, preset, bitrate, channels, profile and pixel format start null (they describe a conversion, not the source). Containers other than mkv/mp4 map to mkv, so such inputs are never identity. `isIdentity` also fails when a kept stream points at a probe index that does not exist, and when a probe stream of any type (data included) is dropped.
- **Diff wording (ruling):** streams are named `<type> #<probe index>` (the index ffprobe prints and `-map 0:N` uses). Lines: `container: ts → mkv` first, then `drop <type> #N (<lang>)` in source order, then one line per changed kept non-data stream in output order, fragments joined by `, ` in this order: `convert <from>→<to> (<encoder> crf|qp|q <n> <preset> <profile> <n>k <n>ch <pix_fmt>)`, `moved to output #N` (when its position among kept non-data streams differs from its rank in source order; N is the real output index), `language a→b` (null shown as `und`), `title "x"` / `title cleared`, `default` / `not default` (same for `forced`, `attached_pic`), `+flag` / `-flag` for other dispositions. Data streams appear only as `drop data #N`; their mode and position are never reported.
- **Encoder resolution:** `h264_nvenc` needs the `cuda` hwaccel like `hevc_nvenc`; QSV and VAAPI encoders need only to be listed.
- **Rendering details:** codec options (`-pix_fmt`, bitrate, channels, encoder quality flags) are rendered only for streams in convert mode; a pixel format on a copy stream is ignored. A subtitle conversion uses `encoder` when set, else `subrip` → `srt` and any other codec as its own encoder. `ENCODER_ARGS` holds libx265/libx264/libsvtav1 (`-crf`, `-preset`), libaom-av1 (`-crf`, `-b 0`, `-cpu-used`), NVENC (`-rc constqp -qp`, `-preset`), QSV (`-global_quality`, `-preset`) and VAAPI (`-rc_mode CQP -qp`, no preset); device setup for VAAPI/QSV belongs in the model's `globalArgs`/`inputArgs`. `profile` is rendered per encoder (see the fix-round entry below). `renderCommandLine` leaves `[A-Za-z0-9_-+=:,./@%]` arguments bare and single-quotes the rest.
- **Verified by hand against Homebrew ffmpeg 9.0.2:** the rendered identity remux of compliant.mkv, tigger-pooh.mp4 and two-defaults.mkv reprobes with identical stream codecs, languages, titles, flags and dispositions, and a libx265 + aac downmix render runs on all three.

### Task 6 fix round (review rulings)

- **Language always written:** a null language renders `-metadata:s:<i> language=und` for mkv and mp4 alike (ffmpeg copies the source stream's tags, so writing nothing kept a cleared tag). Checked by a fixture reprobe.
- **Data streams:** always rendered as `-c:<i> copy`, whatever the model's mode; only an explicit `keep: false` drops one. The drop is listed in `diffFromProbe` (`drop data #N`) and makes the model non-identity (second fix round ruling), so the file is rewritten without it; a data stream's mode and position stay ignored by identity. "Moved" is decided among kept non-data streams only and names the actual output index.
- **Profiles (ruling):** `StreamPlan.profile` (normalised, e.g. `main10`) is rendered per encoder: libx264 and NVENC `-profile:<i>`, libx265 `-x265-params:<i> profile=<p>` (x265 silently encodes Main when asked for main10 on 8-bit input, libx264 refuses a mismatch). Supported: libx264 baseline/constrained-baseline (as `baseline`)/main/high/high10; libx265 main/main10/main12; hevc_nvenc main/main10; h264_nvenc baseline/constrained-baseline/main/high. A profile on any other encoder or a non-video stream, a profile without a pixel format, or a pixel format that is not 4:2:0 at exactly the profile's bit depth throws `RenderError` before ffmpeg starts. The profile shows in the diff's conversion text.
- **`RenderError`** (exported from render.ts): thrown by `renderArgs` for a relative input/output path, a NUL byte in any argument, malformed `globalArgs`/`inputArgs`, or a profile mismatch; the pipeline turns it into a failed job with reason `render: <message>`. `globalArgs`/`inputArgs` may only hold options (`-name`) each followed by at most one value; options in the `BOOLEAN_FLAGS` allowlist (`-y`, `-n`, `-re`, `-nostdin`, `-hide_banner`, `-stats`, `-nostats`, `-copyts`, `-vn`/`-an`/`-sn`/`-dn`, …) take no value, so a token after one is rejected; any other stray token, and `-i`, is rejected.

## M1 engine Task 7: targets

- **Option A (Jan):** targets mean one output file that plays directly on every selected target. (What must play was narrowed by ruling R1, Task 9 fix round: container, video, default audio and default subtitle.) A setting a target cannot play is a validation error (static checks `checkEncodeVideoSettings` / `checkEncodeAudioSettings` / `checkRemuxSettings`, surfaced as `TARGET_UNSUPPORTED` in Task 8) and a build-time `NodeHookError` (Task 9); the engine never silently switches codec.
- **Where `targets` lives:** a `targets` setting (default `[]`, so stored graphs parse unchanged; all nodes stay `version: 1`) on `core:encode-video`, `core:encode-audio` and `core:remux` only. Empty targets never report anything.
- **What is checked (`engine/targets.ts`):** container; video codec, profile (in `normalizeProfile` form), level, bit depth and HDR; audio codec and channel count; subtitle format. Cover art (`attachedPic`), attachments and data streams are exempt. Unknown values (null) are not checked. Messages read `<profile name> cannot play <what>`, naming streams `<type> #<probe index>` like the build-model diff (`… cannot play audio #2: 8 channels (max 6 for aac)`); static setting checks name only the codec or container (`Chrome (desktop) cannot play hevc`).
- **Post-conversion values (ruling):** a copied stream is checked with its probed values. A converted stream uses its planned codec and profile; its bit depth comes from the planned pixel format read the same way `renderArgs` reads it (`yuv420Depth`, now exported from render.ts), else the source's; a pixel format that is not 4:2:0 at a known depth is a `bit-depth` issue (no listed profile plays it); the level is the encoder's choice and not checked; HDR is taken from the source (conservative); channels are the planned count, else the source's.
- **Subtitles:** image→text conversion (PGS/VobSub → text) needs OCR, which ffmpeg does not have, so the design's "convert image subtitles to text where possible" means never in M1. Image subtitles are dropped or kept per `core:remux` `subtitleConversion` and the outcome is reported in the trace. The `'drop-incompatible'` meaning is written into the field description (`Subtitle conversion ('drop-incompatible': Drop subtitles the container or targets cannot carry)`), since JSON Schema enums carry no per-option text.
- **`whenUnsupported` (`core:encode-audio`, default `add-track`):** when a target cannot play a kept audio track, `add-track` keeps it and appends a converted copy (to the node's `codec`) of the first offending track right after it, not default; `convert` converts only the offending tracks; `fail` fails the job.
- **`skipIf: 'plays-on-all-targets'`** on `core:encode-video`: copy the video when `streamPlaysOn` reports nothing for every target.
- **Encoder `'auto'` order** (encoder description `Encoder (auto picks hardware first)`, default still `libx265`): hevc `hevc_nvenc` (needs the `cuda` hwaccel), `hevc_qsv`, `hevc_vaapi`, `libx265`; h264 `h264_nvenc` (cuda), `h264_qsv`, `h264_vaapi`, `libx264`; av1 `libsvtav1`, `libaom-av1`.

## M1 engine Task 8: validation

- **`FINISH_ORDER` (error, on the later node):** a `core:size-gate` reached after a `core:replace-original` / `core:move-copy` on the same path (message `<node> comes after a node that writes the result; put it before`), or a non-finish node reached after any finish node (`<node> comes after a finish node; only finish nodes may follow one`). Finish after finish (e.g. notify after replace, a second gate) is allowed. Reported once per node and rule.
- **`NO_WRITE_PATH` (warning):** a path from the trigger that ends without passing a replace/move-copy. `nodeId` is the last node; message `this path never writes the result` for a node with no connected file output, `this path never writes the result (output "<port>" is unconnected)` for each unconnected file output of a node whose other output is connected (port-aware, e.g. a condition with only `else` connected warns on `pass`). Workflows without any finish node still get it alongside `NO_FINISH_PATH`.
- **The walk:** per-port successor map (`<nodeId>:<port>` → targets, existing edges only), iterative DFS from the single trigger carrying "a write node was passed" and "a finish node was passed", memoised on `(node, flags)` (each node expanded at most four times; stacked diamonds stay linear). Only workflows with exactly one trigger are walked; sub-workflows are not (no finish nodes there).
- **Bypass (ruling):** bypassed nodes run no hook, and the engine passes the file through on their first file output (plan Task 10). So in the walk a bypassed node neither sets the flags nor is flagged for order, and only its first file output is followed. A bypassed replace/move-copy therefore does not count as a write (`NO_WRITE_PATH` on it when it ends the path).
- **`TARGET_UNSUPPORTED` (error, `settingPath: 'targets'`):** the Task 7 static checks on valid current settings of `core:encode-video` (codec), `core:encode-audio` (codec — checked in copy mode too, since `whenUnsupported` converts to it) and `core:remux` (container); message `<node>: <target issue message>`, one issue per target.
- **`ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` (error, `settingPath: 'encoder'`):** `core:encode-video` whose `encoder` is not in the capabilities (`<node>: encoder <name> is not available`), or `'auto'` with no usable encoder for the codec (`<node>: no <codec> encoder is available`; same resolution as the build, `resolveEncoder`, incl. nvenc needing `cuda`). Skipped entirely while `capabilities.available === false`.
- **Output roots:** `ValidationContext.outputRoots` and `nodeAvailability(def, caps, { outputRoots })`. `core:move-copy` is unavailable (`NODE_UNAVAILABLE`, reason `no output roots configured (TRANSCODA_OUTPUT_ROOTS)`) with no roots; with roots, an `outputRoot` not exactly equal to one of them is `INVALID_SETTINGS` `settingPath: 'outputRoot'` (`outputRoot must be one of the configured output roots`). Comparison is exact string equality (normalisation is Task 15's concern). `WorkflowService` takes an optional `config: Pick<Config, 'outputRoots'>` (default no roots); `createAppDeps` passes the server config.

## M1 engine Task 9: core node hooks

- **Shared rules (`nodes/core/stream-rules.ts`):** `matchesLanguage` normalises both sides (`en`/`eng`, `de`/`ger`/`deu`); an untagged stream (null, `und`, `zxx`) matches no code. `pickDefault(streams, order)` takes the first stream of the first code any stream matches. `sortByLanguage` is stable: listed languages in list order, unlisted after, untagged last. `NodeHookError` is the hook failure the walker turns into the job's reason. Hooks never mutate `ctx.model`; they work on a copy.
- **`model.targets`:** `core:encode-video`, `core:encode-audio` and `core:remux` add their `targets` to `model.targets` (union, first-seen order), so verify checks the output against every target any node named.
- **Original language:** `ctx.data.language` counts only when it is a string other than `'unknown'`; a missing input is treated like `'unknown'`. `core:keep-languages` reports `findings.originalLanguage` (normalised code or `'unknown'`) whenever `keepOriginal` is on.
- **Reasons:** languages are shown as tagged (`und` for untagged), empty lists as `none`; streams are `#<probe index>`. `core:has-stream` lists the distinct languages of the matching streams after `has`. `core:video-is` names the first failing criterion (`video is hevc 2160p, wanted at most 1080p → else`); with several `codecs` they read `hevc or av1`. The video bitrate is the stream's, else the container's (mkv streams rarely carry one).
- **`core:file-matches`:** MB means MiB (1024 × 1024 bytes); age is measured from the file's mtime to now (decide takes the wall clock; there is no clock in the hook context); `olderThanDays` is strict (`>`); the glob uses `path.matchesGlob` on the full path. Findings are rounded to one decimal.
- **`core:set-default-track`:** works on kept streams only; subtitles get no original-language preference (the spec rule names audio). Forced flags are never touched.
- **`core:reorder-tracks`:** the audio slots and subtitle slots of the current output order are refilled in sorted order; every other stream (video, cover, attachments, data) keeps its slot, so a video that was first stays first.
- **`core:cover-attachments`:** fonts are attachments with codec `ttf` or `otf`; they survive `keepAttachments: false` while a kept subtitle (as planned at that point) is `ass`/`ssa`.
- **`core:encode-video`:** the encoder is resolved first, so a missing encoder fails the job even when the stream would be copied (validation already blocks such a workflow); `'auto'` with nothing usable reads `no <codec> encoder is available`, as in validation. Every kept non-cover video stream is handled. Skip rules look at the probed source stream; a skip resets the stream to a plain copy. A conversion sets no profile (the encoder's default), so profile/pixel-format mismatches cannot arise here. The target check runs after both copy and convert, so `already-target-codec` with hevc and targets `[chrome]` fails rather than copying a stream Chrome cannot play.
