- **`core:encode-audio`:** encoders `aac` → `aac`, `ac3` → `ac3`, `opus` → `libopus`; a conversion whose encoder ffmpeg lacks fails with `encoder <x> is not available`. `downmixTo: 'stereo'` sets 2 channels only on tracks with more than two (or an unknown count); mono and stereo tracks keep theirs. `whenUnsupported: 'convert'` converts each offending track to `codec`, capping channels at the lowest `maxChannels` the targets allow for that codec (8-channel eac3 → 6-channel aac for Chrome). `'add-track'` adds a **stereo** copy (2 channels, `bitrateKbps`, title `<lang> (<codec>)`, not default, not forced, no other dispositions) of the first offending track right after it, and leaves the offending original as it is. Converted or added tracks are re-checked; any remaining issue fails the job (`target unsupported: …`). `'fail'` lists every offending track. `findings.unsupported` lists the offending source indices when targets are set.
- **`core:remux` (muxer facts checked against Homebrew ffmpeg 9.0.2):** mp4 refuses `subrip` and `ttf` streams; matroska refuses `mov_text` and any data stream (`Only audio, video, and subtitles are supported`). So text subtitles are converted under every `subtitleConversion` (mp4: `subrip`/`ass`/`ssa`/`webvtt`/`text` → `mov_text`; mkv: `mov_text` → `subrip`), and `'keep'` and `'convert'` behave the same in M1; they differ from `'drop-incompatible'` only for subtitles that cannot be converted (image subtitles into mp4, or a subtitle a target cannot play), which fail the job instead of being dropped. To mp4, attachments are dropped (reported); to mkv, data streams are dropped (reported, so the model is not identity). The container is checked against the targets before anything else. The `'drop-incompatible'` description (`Drop subtitles the container or targets cannot carry`) matches this behaviour.
- **Finish reasons:** `notify-webhook` never puts the URL in its reason (`notify webhook (POST)`); a webhook URL may carry a token.

### Task 9 fix round (controller rulings R1–R4, review minors)

- **R1, what "plays on all targets" means (supersedes the Task 7 "every kept stream" reading and the `whenUnsupported` lines above):** the container, every non-cover video stream, the default audio track and the default subtitle track must play directly on every target. The default audio is the first kept audio flagged default, else the first kept audio (what players pick); the default subtitle is the first kept subtitle flagged default, else none. `checkModelAgainstTargets` returns only those blocking issues; `targetWarnings` lists each other kept audio/subtitle track a target cannot play as `<type> #<index> won't direct-play on <profile name>` (once per track and target); `planIssues` is the per-stream check, whatever the stream's role; `defaultTracks` names the two tracks. Hooks put warnings in `findings.warnings` and append `; warning: <message>` to the reason. `core:encode-audio`: `add-track` adds a stereo copy of the **default** audio when it is unplayable, makes the copy the default and clears the original's flag; `fail` fails only on the default audio; `convert` still converts every unplayable kept track. `core:remux`: the default subtitle must play (else `target unsupported`), `drop-incompatible` drops every subtitle a target cannot play.
- **R2, covers into mkv:** an mkv output carries each kept `attachedPic` stream as an attachment (`-attach <file>`, `mimetype` by image codec — png `image/png`, mjpeg `image/jpeg`, bmp/gif/webp likewise, else `image/<codec>` — and `filename=cover.<ext>`, later covers `cover-<index>.<ext>`), because ffmpeg 9.0.2 writes a mapped attached_pic stream into matroska as a plain one-frame video track. ffmpeg attaches only files, so the pipeline first extracts each cover with `coverExtractArgs(input, index, file)` (stream copy to `image2`) into the job temp dir and passes `coverFiles` to `renderArgs`; a missing file is a `RenderError`. Attachments come after every mapped stream: `outputOrder(model)` is the output's stream order (kept order, mkv covers last) for verify; the cover reprobes as a `png` video stream with `attached_pic` (checked on tigger-pooh.mp4 → mkv). The diff says `video #N: written as attachment cover.png` when a non-mkv source's cover goes into mkv, and a cover moving last in mkv is not a move. An mkv source with a cover attachment is unaffected (identity, no diff line).
- **R3, streams a container cannot hold:** `containerCanHold` (matroska: no data streams, no `mov_text`; mp4: no attachments, no subtitle other than `mov_text`) and `fitToContainer(model)` (text subtitles convert to `mov_text`/`subrip`, everything else the container cannot hold is dropped). `modelFromProbe` fits inputs that are neither mkv nor mp4 (ts/avi/mov → mkv), `core:remux` fits after changing the container (reason `dropped (container can't hold it): #4 hdmv_pgs_subtitle, #6 ttf`), and the diff marks such drops `drop data #2 (container can't hold it)`. `renderArgs` refuses a kept stream the container cannot hold (`RenderError`) rather than letting the muxer fail. One exception stays: `core:remux` with `subtitleConversion` `keep`/`convert` asked to carry image subtitles into mp4 fails with `mp4 cannot carry these subtitles: #4 hdmv_pgs_subtitle, … (no OCR; use drop-incompatible)`.
- **R4:** when `core:keep-languages` drops the track flagged default (audio or subtitle) and no kept track of that type is flagged, the first kept track of the type becomes default; the reason ends `; default audio now eng (#2)` / `; default subtitle now deu (#5)`.
- **Minors:** the `subtitleConversion` description now says that `keep` and `convert` convert text subtitles to the container's text format and fail on image subtitles the container or targets cannot carry. `core:has-stream` reasons show the other criteria (`→ has eng (forced)`, `→ none matching language en, codec dts, not default`). `core:video-is` says `bitrate unknown` when neither the stream nor the container has one. `DecideContext` gains `now` (epoch ms, the walk's clock): `core:file-matches` ages files against it, compares the rounded size and age it reports, and writes `1 day`/`n days`. `core:encode-video` reads the planned codec when an earlier node already converts the stream (a skip leaves that conversion unchanged, reason `video already converted to hevc → unchanged`); `plays-on-all-targets` with no targets encodes, reason suffix `; plays-on-all-targets has no targets to check`. Tests check that no build hook mutates `ctx.model`.

## M1 engine Task 10: loading and walk

- **Loading a job's version (`WorkflowService.loadForRun`):** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: loadForRun returns the active version.)* re-reads the workflow row and the pinned version. Missing when the workflow is deleted, is not of kind `workflow`, or the version does not exist; a corrupt version is invalid (`CORRUPT_GRAPH`). Safe migrations are applied to the job's own pinned version too (the same `migratePinned` rule as pinned children, now exported from `validate.ts` with the held node's id, title and stored version), then the graph is validated with its children by `validateWorkflow`. A node whose migration path has a behaviour step (or whose migration throws) makes the version invalid for running: one `NODE_OUTDATED` error on that node (`<node> needs a review: its upgrade from version <v> changes behaviour; run the job on a version upgraded on this server`), never a silent run of the old settings or of the migrated behaviour. A pinned child in that state is `SUBWORKFLOW_BROKEN` on the Run node, as before. `loadForRun` returns `{ kind: 'ok', graph, mode, needsReview }`; `loadWorkflowForJob` adds the job-dependent fields.
- **`resolvePinnedChild(id, version, overrides)`:** the child the walk enters: all-safe migrations, then the Run node's overrides (`applyOverrides`, which ignores unknown keys); null when missing. Held nodes are not re-checked here: `loadForRun` already refused such a version, and versions are immutable.
- **Dry run:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: dry run follows the active version.)* `effectiveDryRun = job.dryRun || mode !== 'enabled' || needsReview`. `note` explains a dry run the job did not ask for: `workflow is in dry-run mode` (checked first), else `workflow needs review`; null when the job asked for one or runs for real.
- **Walk shape:** `walkDecide` walks the file from the single trigger, following the one edge leaving the chosen file output (conditions: the port `decide` returns, which must be one of its file outputs; every other node: its first file output). An unconnected output ends the walk normally (the pipeline then sees no write action); an output with more than one edge fails the walk (`output <port> has <n> connections; a file follows one`), as does a revisited node *(superseded 2026-10-19, finding C1: several finish nodes may share an output; see "2026-10-19 Several finish steps on one output")*. A sub-workflow path that ends before its End node ends the whole walk the same way. `walkBuild` starts from `modelFromProbe(probe)` and runs `build` on actions and `finish` on finish nodes in path order; finish actions are concatenated in path order. `DecidedPath.nodes` lists every node passed, including the trigger, Run nodes and the sub-workflow's Start/End.
- **Trace:** one entry per hook call (`decide` with its port; `build`/`finish` with port null; `data` with `findings = { [outputPortId]: value }`), plus `no-op` entries for a condition/action/finish/data node without its hook and `bypassed` entries. Trigger, Start/End and (non-bypassed) Run nodes have no hook and no entry. A throwing hook (NodeHookError or anything else, incl. invalid settings) is traced with reason `failed: <message>` and fails the walk with the node id, its `workflowPath` and the message. Finish entries carry empty findings (actions may hold a notify URL). Timestamps come from `deps.now()`; `attempt` from the input.
- **Data:** a data node is evaluated once per job (memo keyed `${workflowPath.join('/')}/${nodeId}`) when the walk first reaches a node it feeds (during decide, also for actions and finish nodes, so data entries precede the build entries; the build reads the memo). Every data input of a node starts as `'unknown'`; a connected one gets the source's value for that output port. A data node without a `data` hook (reason `no-op`), bypassed (reason `bypassed`) or whose hook or settings throw (reason `source unavailable: <message>`) yields `'unknown'` on all its data outputs; data never fails the walk. Data nodes feeding data nodes are resolved recursively.
- **Bypass:** a bypassed condition, action or finish node runs no hook and passes the file on its first file output (the rule validation's path check uses, Task 8). It is traced `bypassed` with `port` = that output, in the phase of its kind (decide/build/finish). A bypassed Run node is not entered. Bypass on trigger/Start/End is ignored.
- **Run node path encoding:** entering a Run node appends its node id to `workflowPath` for every node and trace entry inside the child (nested: `[outerRunId, innerRunId]`); the walk then continues at the Run node's file output. A missing child fails at the Run node (`sub-workflow missing`); a child id already being walked fails with `sub-workflow calls itself` (validation already forbids both).

## M1 engine Task 11: ffmpeg process control and progress

- **Spawning:** `nodeSpawner` runs ffmpeg with an argv array, `shell: false`, `detached: true` (its own process group) and stdio `ignore`/`pipe`/`pipe`. `pause`/`resume`/`kill` send `SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT`/`SIGKILL` to `-pid` and never throw: they are no-ops before a pid exists and after the process ended (a process group id can be reused), `ESRCH` is ignored and any other failure (e.g. `EPERM`) goes to the optional `opts.log` as a warning.
- **Exit-time kill and pid file (fix round):** every running group is in a module-level registry (`liveProcessGroups()`); a synchronous `process.on('exit')` handler, installed on the first spawn, SIGKILLs them all (`killLiveProcessGroups`). `opts.pidFile` (the pipeline passes `<job temp dir>/ffmpeg.pid`) gets the pid when ffmpeg starts and is removed when it ends, so the startup sweep can SIGKILL a group left over from a crash before it removes the temp dir (Task 13).
- **`done`:** resolves on `close` (once stdout/stderr are drained) after the job log is flushed, so the log and the last progress sample are complete; when something keeps the pipes open after the exit, it resolves `exitGraceMs` (default 2 s) after `exit` anyway, SIGKILLs the rest of the group and ends the log. It rejects when the file cannot be spawned (e.g. `ENOENT`). stderr is appended to the log (`flags: 'a'`); if the log cannot be written, stderr is still drained (ffmpeg never blocks) and the result carries `logError`.
- **Progress callback:** a throwing `onProgress` is caught and logged (`progress callback failed`), never an uncaught exception; later samples still arrive. The block accumulator keeps at most 100 lines.
- **Progress parsing:** a block ends at `progress=continue|end`; the out time comes from `out_time_us`, else `out_time_ms` (also microseconds in ffmpeg), else `out_time`; a negative start time clamps to 0; a block without an out time (`N/A` before the first frame) yields no sample. `percent` is null without a positive duration; `etaSeconds` is rounded to one decimal, never negative, null without a positive speed or duration.
- **`FakeSpawner`:** records `calls` (`file`, `args`, `logPath`) and `signals`; `emitProgress`/`exit` drive the latest process; `kill()` ends it with `{ code: null, signal: 'SIGKILL' }` as a real kill does, and signals after the end are no-ops and not recorded.

### Task 10 fix round (live job graph)

- **Every path node is traced (supersedes "Trigger, Start/End and Run nodes have no entry" above):** new trace phase `pass` (`TRACE_PHASES` in packages/shared) for nodes without a hook: the trigger (`start: <label or title>`), a non-bypassed Run node (`enters sub-workflow <name> v<version>`, before the child's entries), a sub-workflow's Start (`start`) and End (`returns`). So every `PathStep` has exactly one non-data entry for its node (plus a `failed:` entry where the walk failed). `resolvePinnedChild` / `WalkDeps.resolveChild` now return `{ name, graph }` for the Run entry.
- **Ports:** every path entry carries the file output the file left by: decide its chosen port, build/finish/bypassed/pass entries the node's first file output; an End node's port is the Run node's output it hands the file back on. A node where the walk stopped on an unconnected output still shows that output; a node that failed before choosing one has `port: null` in the path.
- **`seq`:** `TraceEntry.seq` (int ≥ 1) numbers entries in emission order, starting at `WalkInput.firstSeq` (default 1; the pipeline passes the next job-wide number so seq stays monotonic across attempts) and continuing from decide into build (`DecidedPath.nextSeq`). `appendTrace` still assigns its own row seq; the pipeline should keep the two equal.
- **Path on results:** `WalkResult` (`built`) and `WalkFailure` carry `path: { workflowPath, nodeId, port }[]` in walk order; on failure it ends with the failing node. Failures raised outside a hook (unknown node type, missing node, cycle/revisited node, fan-out, invalid Run settings, self-call, missing sub-workflow or Start) append a `failed: <reason>` entry for that node (phase `pass`, or `decide` on a condition). A decide hook returning a port the node lacks is a hook failure (`chose unknown port <p>`).
- **Current node:** `WalkDeps.onNodeStart({ workflowPath, nodeId })` fires as the decide walk reaches each path node and again as the build reaches each action/finish node (bypassed included); the pipeline uses it for `currentNode`.
- **Run nodes have no data inputs** (file input only), so the walk resolves no data for them; a test pins this.
- **Wording:** a behaviour-pending node in the job's own version reads `<node> needs a review: its upgrade from version <v> changes behaviour; save an upgraded version of the workflow and queue the file again`; in a pinned child `<node> needs a review; save an upgraded version of the workflow and queue the file again` (replaces "re-pin …").

## M1 engine Task 12: verify

- **Defaults ruling:** the output must have exactly one default audio track when it has any audio, and at most one default subtitle. A file without audio passes this check (nothing to flag).
- **Checks (`verifyOutput`), all run and failures accumulate:** `probe` (an output ffprobe cannot read fails this check only and stops); `duration`; `stream-count`; `stream-match`; `defaults`; `decode-start`; `decode-end`; `targets`.
- **Duration is measured, not only read from the header:** a truncated mkv keeps its header duration, so besides `|format duration − source| ≤ 1 s` verify reads the packets of the last 2 s before the reported end (`ffprobe -read_intervals <end−2>%`) and requires the last audio/video packet's end to be within 1 s of the source duration too. No packets there is a `duration` failure.
- **Decode checks are strict:** `ffmpeg -nostdin -v error -xerror (-t 1 | -sseof -1) -i out -copyts -f null -` must exit 0 **and print nothing**: ffmpeg exits 0 on a truncated file and only reports `File ended prematurely`. `-copyts` keeps the null muxer from reporting non-monotonic timestamps after the end seek, so a healthy output is silent. Consequence: a source with decode errors copied verbatim fails verify (the original stays; safe direction).
- **Stream comparison** follows `outputOrder(model)` (mkv covers last). A source `attached_pic` written into mkv matches an output cover attachment (reprobed as a `png`/`mjpeg` attached_pic video stream, or an `attachment` stream) with the same codec; language/flags are not compared for it. Other streams: type; codec (the plan's codec, which after conversion is `hevc`/`h264`/`aac`/`subrip`/`mov_text`); language (both untagged, or `sameLanguage`); default, forced, attached_pic. Data streams: type only.
- **Targets (controller rulings R1 and Task 7):** run on every stream of the re-probed output, copied or converted, so levels are enforced here. The container, every video stream, the default audio (first flagged, else first audio) and the default subtitle (first flagged) block (`targets` failure); other tracks a target cannot play are returned as `warnings` (`<type> #<output index> won't direct-play on <target name>`), which both result shapes carry. Covers, attachments and data streams are exempt.
- **Timeouts:** ffprobe/ffmpeg calls in verify get 300 s each (decoding one second of a large file on a busy CPU).

### Task 12 fix round (no false passes)

- **Stream ends, per stream (supersedes "Duration is measured" above):** the container duration is no longer compared. Each output video (not cover art) and audio stream must end where its source stream ends: `|output end − source end| ≤ 0.25 s + the longer of the two last packet durations`, where a stream's end is `pts + duration` of its last packet. Both files are read only in a bounded window, `ffprobe -read_intervals <ref−2>%+<window to ref+3>`, so no file is ever scanned whole. A stream with packets in the window on one side and none on the other fails.
- **Reference duration:** the source's container duration, else its longest stream duration (ffprobe stream `duration` or the mkv `DURATION` tag), exported as `referenceDuration`. When neither is known, or the source's end cannot be read, the stream ends are not compared and verify adds a warning (`the source duration is unknown; stream ends were not compared`).
- **Decode every stream:** the decode checks map `-map 0:V? -map 0:a?`, i.e. every video stream other than cover art and every audio stream (ffmpeg's default picks one of each, so a broken second audio track passed).
- **`source-damaged`:** `VerifyInput` gains `sourcePath`. When the output fails a decode or duration check, the same check runs on the source (decode: the same command on the source; duration: do the failed streams in the source reach the source's own reference duration, and can its end be read). If any of them fails the same way, the result's `kind` is `source-damaged` and `sourceFailures` lists those failures (messages prefixed `source: `); otherwise `kind` is `output-invalid`. The pipeline (Task 16) ends a source-damaged job `kept_original` ("source has decode errors; nothing was replaced") without a retry.

## M1 engine Task 13: replace

- **Intent journal:** `replaceOriginal` writes the *intent* (`checking`, `writing_tmp`, `moving_original`, `installing_tmp`, `cleaning`) to `jobs.replace_journal` before the action it names, then `null` when done. The journal carries original, tmp, output and copy paths, `recycling`, the probed fingerprint and the output size. Reads (output size, a free recycle path) happen before the `checking` intent; every write after it. Any throw returns `failed` at the current intent and leaves the journal for the sweep; a check that fails before anything moved (different filesystem, taken tmp/copy path, original outside the library root) clears the journal and returns `failed` at `checking`.
- **Steps:** the tmp `.transcoda-<job>.tmp` is a copy (never a move) of the verified output beside the original (`COPYFILE_EXCL`), chowned to the configured owner when it differs (`EPERM` is a logged warning), then `chmod` to the original's mode (after chown, which may clear set-id bits), `utimes` to its atime/mtime, fsynced. The original's fingerprint is checked after any wait for space and again right before it moves; a change discards the output (and tmp) and returns `fingerprint-changed`. The original moves to the copy path (recycle folder, or `<file>.transcoda-<job>.orig` when recycling is off), the copy's directory is fsynced, the `recycled` row is recorded; the tmp is renamed onto the original path and the directory fsynced; `cleaning` unlinks the `.orig` when not recycling. Free space on the original's filesystem must be at least output size + 5 GiB (`waitForSpace`, default: re-check every 30 s).
- **Never rename over an existing path:** every rename in the replace and the sweep goes through `renameNoClobber`, which throws `ClobberRefused` when the destination exists. A taken recycle path gets `.1`, `.2`, … appended.
- **Same filesystem:** the recycle folder (created with `mkdir -p`) must be on the original's filesystem (`stat().dev`), else the job fails with `recycle folder <path> is on a different filesystem than <original>`; there is no copy+unlink fallback.
- **Cancel during replace:** an abort is honoured only before the replace starts (`failed` at `checking`, `cancelled before the replace started`, no journal written); once started the replace completes.
- **Startup sweep, decided from the disk** (O = original path exists, T = tmp exists, C = copy path exists; existence is `lstat`):

  | O | T | C | action | bucket |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | yes | – | no | unlink tmp, journal null, `requeueOnce(job, 'interrupted')` | `interrupted` |
  | yes | – | yes | leave both, journal null, `failed: replace left two copies: <original>, <copy>` | `conflicts` |
  | no | yes | yes | tmp → original (no clobber), fsync dir, journal `{ step: 'cleaning' }`, state stays `replacing` for the engine to finish | `completed` |
  | no | no | yes | copy → original (restore), drop the recycled row, journal null, `failed: interrupted; original restored` (not requeued) | `rolledBack` |
  | no | yes | no | leave tmp, journal null, `failed: original missing` | `conflicts` |
  | no | no | no | `failed: original missing` | `conflicts` |

  Refinements (found by fault injection; the step only says where to look, the disk decides): **O yes, C yes** where C has the journal's fingerprint (inode, size, mtime: the moved original) and O is a different file of exactly the output size with no tmp → the install happened and only the `cleaning` intent was lost → `completed`. **O yes, C no** at intent `installing_tmp`/`cleaning` where O is not the original and has the output size → the `.orig` was already removed → `completed` (the engine's unlink of the `.orig` must accept ENOENT). **O no, T yes, C yes** with a tmp that is not the output size → the original is restored instead (`rolledBack`) and the tmp removed. `completed` re-inserts the `recycled` row when it is missing. A restore refused because something now sits at the original path → `conflicts` (`could not restore the original: … ; the original is at <copy>`). Any other error while settling a job keeps its journal, marks it `failed: replace recovery failed: …` and the next start tries again.
- **Then:** running jobs without a journal are requeued once (`interrupted`); before anything in the transcode dir is touched, each `<transcodeDir>/<job>/ffmpeg.pid` is read and that process group SIGKILLed **only when the pid is alive and its command name contains `ffmpeg`** (`/proc/<pid>/comm` on Linux, `ps -o comm=` elsewhere), since a pid from before a restart may have been reused; everything under the transcode dir is removed; `.transcoda-<job>.tmp` files under library roots whose job had no journal are removed (symlinks are not followed). `tempRemoved` counts both. Nothing else is touched (`.orig` files are never removed by the sweep).
- **Requeue once:** `requeueOnce` sets `queued`, `requeues + 1`, reason, no lane/progress, `finished_at` cleared while `requeues < 1`; otherwise `failed` with the same reason. `attempt` is unchanged. A rolled-back job is not requeued (the file is back; the user decides).
- **Recycle index:** the `recycled` table (path, library, job, size, time) is written right after the original moves. `pruneRecycle` removes rows older than `recycle.retentionDays`, then per library the oldest rows while recycled bytes exceed `recycle.maxFreePercent` % of (free + recycled) on that filesystem; a missing file just drops its row, a file that cannot be removed keeps it (warning).
- **Interface additions beyond the plan:** `ReplaceInput` gains `libraryId` and `libraryRoot` (the recycle path and index need them); `ReplaceDeps` gains `recordRecycled(row)` and `log`; `SweepDeps` gains an optional `killStaleGroup(pidFile)` (default `killStaleFfmpeg`); `recyclePathFor` takes an optional suffix number `n`.

## M1 engine Task 14: scheduler

- **Lanes and capacities:** two lanes, `encode` and `quick`, one job each (structural: the lane map holds one job id per lane, so there is no capacity option). Probe/decide/build run outside the lanes, at most `prepWorkers` jobs at once (default 2). A prepared job waits in memory (`ready`) and lanes are granted by priority DESC, then queued time, then the order it became ready. A job leaves `queued` by moving to `probing` (with `startedAt` = now), so queued jobs are never held in memory.
- **Pause frees the lane:** pausing a job that holds its lane SIGSTOPs its attached process and frees the lane, so the next ready job starts; the job keeps its state (e.g. `encoding`) with `paused: true`. On resume it waits for its lane and gets it back before any ready job, then SIGCONT. A paused job that holds its lane but has no process attached (e.g. between ffmpeg runs) keeps the lane until a process attaches, which is then frozen at once. Queued, preparing and ready jobs only get `paused: true` and are skipped. `pauseJob` on a final or unknown job → `not-active`; `resumeJob` on a job that is not paused → `not-paused`.
- **Cancel:** a queued or ready job becomes `cancelled` at once (a ready job's temp dir is left for the startup sweep). A preparing or running job (frozen included) gets `signal.abort('cancel')` and its attached process `kill()`; the executor records `cancelled` (or `done`, "cancelled too late", once the replace started). If the executor settles without a final state after a cancel, the scheduler records `cancelled`. An executor that throws (not on cancel/shutdown) leaves the job `failed: internal error: <message>`.
- **Queue pause persists:** `pauseQueue` writes `queue.paused = true`, SIGSTOPs every process holding a lane and grants nothing new and prepares nothing new (preparations already started finish into `ready` and wait, so a job between build and lane grant never starts). `resumeQueue` writes `false`, SIGCONTs only the processes the queue pause stopped (user-paused jobs stay paused) and ticks. `start()` reads `queue.paused`, so the pause survives a restart.
- **Shutdown:** `stop()` aborts every job with `shutdown`, kills attached processes and waits for the executors; neither the scheduler nor the executors write anything then (state and temp stay for the startup sweep). Pause/resume/cancel after `stop()` report `not-active` / `not-paused`.
- **Events:** every scheduler state change goes through `updateJob` and publishes `job.changed` (which carries `currentNode`); lane and queue-pause changes publish `queue.changed`; final states the scheduler records (cancelled, internal failure) also publish `run.changed`. `EngineEvents.subscribe` starts at the call, interleaves a `ping` every 30 s (injectable), drops the oldest of more than 100 unread events and ends when its signal aborts.
- **Interface additions beyond the plan:** `JobControl` gains `setCurrentNode(node)` (sets the node shown in `job.changed` and publishes) and `changed(row)` (publishes `job.changed` for a row the executor wrote); `events.ts` exports `CurrentNode` and `jobChangedEvent(row, currentNode)`. `SchedulerOptions.prepWorkers` is optional (default 2) and there is no lane-capacity option.

### Task 14 fix round

- **Bounded ready backlog, with look-ahead per lane:** a job's lane is only known after Build. A new preparation starts only while (a) fewer than `prepWorkers` jobs are preparing, (b) some lane has no ready job waiting (preparation keeps looking ahead until every lane has one, so a quick job further down the queue still reaches the idle quick lane while encode jobs wait, as the two-lane design requires), and (c) preparing + ready jobs stay below `MAX_READY` = 4. (c) counts preparing jobs too so the ready backlog can never exceed 4; a long encode never gets the whole queue prepared. User-paused ready jobs count for none of these. `status()` gains `ready: string[]`.
- **Exception safety:** `runPrepare`/`runExecute` remove the job from memory and free its lane in `finally`, so a throwing `updateJob`/publish while recording an outcome never holds a lane; every tracked promise has a `.catch` that logs `scheduler task failed` (no unhandled rejection).
- **`JobExecutor.discard?(job)`** (optional, implemented in Task 16): called after the scheduler itself records `cancelled` for a job that had been prepared or started (a cancelled ready job; a prepare/execute that settled after a cancel without a final state) so the executor removes its temp dir. Not called when the executor recorded the cancel itself, nor for a queued job (it has no temp). Errors are logged.
- **Prepare returning `final` without a final state:** after a cancel → `cancelled` (+ discard); otherwise logged and `failed: internal error: prepare ended without a final state`.
- **`subscribe()`** returns a hand-written async iterator: `return()` releases the subscription and heartbeat timer at once (also before the first `next()`); an abort finishes a pending `next()`.
- **Slow subscribers:** when a subscriber has `MAX_BUFFERED_EVENTS` (100) unread events, the buffer is replaced by one `{ type: 'resync', at }` event (the dropped events are snapshots a refetch supersedes, so keeping the newer half would only replay stale state after the refetch); new events follow it. `EngineEvent` gains `resync`; the contract (Task 17) must carry it.
- **`startedAt`** is the start of the latest attempt: set to now every time the job leaves `queued` for `probing` (requeues included).

## M1 engine Task 13 fix round (review rulings)

The two corrections below supersede the matching bullets in the Task 13 entry above.


- **Correction (intent journal bullet):** replace the sentence "a check that fails before anything moved … clears the journal" with: *Any throw while the intent is `checking` (a failed check, an I/O error, a cancel) clears the journal: nothing has moved yet. A throw at a later intent leaves the journal for the sweep.*
- **Correction (cancel bullet):** *Cancel is honoured throughout `checking`, including while waiting for free space (nothing on disk has changed): the wait is raced against the abort, the journal is cleared and the result is `{ kind: 'cancelled' }` (the output stays in temp for the caller to discard). From the `writing_tmp` intent on, the replace completes and the job reports cancelled-too-late.*
- **Durable journal:** journal writes and the `recycled` insert are committed with `synchronous = FULL` (`durably()` toggles the pragma around one transaction; the DB otherwise stays WAL + NORMAL). Task 16 passes `durableReplaceWrites(db, jobId, now)`. A lost intent after a persisted rename is the one journal loss the disk cannot repair.
- **Atomic no-clobber rename:** `renameNoClobber` is `link(a, b)` (the kernel refuses an existing `b` with EEXIST) then `unlink(a)`. Where hard links are unavailable (EPERM, ENOTSUP/EOPNOTSUPP, EXDEV, EMLINK, ENOSYS: SMB, FAT, some FUSE) it falls back to check-then-rename, which keeps a small race window. A crash between link and unlink leaves one inode under two names; the sweep removes the extra name (O = C, or O = T, by `st_dev` + `st_ino`) instead of reporting two copies.
- **Different filesystem, also by EXDEV:** besides the `st_dev` compare, EXDEV from moving the original (two bind mounts of one filesystem share `st_dev`) removes the tmp, clears the journal and fails at `checking` with the same message; still no copy+unlink fallback.
- **fsync:** after a cross-directory move both directories are fsynced (replace move, sweep restore). A mount answering EINVAL/ENOTSUP/EOPNOTSUPP to fsync is logged as a warning and the replace continues; EIO and everything else stay fatal.
- **Unreachable library:** the sweep decides nothing for a journaled job unless its library root and the original's folder exist (and, when the original is absent, the copy's folder): an unmounted share reads as empty and would otherwise turn into an irreversible "original missing". Such a job keeps its journal, is marked `failed: library root unavailable: <path>` and counted in `conflicts`; the next start settles it from the disk. The stray-tmp walk skips missing roots and every tmp whose job had or still has a journal. (Limit: a share whose mount point exists but is empty, with the file directly in the root, is not detected.)
- **Settled jobs are not resurrected:** the O-yes/C-no row requeues once only when the job is in a running state; a job the pipeline already settled (failed, cancelled, queued) gets its tmp removed and journal cleared and keeps its state (`SweepResult.cleared`). Jobs marked `failed` by the unavailable/recovery-failed paths therefore are not requeued later either; the user requeues.
- **Restored earlier:** the original at its path at intent `installing_tmp`/`cleaning` (it had certainly moved) with no copy means an earlier sweep restored it and stopped before recording: `rolledBack`, not requeued.
- **Recycle prune vs. in-flight replaces:** `pruneRecycle` never removes a row whose job has a replace journal (between the move and the install, or while a replace awaits the sweep, it is the only copy of the original); such rows still count towards the cap. The check is repeated right before each unlink.
- **Sweep reads journaled jobs only** (`replace_journal IS NOT NULL`), not every job.
- **Stray-tmp walk cost (noted, not changed):** the walk recurses every library root (including the recycle folder) on each start; on a large NAS this can take minutes. Spec-mandated for now; a later change can limit it to the folders of jobs that ever wrote a tmp (from the jobs table).
- **Stale ffmpeg kill and pid namespaces:** in the container the server owns its pid namespace, so a stale `ffmpeg.pid` can only name a process of the container. Outside a container a reused pid that is another program's ffmpeg group leader (Jellyfin, Plex) would be killed; accepted (only a crash leaves a pid file; the alive + name check excludes everything else).
- **Interface changes:** `ReplaceJournal.libraryRoot`; `ReplaceResult` gains `{ kind: 'cancelled' }` (also for an abort before start); `ReplaceDeps.waitForSpace(needed, available, signal?)`; `pollForSpace(needed?, available?, signal?)`; `durableReplaceWrites`, `durably`; `SweepResult.cleared`; repo `jobsWithReplaceJournal`, `hasReplaceJournal`; fs-ops `syncDir`, `syncFile`, `syncDirs`.


## M1 engine Task 12 second fix round (verify: stream ends, decode-end, damaged sources)
- **Times are per file.** Every packet time is measured from its own file's start: container start_time, else the earliest stream start_time, else the first packet's pts. An mpegts source (start ≈ 1.4 s) and its mkv remux (start 0) therefore compare equal. ffprobe `-read_intervals` takes absolute times, so windows are offset by the start.
- **Each stream is compared at its own end.**
  - Expected end: the source stream's own duration (ffprobe `duration` + start_time, or the mkv `DURATION` tag). Otherwise the container duration. Otherwise the measured file end, read from a seek past the end, which lands on the demuxer's last seek point.
  - The search reads only that stream in [expected − 2 s, expected + 3 s]. With no packets it goes back ×4 each time, up to 600 s. With packets within 1 s of the window end it goes ahead ×4, up to 600 s.
  - The output stream is searched the same way around the source stream's found end. Tolerance (0.25 s + longer last packet) applies to the found ends, not to window edges.
  - A stream is always compared. If either end cannot be found within those bounds, or cannot be read, that is a `duration` failure (fail-safe).
  - Known cost: an mkv without cues (e.g. a truncated file) has no seek index, so libavformat reads it from the start to reach the end. Verify of such a file is slower, never wrong.
- **Duration failures are always `output-invalid`** (Task 16 may retry once). The source is never "duration-checked".
- **`source-damaged` needs proof on the source.** It applies only when every failure is a decode failure, at least one output stream is broken, and each broken stream's own source stream fails the same decode check.
  - Each stream is decoded alone with `-nofind_stream_info`, because stream probing decodes all streams and misattributes errors.
  - A damaged stream the job does not keep never explains anything.
  - mpegts streams are mapped by PID (`0:i:<id>`).
- **decode-end starts at a keyframe.** It starts at the last keyframe of the first non-cover video stream (else first audio) at least 3 s before that stream's end. The keyframe search covers 10 s, then up to 120 s.
  - mkv/mp4 and others: `-ss <keyframe>` (index seek lands on it).
  - mpegts/mpeg-ps: time seeks land mid-GOP and HEVC reports `Could not find ref with POC` (measured, including `-ss` at the exact keyframe time). These are opened at the keyframe's byte offset via ffmpeg's `subfile` protocol.
  - No keyframe found: the output falls back to `-sseof -3` (errors fail, the safe direction). A byte-seek source is not end-decoded and cannot explain a failure.
- **Stale source header → warning.** If the source container duration exceeds the latest kept stream end by more than 2 s, the warning is "the source header says X, but its streams end at Y". It is not a failure: the output is compared with what the source really contains.



## M1 engine Task 15: queueing

- **Media allowlist:** a folder queues only `.mkv .mp4 .m4v .mov .avi .ts .m2ts .webm .wmv .flv` (case-insensitive); everything else is never listed. A single non-media file queued directly becomes one `skipped: not media` job. The listing skips the recycle folder (`<root>/.transcoda-recycle` and a configured one) and replace tmps (`.transcoda-<job>.tmp`), does not follow symlinked directories (no loops, no escapes), and leaves out an unreadable subfolder.
- **Library roots by realpath:** `checkPath` resolves the path and every live root with `realpath`, so a trailing slash, a symlinked root (stored or given) and a root stored by another spelling all match. A path written under a root must resolve under *that* root: a symlink that leads out, even into another library, is `symlink escapes root` (a direct queue is `path-rejected`; inside a folder it is a `skipped` job with that reason). A path written outside every root is accepted when it resolves into one (an alias of the root). Unresolvable roots (unmounted) and soft-deleted libraries hold nothing. Jobs, runs (`requestedPath`) and `files` store realpaths; `PathCheck.library.path` is the root's realpath (Task 16 passes it to the replace).
- **Two names for one file** (a symlink to a sibling) in one folder queue give one job.
- **Settle window:** 3 minutes by default (`SETTLE_MINUTES`, injectable; 0 turns it off): a file modified less than that ago is `skipped: still growing`. Hardlinked files (`nlink > 1`) are `skipped: hardlinked` unless the library processes them. A file that cannot be stat'ed or opened for reading is `skipped: unreadable` (also one that vanished between the listing and the check).
- **History rule:** a file whose fingerprint has a final result (`done`, `nothing_to_do`, `kept_original`, `failed`) for the same workflow *and version* from a real (non-dry-run) job is `already_processed`, reason `already <outcome> on <createdAt>` (the newest). A failure already retried once stays final. Dry-run jobs never count, whatever their outcome (the query joins `jobs.dry_run = 0`; the unfiltered repo helper `findFinalResult` was removed in the final fix wave). `force` skips the rule and queues every processable file again; skip reasons still apply.
- **Workflow:** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: the active version wins, not the mode.)* `workflowId` ?? the library's default ?? `no-default-workflow`. The current version is pinned and loaded through `loadWorkflowForJob` (all-safe migrations, validation): a missing/deleted workflow or a sub-workflow is `workflow-missing`, an invalid or behaviour-migrated version `workflow-invalid` with its issues. **Workflow mode wins:** the run (and its jobs) is a dry run when asked, when the workflow is in dry-run mode, or when it needs review (the Task 10 rule). *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
- **One transaction per run** (run, files, jobs); then one `run.changed` and one `onQueued()` (scheduler tick). A folder with no media files is a run with no jobs. Queueing a path inside the recycle folder is `path-rejected: in recycle folder`.
- **Libraries:** the path must exist and be a directory; its realpath is stored. Library roots may not nest (equal, inside or around another live root, compared by stored and real path) → `overlaps`. A relative recycle folder is resolved under the root. A default workflow that is missing or not of kind `workflow` → `workflow-missing` (an addition to the planned `createLibrary`/`updateLibrary` results, so the API can return a 4xx instead of an FK error). Delete is a soft delete, `busy` while any job of the library is active. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
- **Run detail:** `runSummary` lists every job in queue order with state, reason and the result's before/after bytes and `replacedTo` (null until a result exists); `listRuns` returns summaries without files, newest first.
- **Browse:** `relPath` must be relative and stay under the root after `realpath`; a symlink entry is shown only when it resolves inside the same library; recycle and tmp entries are hidden; files are media only; names sorted by code unit. A missing folder or a file is `not-found`, anything leaving the root `path-rejected`.
- **`verifyFullDecode` per library (Jan's ruling):** boolean, default false (column `libraries.verify_full_decode`, `0/1` CHECK, added to the unshipped migration 0003). When true, verify decodes the whole output before replacing instead of only its start and end. It is set through `createLibrary` / `updateLibrary` and the repo. The verify side is wired in Task 16; `verify.ts` is unchanged here.

## M1 engine Task 16: pipeline

- **Temp layout:** everything a job writes while it runs is under `<transcodeDir>/<jobId>/`: `out.mkv|out.mp4`, `ffmpeg.pid`, and `cover-<sourceIndex>.<ext>` (mkv covers, extracted with `coverExtractArgs` before the encode; a missing image is `failed: render: …`). The pipeline creates the dir (and `<configDir>/logs/jobs`) itself, only in execute: a dry run writes nothing at all. The temp dir is removed *before* a final state (or a retry) is written, so a retry's fresh dir is never hit by an old removal; on a shutdown nothing is removed (the sweep does it).
- **Log path:** *(superseded by the Task 16 fix round: one log per attempt, `<configDir>/logs/jobs/<jobId>.attempt-<n>.log`, truncated at the start of the attempt)* `<configDir>/logs/jobs/<jobId>.log` (ffmpeg's stderr, appended across attempts), stored as `job_results.log_path`. ffmpeg runs with `-loglevel level+warning` (warnings and errors only, each line tagged `[warning]`/`[error]`) plus the renderer's `-nostats`; the dry-run `command` shows the same argv. A failure reason reads only the last 8 KiB of the log; `jobLog` returns at most its last 64 KiB.
- **Re-check at job start (coordinator ruling, Task 15 review):** `checkPath` runs again on `job.path`. Not ok → `skipped` with the check's reason (a library removed since queueing is `outside library root`, a folder swapped for a symlink out of the root `symlink escapes root`); another library → `skipped: library changed`; a realpath that is no longer the job's path → `skipped: path now leads to another file`. Nothing is touched in these cases. `skipReasonFor` runs again; any reason, `still growing` included, → `skipped` with that reason (superseded the first version's `requeueOnce` for `still growing`: the scheduler would take it again at once). The check runs once more right before the replace, which then gets `PathCheck.realPath` and `PathCheck.library.path`, both asserted equal to their own `realpath`.
- **Probe stored on the file:** the job's probe is written to `files.last_probe` through `upsertFile`; `upsertFile` now keeps the stored probe when it is given `null` (a re-queue no longer wipes it). Job detail shows that probe without `raw`.
- **Walk path persisted:** new column `jobs.walk_path` (JSON `WalkStep[]`, unshipped migration 0003): the decided path after Decide, the built path after Build, `WalkFailure.path` on a walk failure (then `currentNode` is set to its last step). Trace entries are stored at their own `seq` (`appendTrace` no longer numbers them; `nextTraceSeq` gives a new attempt its first seq), so DB order is walk emission order across attempts.
- **Outcomes in prepare:** workflow missing → `failed: workflow missing`; invalid or behaviour-migrated → `failed: workflow invalid: <n> error(s)` with the issues as warnings; identity model → `nothing_to_do` (result with `plan.rewrite = false`, no lane); a changed model whose finish actions contain neither `replace` nor `move-copy` → `kept_original: path never writes the result` (the trace shows the build); a model `renderArgs` refuses → `failed: render: <message>` (also the muxer-compatibility check of kept data/attachment/subtitle streams); effective dry run → `dry_run` with the rendered `command`, the `estimated_bytes`, the reason `loaded.note` and the warning `owner would change from <uid> to <puid>` when PUID differs from the file's owner. Otherwise the lane: `encode` iff a kept non-cover video stream is converted, else `quick`.
- **Estimate:** median after/before ratio of the workflow's 20 newest `done` results (any version) × before bytes; null without history.
- **Plan (`JobPlan`):** one entry per probe stream (dropped → `drop` with the diff's parenthesised note); a second plan for the same source (an added audio track) gets its own entry marked `added: …`; notes are diffFromProbe's per-stream text. `isOriginalLanguage` compares with the first `language` a data node produced on the path.
- **Progress:** written at most once per second (`PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS`), `toJobProgress` against the source duration; `-progress` frame counts are parsed too (`ProgressSample.frames`).
- **Verify depth (Jan's ruling):**
  - (a) *In-pass checks* after a 0 exit: every `[error]`/`[fatal]`/`[panic]` line of the job log fails the job. A line whose first context is the input side (`in#…`, `…ist#…`, `dec:…`, or a bare `[<codec> @ 0x…]` naming a source stream's codec that is not also an encoder of the job, i.e. libavcodec's decoder) means the source is damaged → `kept_original: source has decode errors; nothing was replaced`, never retried; any other error line (encoder, muxer, `out#`, unknown) → output-invalid (retried). The output's video frame count (`frame=` of the last progress block) must match the source's exact count within max(2 frames, 0.5 %) — checked only when the source states it (`nb_frames`, or mkvmerge's `NUMBER_OF_FRAMES` tag) and exactly one non-cover video stream is kept; `-progress` reports no audio sample count, and the verify duration check covers audio.
  - (b) *Sampled decode:* verify decodes 10 one-second spots spread evenly over the output (each ≥ 1 s from the start and ≥ 2 s from the end; outputs under 4 s rely on decode-start/end), check `decode-sample`. mkv/mp4 are opened with input `-ss` (the demuxer seeks to the keyframe before the spot); byte-seek formats (mpegts) at the anchor stream's last keyframe at or before the spot via `subfile` (as decode-end). A failing spot is attributed per stream exactly like decode-start/end: decoded on the source at the same time, `source-damaged` only when every broken output stream's source stream fails there too.
  - (c) *Full decode* when the library's `verifyFullDecode` is on: the whole output (`decode-full`, attributed the same way), with its own 6 h timeout.
  - `source-damaged` from verify → `kept_original` with that reason, no retry.
- **Retry once, inside the job:** a failure a second attempt can change (ffmpeg could not start or exited non-zero, output-side encode errors, frame count, verify `output-invalid`, replace failed, move/copy I/O) on `attempt 1` puts the job back to `queued` with `attempt 2` (same job id; `paused` and `finished_at` cleared; reason kept; trace entries keep their `attempt`). Deterministic failures (workflow missing/invalid, walk/build failure, render error, cover extraction, probe failure, path checks) and anything on attempt 2 end `failed`. No retry while the job has a replace journal (the sweep owns it).
- **Requeue once on a fingerprint change** before or during the replace (`replaceOriginal` → `fingerprint-changed`): `requeueOnce` (`queued`, `requeues + 1`, reason `file changed during processing`); the second time `failed` with that reason. `requeueOnce` now also clears `paused`, and refuses (throws) while the job has a replace journal.
- **Cancel:** during encode → the group is killed, temp removed, `cancelled` with a result. `replaceOriginal` honours the cancel until it writes the tmp (`{kind: 'cancelled'}` → `cancelled`); after that it completes and the job ends `done` with reason `cancelled too late`. A job cancelled while ready (prepared, waiting for its lane) is recorded by the scheduler; `Pipeline.discard` removes its temp dir and records a `cancelled` result with its plan.
- **Shutdown:** the executor returns at once without a database write or temp cleanup (the row keeps its running state for the sweep), with one exception: a replace that completed is recorded as `done` even then (otherwise the sweep would requeue an already-replaced file). The scheduler accepts a prepare that put its job back in the queue (`requeueOnce`) instead of failing it.
- **Finish actions in order:** `size-gate` saving `(before − after)/before × 100` (one decimal) below the minimum → `kept_original: saved only X%, below N%` and stop; `replace` → recycle folder `action ?? library ?? <root>/.transcoda-recycle` (a relative folder resolved under the root), owner from PUID/PGID (both set), journal and recycle index written with `durableReplaceWrites` (each its own `synchronous = FULL` commit, never inside a transaction), `replaced_to = 'recycle'` (always, with this rule); `move-copy` → `<outputRoot>/<path relative to the library root>` with the output's extension, only under a configured `TRANSCODA_OUTPUT_ROOTS` entry (else `failed`), written as `.transcoda-<job>.tmp` then renamed without clobbering (an existing destination fails); `notify` → `fetch` with a JSON summary (`jobId, runId, path, workflowId, workflowVersion, outcome, beforeBytes, afterBytes, replacedTo`) and a 10 s timeout; a failure or non-2xx is a warning, never a job failure.
- **Webhook URLs are never stored:** warnings name the URL as `scheme://host/…` (also scrubbed out of error messages); the trace's finish findings stay empty; the URL is not logged.
- **Results:** `job_results` is written on every final state the pipeline records (outcome = state, `plan` whenever a model was built, `before_bytes` = the probed size), replacing an earlier row of the same job; `run.changed` follows. A job that ends before it is fingerprinted (workflow missing/invalid, path checks) has no result row.
- **Engine service:** `start()` = startup sweep → recycle prune (settings `recycle.retentionDays`/`recycle.maxFreePercent`) → each job the sweep left `replacing` at intent `cleaning` is finished (`.orig` unlinked when not recycling, ENOENT accepted; `done` with a result) → scheduler start. Bootstrap awaits it after `upgradeAll()`; the 10-minute maintenance timer also prunes the recycle folder; `main.ts` awaits `engine.stop()` before closing the server. `createTestApp` never starts the engine and defaults `settleMinutes` to 0.

## M1 engine Task 18: examples and the second-run gate

1. **The gate drives the services, not REST.** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: setMode is now activate.)* Task 17 is not merged, so the gate calls the services directly. A small `gateApi(t)` object maps one-to-one onto the REST routes (workflows create/save/setMode, jobs.queue, runs.get, jobs.get). Moving the gate to REST means changing only that object, plus the `confirm` flag on setMode.
2. **The gate does not pass `exec: defaultExec`.** The engine uses each module's own exec (probe 60 s, verify 300 s, cover 60 s), as `main.ts` does in production. `defaultExec` has a 10 s timeout and a 4 MB buffer, which is meant for the capability probe.
3. **Track cleanup keeps the cover.** I read `cover-attachments keepCover keepAttachments false` as keepCover = true and keepAttachments = false, the same notation as `keepOriginal keepUntagged`. So the test asserts the cover is kept, where the brief's outcome list says "no cover". The brief contradicts itself; if "no cover" was meant, `extras.settings.keepCover` becomes `false` in `track-cleanup.json` and the test's `attachedPic` assertion flips.
4. **Device-optimised `h264-big.mkv` → `nothing_to_do`**, which is the brief's alternative. H.264 High plays on android-tv, the file is already mkv, and its flags are untouched.
5. **Shrink more is pinned more tightly than the brief allows.** The brief says "every file done or kept_original", but the HEVC files take the unconnected `pass` port and end `nothing_to_do`. The brief contradicts its own graph here. The test pins h264-big → `done` and the HEVC files → `nothing_to_do`.
6. **A second-run `already_processed` is accepted when the first outcome was `nothing_to_do` or `kept_original`.** Both leave the file untouched; Shrink more's README note covers the `kept_original` case.
7. **The fixture manifest's `hevcEncoder` field means the encoder picked for the run.** For `h264-big` it is not used; the README says so.
8. **Open question (pending Jan's ruling): a replace keeps the original name when the container changes.** With Device-optimised, `tigger-pooh.mp4` is replaced in place and then holds Matroska under its `.mp4` name: `replace` installs the result at the original path, while `move-copy` uses the output's extension. The gate and `examples/workflows/README.md` pin this current behaviour. The alternative is renaming to `<stem>.mkv` on replace (no clobber), which touches the replace journal, the startup sweep and the `files` path.
9. **The gate proves the originals and the no-write rules on disk:** every untouched file hashes to its fixture, every replaced original sits in `<root>/.transcoda-recycle` hashing to its fixture, and a snapshot of the whole library root (dotfolders and recycle folder included) plus the temp dir listing is identical before and after the second run and the dry run. The settle deadline is 100 s per run; each example's timeout is 240 s.


## M1 engine Task 17: API


- **Scopes.** Reads need `read`. `jobs.queue` needs `jobs:create`, or `plan` for `dryRun: true` only (`requireAnyScope`, plus a FORBIDDEN check in the handler). Library changes, job pause/resume/cancel and queue pause/resume need `jobs:manage`. `events.subscribe` needs `read`. No new scopes; this supersedes the design's `jobs:read`/`jobs:write`. Sessions pass every scope check, and their mutations need the CSRF token.
- **SSE.**
  - `GET /api/v1/events` is served by oRPC's event iterator: `eventIterator(EngineEventSchema)`, with each value validated.
  - The first event is a `queue.changed` snapshot.
  - The engine's `ping { at }` heartbeat arrives every 30 s. oRPC also sends its own comment keep-alive every 5 s.
  - `resync { at }` means events were dropped for a slow reader; the client refetches.
  - Data lines are the event JSON.
- **Error codes:**
  - `PATH_REJECTED` (400, `{ path, reason }`): `reason` is the queue's `SkipReason`/`PathCheck` text, e.g. `outside library root`, `symlink escapes root`, `in recycle folder`, `unreadable`, `path too long`, `not found`.
  - `LIBRARY_PATH_INVALID` (400, `{ reason }`): `not found`, `unreadable`, `not a directory`, `path too long`, or `overlaps library "<name>"`.
  - `DEFAULT_WORKFLOW_INVALID` (400): a library's `defaultWorkflowId` (create or update) that is not an existing workflow. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
  - `LIBRARY_BUSY` (409).
  - `NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW` (400). *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
  - `JOB_NOT_ACTIVE` (409): pause or cancel of a job that is not active, and resume of a job that is not paused.
  - A missing workflow given to `jobs.queue` is 404 NOT_FOUND.
  - A path containing NUL is rejected by the input schema (400 `BAD_REQUEST`) before any disk access.
- **M2 Phase B additions:**
  - List rows carry `libraryName`, `workflowName`, `reason` and `result { outcome, beforeBytes, afterBytes, replacedTo }`.
  - `jobs.list` returns per-state `counts`: every state, filtered by `libraryId` only.
  - `jobs.list` `state` takes one or several states (repeat the parameter); `before` is the id of the last job seen; `limit` is 1–200, default 50.
  - `jobs.get` adds `trace`, `result` (including `estimatedBytes`, `command` and `warnings`), `probe` (without the raw ffprobe JSON), `graph` (the pinned version), `plan` and `path` (the walk path, partial on failure).
  - `runs.get` adds `files`, with `libraryName` and `workflowName` on the summary.
  - New: `jobs.stats({ since })`, `libraries.browse({ id, path })`, and `system.capabilities.deviceProfiles`.
  - `job.changed` carries `libraryId`, `workflowId` and `currentNode`.
- **Naming deviations from the M2 design:**
  - The row field is `finishedAt`; the design wrote `endedAt`.
  - `replacedTo` is `'recycle' | 'deleted'`; the design wrote `'orig-file'`. Every M1 replace recycles (`recycle`); `deleted` only appears for a replace that took the `.orig` path (no recycle folder) and was finished by the startup sweep, and that `.orig` copy is removed, so there is no file to point at.
- **Other choices:**
  - **Graph:** `JobDetail.graph` is nullable. It is null when the pinned version cannot be read; the job itself still reads.
  - **Log:** `jobs.log` returns `{ log }` (the last 64 KiB), not a bare string.
  - **Job controls:** pause, resume and cancel return `{ ok: true }`.
  - **Queue:** `queue.pause` and `queue.resume` return the new `QueueStatus`. `QueueStatus` omits the scheduler's internal `ready` list.
  - **Capabilities:** the API capabilities schema is `CapabilitiesSchema`, and the probe kept by the server is `FfmpegCapabilitiesSchema` / type `Capabilities`.
  - **Webhook URL:** a `core:notify-webhook` URL shows only its scheme and host (`https://host/…`) in `JobDetail.graph`. Trace findings, results and warnings were already free of it (Task 9/10).

### Task 17 fix round (review rulings)

- **Recycling cannot be switched off in M1.** A library's `recycleFolder: null` means the default `<root>/.transcoda-recycle`; a relative folder is under the root. The contract comments say so (they had said null meant "recycling off").
- **Redaction covers Run-node overrides.** In `JobDetail.graph`, a `core:run-subworkflow` override whose setting key is `url`, or whose string value parses as an http(s) URL, also shows only scheme and host.
- **Browse reasons come from the queue service.** `QueueService.browse` returns `{ kind: 'path-rejected', reason }` (`outside library root`, `symlink escapes root`, `in recycle folder`, `unreadable`, `path too long`); the procedure passes it through. A folder that cannot be listed is `unreadable`, not a 500.
- **Over-long paths.** `ENAMETOOLONG` maps to `path too long` in `checkPath` (and for library creation), so it is a `PATH_REJECTED`/`LIBRARY_PATH_INVALID`, never a 500.
- **Accepted for M1:** an open SSE stream outlives the revocation of the token or the end of the session that opened it (credentials are checked when the stream opens; the client reconnects with its current credentials). Library roots are unrestricted (any directory the server can read) and need `jobs:manage`; Transcoda is a single-admin tool, so whoever holds `jobs:manage` decides what the engine may touch.

## 2026-10-16 M1 engine step

Summary of the M1 engine track. The rulings themselves are in the sections named here; where a later section supersedes an earlier bullet, the later one wins (the fix-round and second-fix-round sections say so).

- **Task 1, deferred cleanups** (`M1 engine Task 1: deferred cleanups`): validation cache shape, transitive stale-parent flags, unupgradable children, reviews that stick across restarts (`workflow_review_acks`), `listNodes` cache, `verify` removed from `core:replace-original`.
- **Task 7, targets** (`M1 engine Task 7: targets`): option A (one output that plays on every selected target), where `targets` lives, post-conversion checks, no image-to-text subtitle conversion, `whenUnsupported`, `auto` encoder order. Narrowed by the R1 ruling in the Task 9 fix round (container, video, default audio and default subtitle must play; other tracks only warn).
- **Task 8, validation** (`M1 engine Task 8: validation`): `FINISH_ORDER`, port-aware `NO_WRITE_PATH`, bypass in the walk, `TARGET_UNSUPPORTED`, `ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`, output roots.
- **Task 10, loading and walk** (`M1 engine Task 10: loading and walk` and `Task 10 fix round (live job graph)`): the job runs its pinned, migrated, validated version; a behaviour-pending node blocks the run; dry-run rules and workflow mode; trace entries (including the `pass` phase, `seq`, ports), the persisted walk path, `currentNode`.
- **Task 12, verify** (`M1 engine Task 12: verify`, `Task 12 fix round (no false passes)`, `M1 engine Task 12 second fix round (verify: stream ends, decode-end, damaged sources)`): checks run all-or-report; per-stream end comparison; decode of every stream; decode-end from a keyframe; `source-damaged` needs proof on the source; defaults rule only when audio exists.
- **Task 13, replace** (`M1 engine Task 13: replace` and `Task 13 fix round (review rulings)`): intent journal, never rename over an existing path, same-filesystem recycle, startup sweep decided from the disk, durable journal writes, unreachable-library handling, recycle index and pruning.
- **Task 14, scheduler** (`M1 engine Task 14: scheduler` and `Task 14 fix round`): two lanes, pause frees the lane, cancel, queue pause persisted, bounded ready backlog, shutdown writes nothing, event stream with ping and resync.
- **Task 15, queueing** (`M1 engine Task 15: queueing`): media allowlist, realpath library roots, settle window, history rule and `force`, workflow mode wins, one transaction per run, library rules, run detail, browse, per-library `verifyFullDecode`.
- **Task 16, pipeline** (`M1 engine Task 16: pipeline`): temp layout and logs, re-check at job start, outcomes in prepare, estimate, verify depth, retry once, requeue on fingerprint change, cancel, shutdown, finish actions, webhook URLs never stored, results, engine service start order.
- **Task 17, API** (`M1 engine Task 17: API`, written by that task): scope mapping, SSE, error codes, the additions the M2 editor needs, `finishedAt` and `replacedTo: 'recycle' | 'deleted'`.

Verify depth as built (Jan's ruling): in-pass error and frame-count checks on every encode, a sampled decode of the output (start, end and ten spots), and an optional full decode per library (`verifyFullDecode`, off by default). The replace is crash-safe (journal plus startup sweep) and the original goes to the recycle folder.

Rulings that belong to no single task:

- **No engine-owned UI.** All job screens (queue, job page with the live graph trace, run summary, dry-run plan) moved to the M2 editor track; nothing under `apps/web` changed in this track. The API and events carry what those screens need: trace entries with `nodeId`, `workflowPath`, `reason`, `findings`, the persisted walk path, `progress`, `lane`, `paused`, `currentNode`, the per-stream `plan`, and run counts that reconcile.
- **No new scopes.** The scopes stay exactly `read`, `plan`, `jobs:create`, `jobs:manage` and `workflows:write`, and are exact (no implication); a dry run needs `plan` or `jobs:create`, a real run `jobs:create`.
- **Examples use `auto` encoders** (`hevc_nvenc` on the NVIDIA server, `libx265` on a CPU-only machine), so the same graphs run everywhere. Encoder `auto` never switches codec, only the implementation.
- **Tests are CPU-only.** Encoders in tests are `libx264`/`libx265`, real-ffmpeg tests run only when the fixtures exist (`pnpm fixtures`), and NVENC is exercised only by the manual copy run in [`copy-run.md`](copy-run.md) (RTX 3060: NVENC H.264 and HEVC; no AV1 encoder).
- **The engine gate is a manual run on copies:** a dry run and a real run over 20 copies on the home server, originals intact and a second run that does nothing; 500 files across runs without a loss before the real library is touched.

### M1 engine Task 16 fix round (review rulings)

- **Never retry after a write.** Once a replace or move-copy has written, a later failure, refusal, size gate or stop does not retry. The job ends `done` with `replaced_to`/`after_bytes` and reason `finished with a problem: <…>`, and the problem is also in `warnings`. A shutdown after a write records `done` and skips the remaining actions.
- **Retry classes.**
  - Retried once (`attempt 2`, same job): ffmpeg could not start or exited non-zero; output-side errors during the encode; unconfirmed source-side errors; `nb_frames` mismatch; verify `output-invalid`; move-copy I/O errors before anything was written.
  - Final at once: workflow missing/invalid; walk/build failure; render and cover errors; probe failure; path checks at start or before the replace; every replace failure (at `checking`: the check would refuse again; past it: settled at once); move-copy destination exists / root not configured; anything on attempt 2.
  - `source-damaged`: `kept_original`, never retried.
  - Requeues (retry, or a fingerprint change: `requeues + 1`, then `failed: file changed during processing`) are returned to the scheduler. The scheduler writes `queued` after the lane is released.
- **Still growing at start → `skipped: still growing`.** It is not requeued; ingest or the user requeues it later.
- **In-pass checks read stderr live, not the log.** They do not use `-err_detect`/`-xerror`.
  - ffmpeg runs with `-loglevel level+warning -stats_period 0.1`, and each stderr line reaches the pipeline as it is written (and is still logged).
  - Lines are classified by `classifyErrorLine` (input `in#`, input-stream `…ist#`, decoder `dec:` or a bare source codec that is not one of the job's encoders → source; anything else → output).
  - Source errors before the first progress report with frames written are ignored and counted in a warning (a stream cut mid-GOP).
  - A later source error must be reproduced on the source before the original is kept: a 7 s decode from 1 s before the output time at the error. It starts that early because the encoder's lookahead puts output time behind the decoder. It is keyframe-aligned (`-ss` for mkv/mp4, `subfile` at the last keyframe for mpegts). Otherwise it counts as output-invalid.
  - Known limit: on a very fast encode, damage in the first ~0.1 s of wall time counts as leading. Verify still checks the output.
- **Verify on a stream cut mid-GOP:** when the kept media is video only, the source's timeline is measured from its first video keyframe (within 30 s), where ffmpeg starts the output. With audio kept, the source's own start stays, as ffmpeg keeps it.
- **Per-attempt logs:** `<configDir>/logs/jobs/<jobId>.attempt-<n>.log`, truncated when the attempt starts (a fingerprint requeue keeps the attempt number and so reuses its file). `jobLog` returns the result's log for a final job and the current attempt's log otherwise.
- **Replace failures past `checking` are settled immediately** with the startup sweep's rules (`settleReplaceNow`, never requeued):
  - `completed` → `.orig` removed and the job continues as replaced, with the failure as a problem;
  - original restored, or never moved → final failure;
  - undecidable (library unreachable, two copies) → final failure with the sweep's reason; the journal stays for the next start.
  - The startup finish of a `cleaning` replace replaces any earlier result row of the job (keeping its plan, command, log and estimate).
- **Abortable tools:** every ffprobe/ffmpeg run outside the encode (probe, cover extraction, verify, source confirmation) gets the job's abort signal. `makeExec` SIGKILLs the child on abort; `execFile`'s own `signal` sends SIGTERM, which a stuck process can ignore. A shutdown therefore leaves no ffmpeg behind, and a cancel during verify takes effect at once.
- **currentNode:** the decide/build node while walking; the last build-phase node while encoding and verifying; null while replacing.
- **Frame count:** fails on `nb_frames` only; a disagreeing mkvmerge NUMBER_OF_FRAMES tag is a warning.
- **Recycle folder:** a node's folder is resolved under the library root and must stay within it (`replace: recycle folder X is outside the library root R`, final). The library's own setting is the admin's choice.
- **Shutdown and startup:** `main.ts` gives shutdown 10 s overall (`SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS`): the engine stops first (ffmpeg groups SIGKILLed, the pipeline writes nothing, the spawner's 2 s exit grace bounds the wait), then the HTTP server closes. A failing engine start (e.g. an unreadable transcode dir, or a sweep that throws) is logged as `engine did not start: …; no jobs will run`. The server still starts and the engine stays stopped with `engine.startError` set; Task 17 can surface it.

## M1 engine final fix wave (final review)

Rulings and fixes from the whole-branch review (`final-review.md`); they supersede earlier entries where they differ.

- **Default flags are normalised before render (I1).** After the build, a model that writes something (not the identity) gets its kept streams' default flags set to what the output will hold, in output order: per type the first flagged default stays and the others are cleared; with no default audio the first kept audio becomes default; an mp4 with subtitles and no default subtitle gets its first kept subtitle flagged (ffmpeg's mp4 muxer flags the first audio and the first subtitle when none is flagged, measured with ffmpeg 9.0.2). Each change is noted on that stream in the plan (`default; no audio track was default`, `not default; only one default audio track`, `default; mp4 cannot have no default subtitle`). The identity check runs before it, so an unchanged file is never rewritten just for its flags; a model whose only change normalisation undoes is `nothing_to_do`. Verify's rules (exactly one default audio when there is audio, at most one default subtitle, every flag as planned) stay strict. Fixtures `no-default-audio.mkv`, `two-defaults-h264.mkv`, `no-default-sub.mkv` and examples-gate cases cover Downsize without a default audio and with two, an mkv → mp4 remux without a default subtitle, and `core:set-default-track` with `subtitleLanguages: []` into an mp4.
- **Matroska `default_mode` must be passthrough.** Normalisation assumes the matroska muxer writes flags as given: ffmpeg 9.0.2 reports `default_mode … (default passthrough)`. The server's jellyfin-ffmpeg has to be checked on the box; the copy-run playbook has that check step (stop if it says `infer`/`infer_no_subs`).
- **Done after any write (I2, I3).** Once a replace installed the result or a move-copy finished, the job ends `done` with `replacedTo`/`afterBytes`, whatever happens next: a replace whose `.orig` cannot be removed after an at-once settle is `done` with `finished with a problem: …; the original copy <path> could not be removed, the next start retries` (the journal stays; the startup sweep finishes the cleaning), and an unexpected throw after a write (the pipeline's catch-all) is `done` with `finished with a problem: internal error: …`. What has been written is kept on the prepared job, where the catch-all sees it.
- **Sub-workflows are never flagged (I4).** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: no review flags exist any more.)* The upgrade pass sets `needsReview` and enabled → dry run only on workflows of kind `workflow`: a sub-workflow upgraded with a behaviour change, or pinning a stale child, keeps its flags; its reasons reach every parent through the propagation (`Sub-workflow M needs a review because …`). Supersedes the parts of M1 engine Task 1 (N3/N5) that flagged middle sub-workflows.
- **Webhook URLs are write-only in the workflow API (I6).** `workflows.get` returns a notify node's `url` and URL-valued Run-node overrides as `scheme://host/…` (the job API's redaction, now in `rpc/redact.ts`), also a notify `url` default in a sub-workflow's settings list; `list`, `versions` and `save` return no graph. `workflows.save` replaces a value that equals the redacted form of the base version's value (same node id and type) with the stored value; any other value is stored as sent. The editor shows these fields like a password field. Redaction stays conservative: any Run-node override string that parses as an http(s) URL is redacted too (a media-server base URL as well).
- **Container change keeps the file name (Jan's ruling, I5).** A replace that changes the container installs the result at the original path, so an `.mp4` may hold Matroska (Device-optimised). Players that route by extension may mis-handle such files; a future opt-in setting "rename on container change" (no-clobber rename to `<stem>.<container ext>`) can change that. This is a decision, not an open question.
- **Who drops a workflow to dry run (Jan's ruling).** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: saves never change what runs.)* A behaviour change the user saves (nodes, settings, edges) keeps an enabled workflow enabled; automatic changes — an upgrade with a behaviour change, a changed or broken sub-workflow pin — drop it to dry run with `needsReview`. As built: `workflows.save` flags only Run-node pin changes, dropped overrides and (separately, M1 final review I3) a save with errors; `upgradeAll` flags behaviour upgrades and the parents of changed or broken sub-workflows.
- **Pause outside ffmpeg (M1, M2).** A job paused while no process runs (verify, between the encode and the finish actions) waits at the start of verify and before the finish actions until it is resumed, keeping its lane; a cancel or shutdown still ends it. Every final-state write clears `paused` (in `updateJob`, like `finished_at`).
- **Queueing inside the recycle folder (M4):** a file, like a folder, in the recycle folder (or a replace tmp) queued directly is `path-rejected: in recycle folder`, no run.
- **Library recycle folder (M6):** must be strictly inside the library root; the root itself, a parent of it, or a folder outside it is `path-invalid: the recycle folder must be a folder inside the library root` (`LIBRARY_PATH_INVALID` on create and now on update). Supersedes "the library's own setting is the admin's choice" (Task 16 fix round).
- **Bypassed nodes (M5):** `TARGET_UNSUPPORTED` and `ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` are not reported for a bypassed node (it runs no hook; it may be bypassed because its encoder is missing).
- **Output roots (M7):** `TRANSCODA_OUTPUT_ROOTS` entries are normalised at load (`resolve`: no trailing slash, no `..`), and validation compares a move-copy `outputRoot` resolved, as the pipeline does.
- **Probe (M8):** an empty `title` tag is no title (`null`).
- **Dead exports (M9):** `findFinalResult` (no dry-run filter) and `libraryForPath` (a lexical prefix match, against the realpath rule) were removed; `fingerprintOf`, `walk()` and `liveProcessGroups` stay as `@internal` test helpers.
- **FsOps (M10):** queueing's folder listing (`readdir`) and read check (`canRead`, new) and the pipeline's log truncation (`truncate`, new) go through `FsOps`; only `readTail` opens files directly.
- **Job counts and stats (M12, M13):** `jobs.list` `counts` honour the list's scope (`runId` and `libraryId`), not its `state` filter or paging. `jobs.stats` `failed` counts jobs in state `failed` that finished since `since` (a job the sweep failed has no result row).
- **Test budgets (I7):** the polling `until()` helpers of the engine, pipeline and engine-API suites wait up to 10 s (test timeout 30 s in those files); the SSE release test polls with `vi.waitFor` instead of one macrotask.


## M2 editor: merge of the M1 engine

- **Write-only webhook URLs in the editor.** A `url` field whose value is the API's redacted form (`scheme://host/…` or `an invalid URL`, `isRedactedUrl` in `editor/model/schema-fields.ts`) reads like a saved secret: an empty input with "Saved (https://host/…); type to replace". The draft keeps the redacted value until something is typed, so an untouched URL is saved back unchanged and the server keeps the stored one; emptying the input again goes back to the saved value. The same applies to a URL-valued Run-node override.
- **Redacted values never travel to a new place.** The server restores a redacted value only for the node it was read from, so a duplicated node drops its redacted URLs (top-level settings and Run-node `overrides`; a duplicated notify node then needs a setting), and turning on an override whose sub-workflow default is redacted starts empty instead of copying the default.
- **Hints in parentheses.** A field description splits into label and hint at the first "; " or " — " outside parentheses, and a trailing parenthetical that reads as a sentence (contains "; " or ": ") becomes the hint, not the unit (the engine's `core:remux` `subtitleConversion` description).
