- **Sub-workflows and reviews compose.** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: no review flags exist any more.)* The engine never flags a sub-workflow in the upgrade pass (I4) and the editor shows no mode or review banner for one; the editor keeps hiding a review on a sub-workflow defensively, since `workflows.save` can still set `needsReview` on any kind (a Run-node pin change inside a sub-workflow).

## M2 Task 21: event stream

- **RPC event iterator, no SSE fallback.** The browser reads `events.subscribe` through the existing oRPC `RPCLink` (`POST /rpc/events/subscribe`). Verified against the real server through the Vite proxy: the `queue.changed` snapshot arrived 5 ms after subscribing and the first `ping` 30.01 s later; stopping the server makes the iterator throw (`TypeError: terminated`), which the client treats as a dropped stream. The `fetch('/api/v1/events')` fallback was not needed and is not built.
- **One stream per tab, above the routes.** `EventsProvider` wraps the route `Switch` in `App` and connects only while the session is `authenticated` (it closes on sign-out). It sits above `RequireAuth` rather than inside it because each route renders its own `RequireAuth`, so a provider there would reconnect on every navigation. Outside a provider the hooks see an inert stream (never live, no events) so component tests need no stream.
- **Reconnect and backoff.** Any end or error of the iterator (network, 401, server restart, our own abort) sets the status `reconnecting` and retries after 1 s, 2 s, 4 s … capped at 30 s; the delay resets to 1 s once a connection delivers its first message (the snapshot), which is also when the status turns `live`. Status starts `connecting`; `LiveDot` shows "LIVE" only when `live` and the muted "reconnecting…" otherwise (also while first connecting).
- **Dead-stream rule.** 45 s without any message (event or ping; the server pings every 30 s) aborts the request and reconnects. The timer starts at the subscribe call, so a hung connect counts too.
- **Missed events.** Reconnect listeners (`useOnReconnect`) fire once on every connect after the first, when its first message arrives, and on every `resync` event (the server dropped events for a slow reader; the contract says refetch). `useRefetchOn` reloads on both.
- **Coalesced refetch.** `useRefetchOn(filter, reload)` reloads at most once per 1000 ms per hook: the first trigger reloads at once, later triggers inside the window collapse into one trailing reload at its end; a pending trailing reload is cancelled on unmount.
- **Apply in place for `job.changed`.** Views that hold job rows (Jobs page, Task 22) patch a listed job from `job.changed` (state, paused, progress, lane, currentNode) without a refetch; a `job.changed` for a job not on the current page, `run.changed` and anything that changes counts go through `useRefetchOn` instead (Review Focus 5). `queue.changed` carries `paused` and `lanes` only (no `preparing`), so the queue bar applies it in place and refetches `queue.status` for `preparing`.
- **Byte formatting.** `fmtBytes` uses SI units (1 kB = 1000 B), two decimals from 1 GB up and none below; a value whose rounding reaches 1000 moves to the next unit ("1 MB", not "1000 kB"). `fmtPercent` rounds to a whole percent with U+2212 for a decrease, "+" for growth, "0%" when it rounds to zero, and "—" when there is no original size.

## M2 Task 22: jobs page

- **One page from the top, no paging.** The list is `jobs.list({ libraryId?, state?, limit: 200 })` (active jobs first, the server's order) and every reload fetches from the top again; the `before` cursor is not used. With a state filter that leaves out some active states, a second `jobs.list` of the library's active states backs the "Paused" chip count (the paused flag is not in `counts` and only active jobs carry it).
- **In place vs reload.** A `job.changed` for a job on the page patches state, paused, progress and lane and moves its `counts` entry; it reloads (coalesced ≤ 1/s, `useRefetchOn`) when the job reaches a final state or leaves the current state filter, when an active job off the page enters it, and when a job not on the page changes state (a tracker per job id, so progress ticks of an unseen job do not reload every second). A job of another library is ignored while a library is selected. `run.changed` reloads; reconnect and `resync` reload. Deviation from the brief: `queue.changed` does not reload the list (it moves no job between states; the job.changed events of a lane change carry them); the queue bar applies it in place and refetches `queue.status` on reconnect. `preparing` is not shown.
- **Nav count.** `useJobsCounts()` calls `jobs.list({ limit: 1 })` (the engine's minimum is 1, not 0) and reads only `counts`; it refetches on `run.changed` and on a job state change. The Jobs nav item shows the running states' sum as a pill (hidden at 0); its accessible name is "Jobs, 2 running".
- **Copy.** Detail lines per verdict as screen 07, with the byte figures from `fmtBytes` ("41.80 GB", two decimals from 1 GB, where the screen draws "41.8 GB"); a replace whose `replacedTo` is not `recycle` says "original replaced", never the recycle folder. Engine reasons start lower case and are printed sentence-cased; a missing reason prints the suffix alone ("Original untouched", "File untouched", "Plan ready"). ETA: "ETA 40 s", "ETA 3 min", "ETA 1 h 23 min". Undrawn: queued "Waiting for a lane" (paused: "Paused"), cancelled "Cancelled · original untouched", empty states "No jobs yet. Queued files show up here." / "No jobs match this filter.", load error "Couldn't load jobs" with "Try again", control errors "Couldn't pause/resume/cancel <file>". Cancelled and Already processed chips use the noop colours with an x-circle / check-circle icon. Failed rows keep their reason in the list; the log is on the job page (Task 23) behind "Details".
- **Controls.** Pause/Resume and Cancel on every active row (queued too); Cancel asks first ("Cancel <file>?" / "The original stays untouched." / "Cancel job" / "Keep running", focus on "Keep running"). `JOB_NOT_ACTIVE` → info toast "That job already finished" and a reload; every control reloads after it answers so the row is right even while the stream reconnects. Whole-queue "Pause queue" / "Resume queue" with the banner "Queue paused — running jobs are frozen, nothing new starts" and "Encode lane: <file> · Quick lane: idle" (a lane job not on the page shows "busy").
- **Phone (< 640 px, screen 08).** `matchMedia('(max-width: 639px)')` switches to the phone layout: own header (mark, "Jobs", LIVE, Menu), tiles SAVED TODAY / QUEUED / FAILED from `jobs.stats` (since local midnight), the queue bar, RUNNING cards (percent, bar, "Encoding · 118 fps · ETA 3 min", full-width Pause/Resume and Cancel) and FINISHED links (verdict icon tile, `${VERDICT_WORD} · ${short reason}`: the reason's first clause in lower case, "saved 790 MB" for a replace, "original untouched" for failed and cancelled). Queued jobs are only counted. The phone view ignores the desktop filters (all libraries, all states). The shell's top bar and nav are hidden by CSS (`.shell:has(.jobs-page.is-phone)`) and the Menu button shows them above the page (no Shell change).
- **Device profile names (gate item 4).** `EnumListField` labels the chips of a setting keyed `targets` with `system.capabilities().deviceProfiles` names (fetched once per page load, shared; an unknown id or a failed fetch shows the id); other enum lists keep `humanise`.

## M2 Task 26: Libraries page

1. The recycle folder has two states: default (null = `<root>/.transcoda-recycle`) or a custom path inside the root. "Off" can't be expressed: recycling cannot be switched off in M1.
2. A LIBRARY_PATH_INVALID whose reason mentions the recycle folder goes under the recycle input, not the Path field, because that's the field at fault. On edit, every LIBRARY_PATH_INVALID goes there, since the path can't change. Other reasons go under Path, as "This path cannot be used: <reason>".
3. On edit the Path is shown but disabled: the contract has no path update.
4. Update sends every editable field (name, recycleFolder, processHardlinked, verifyFullDecode, defaultWorkflowId), not a diff. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
5. The client requires an absolute path (it starts with /) before asking the server. Without this, the server would resolve a relative path against its own working directory.
6. verifyFullDecode is in the form, not in the table, to keep the table minimal.
7. The default-workflow select lists every kind 'workflow' (enabled and dry run *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: there are no modes; it lists every workflow)*), by name only.
8. The page head uses `page-head page-head-row` like WorkflowsPage (the brief says "page bar").

## M2 Task 25: run summary

1. Outcome icons reuse the existing set: done check, kept_original undo (screen 07's back-arrow), nothing_to_do bypass, failed cross, cancelled x-circle, dry_run eye, skipped chevron-right, already_processed check-circle, in_progress clock.
2. Tile order follows the design's run-summary list, not `RUN_OUTCOME_KEYS` order.
3. Per-file size shows "before → after" only for Replaced; other outcomes show the original's size (the file is unchanged), since kept_original jobs carry the trial output's afterBytes.
4. Reason column appends the original's fate ("original in recycle folder" / "original deleted" / "original untouched" for failed and kept_original).
5. Bytes line is shown only when `done > 0`; otherwise "No file replaced yet." / "No file replaced.".
6. A failed refetch keeps the last loaded run visible with an alert.
7. Every job row on the Jobs page (desktop) links to its run summary with " · run" after the library and workflow line (`/runs/:runId`, accessible name "Run summary for <file>"), also for a run of one file: a job does not say how many jobs its run has.

## M2 Task 24: dry-run plan, file picker, try on a file

1. The library line is "files in <libraryName>" with no file count (the engine has none). It comes from `job.libraryName`, so `libraries.get` is not needed.
2. Subtitles use the same table as audio ("Subtitles likewise" in the brief), not screen 06's card grid. The "· mov_text → copied as is" suffix is left out.
3. Track numbers count within the stream's type, worked out from the probe (as in ffmpeg `0:a:2` and the screen). `trackLabel` takes `(stream, probe)`.
4. Rows vs the drop line: dropped languages get a row only when they lose the default flag; the rest fold into the `<details>` line ("Drop N more …", "more" always).
5. *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: PlanView takes no `mode`; it offers "Activate vN of …" for the job's own version.)* The PlanView `workflow` prop also takes `mode` (to hide Enable); `result` may be null. PlanView renders only for dry-run jobs with a plan (the eyebrow would be false on a real job).
6. The header button reads "Save, then try" while dirty. The picker has no separate save button.
7. FilePicker `onPick` resolves to a refusal reason (string) or null, so PATH_REJECTED can be shown inline.
8. `useTryOnFile.ts` stays `.ts` (uses createElement).
9. A pick whose `jobs.queue` answer holds more than one job (a folder) opens the run summary `/runs/:runId`; one job opens `/jobs/:id` (with `?node=<id>` when a node asked); none keeps the picker open with "No media file to try there".
10. `JobPage` is a stand-in until Task 23's job page replaces it (keeping one `/jobs/:id` route and adding `<JobPlanSection>` there).

## M2 Task 23: job page

1. **Trace API follows the engine contract, not the brief names.** `buildTraceOverlay(graph,
   source: { trace, steps: JobDetail.path, jobState, currentNode }, workflowPath)`; the taken path
   comes from the walk, the current node from `job.changed.currentNode` (JobDetail has none; until
   the first event the last traced node stands in).
2. **TraceCanvas props:** `source` instead of `trace`/`jobState`; `selected: NodeRef | null` and
   `onSelect(ref)` instead of `selectedNodeId`; `expanded: string[]` (chain of opened Run nodes)
   and `onExpand(chain)` instead of `expandedRun`/`onExpandRun`; `subgraphs` keyed by `pathKey`
   ("run-a", "run-a/run-x"); optional `workflowPath`, `activity`. Reason: nested sub-workflows and
   decisions inside them need full node references.
3. **`decisions(…, children)` keys children by `pathKey`** (equals the Run node id at one level).
4. **A `failed` trace status** (red ring + "failed: …" row + ", failed" in the name) — the brief's
   four statuses cannot show where a job failed.
5. **Sub-workflow graphs are fetched eagerly** (once per entered Run node) rather than only on
   expand, so decisions inside sub-workflows get node titles; the expanded canvas reuses them.
6. **Decisions list includes data entries** (screen 06 lists "Original language") and leaves out
   sub-workflow Start/End.
7. **Only Run nodes the file entered get "Open"** (an untaken Run node has nothing to trace).
8. **Refetch filter:** `job.trace` for this job, or `job.changed` for this job whose state or paused
   flag differs from the shown one; progress/lane/currentNode-only changes are applied in place (no
   refetch per progress tick).
9. **Trace canvas fit at 50–100 % zoom, fixed 520 px height (child 420 px), wheel scrolls the page**
   (zoom via controls/pinch).
10. **Log** `<details>` loads on first open; "Reload log" while the job is active.
11. **Result:** sizes only when both are known (the engine gives no estimate for a copy-only dry
    run, so that dry run shows just "Dry run").
12. **Merged with Tasks 22 and 24.** The job page's words come from Task 22's `jobs/jobText.ts` (`detailLine`, `percentOf`, `PHASE_WORD`, `whereLine`; `stateWord` now takes `Pick<Job, 'state'>`) and its chip is `JobStateChip` (with the "Paused" chip); `JobResult` uses the same `VERDICT_ICON`. Task 23's own `jobWords.ts`, local chip and `.job-chip.v-*` rules are gone, and its header classes are `job-head-where`, `job-head-chips`, `job-progress-detail`, `job-progress-actions` so they do not restyle the jobs list. Task 24's stand-in job page is replaced: `<JobPlanSection>` sits between the header and the trace and decisions, the header's DRY RUN badge shows only when there is no plan (the plan carries it), and `?node=<id>` selects that top-level node on the canvas and in the decisions, and `DecisionsList`'s `reveal` scrolls its item into view once it is listed.

## M2 Phase B: jobs

Summary of Phase B (Tasks 21–26) and what the Task 27 gate run added. The task sections above hold the detail.

- **Event stream (Task 21):** one `events.subscribe` oRPC iterator per tab, above the routes, open only while signed in; no SSE fallback. Any end or error reconnects after 1 s, doubling to 30 s, reset once a connection delivers its first message; 45 s without a message (the server pings every 30 s) counts as dead. Reconnects and `resync` fire reconnect listeners once, and `useRefetchOn` reloads at most once per second per hook (first trigger at once, one trailing reload).
- **In place vs refetch:** `job.changed` patches a listed row (state, paused, progress, lane, current node) without a request; a job not on the page, a final state, a filter change, `run.changed`, reconnect and `resync` go through the coalesced refetch. The job page refetches only when its job's state or paused flag changes; progress ticks are applied in place.
- **Verdict and detail copy (Task 22, `jobs/jobText.ts`):**

  | State | Chip | Detail line |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | done | Replaced | "2.41 GB → 1.62 GB, saved 790 MB · original in recycle folder" ("original replaced" when not recycled) |
  | kept_original | Kept original | "<Reason> · file untouched" |
  | failed | Failed | "<Reason> · original untouched" |
  | nothing_to_do | Nothing to do | "Already complies with every rule" |
  | already_processed | Already processed | "Processed on <date>: <earlier verdict>" (Task 27, below) |
  | skipped | Skipped | "<Reason>" |
  | dry_run | Dry run | "Plan ready[: <reason>]" |
  | cancelled | Cancelled | "Cancelled · original untouched" |
  | queued | Queued | "Waiting for a lane" / "Paused" |
  | running states | phase word | "42% · 118 fps · ETA 3 min" or the phase's line |

- **Phone breakpoint:** the Jobs page alone has a phone layout below 640 px (`matchMedia('(max-width: 639px)')`, screen 08); the editor stays desktop-only (read-only below 1024 px).
- **File picker (Task 24):** "Try on a file" opens a picker that browses a library (`libraries.browse`) and also takes a typed absolute path; refusals (`PATH_REJECTED`) show inline. It always queues a dry run. A folder pick opens the run summary, a file pick the job page.
- **"Scan now" on the library row (Task 27):** the real run starts from `/libraries`: each row has "Scan now", which calls `jobs.queue({ path: library.path, dryRun: false })` with the library's default workflow and opens `/runs/<runId>`. Refusals are error toasts: `NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW` "<Library> has no default workflow. Edit the library to pick one.", `WORKFLOW_INVALID` "The default workflow of <Library> has errors. Fix it in the editor first.", `PATH_REJECTED` and anything else "Couldn't scan <Library>: <reason>". The buttons are disabled while one scan is being queued. *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: the active version decides, not the workflow mode.)* A workflow still in dry run makes the engine run the scan as a dry run (workflow mode wins). *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
- **libraryLine without a file count:** the plan and the enable dialog say "files in <library>" with no number; the engine does not count a library's files.
- **`data/languages.ts` vs the shared table:** the web keeps its own picker table (ISO 639-1 codes with English names, `unknown` for untagged) so `apps/web` does not import the engine's language rules; the engine's `LANGUAGES` in `@transcoda/shared` normalises 639-1/639-2 codes. The two lists are not kept in sync automatically.

### Found and fixed in the gate run (Task 27, with tests)

- **Decisions out of walk order.** The engine traces the decide phase (conditions, Run nodes) before the build phase (actions), so on the else branch "Run sub-workflow" was listed before "Encode video" and the sub-workflow's actions read as part of the encode. `decisions()` takes the walk (`JobDetail.path`) and orders entries by attempt, then their node's first position on the walk, then `seq`; an entry off the walk stays after the one before it.
- **"Already nothing_to_do on 2026-10-17T04:18:10.386Z".** The queue's already-processed reason is printed as "processed on <date>: <earlier verdict>" (`reasonOf` in `jobText.ts`), on the Jobs page, the phone list, the run summary and the job page. Other reasons pass through unchanged.
- **Nothing-to-do rows said "—".** The engine gives nothing_to_do no reason; the run summary and the job page now say "already complies with every rule", like the Jobs page.
- **"SIZE (EST.) 379 kB" with no estimate.** A finished rewrite whose `estimatedBytes` is null showed the original size alone as if it were the estimate; the tile now adds "no estimate yet".
- **Unexplained default flag.** With "Set default track" subtitles empty the mp4 output still marks the English subtitle default (the engine's `normalizeDefaults`: "mp4 cannot have no default subtitle"); the plan showed "no → yes" with no reason. The DEFAULT cell now prints the engine's default-rule note (only the three `normalizeDefaults` phrases; plain notes such as "default" or a language code stay out).
- **Recycle retention in the enable dialog.** Screen 06's "kept for 14 days" is wrong for the engine, which keeps recycled originals 7 days by default (`recycle.retentionDays`) and removes them sooner when the disk runs short. The dialog now says "is kept there for up to 7 days" (deviation from screen 06).

### Found and left for later

- **Server: `JobDetail.probe` is the file's latest probe** (`files.last_probe`), not the job's. After a file is replaced, its older dry run's plan shows the new file's track numbers ("a:3" instead of "a:5", "s:21" fallbacks). The job needs its own probe snapshot (engine track).
- **Engine: "Set default track" says "empty means no default subtitle"** but an mp4 always gets one (see above); the node description or the inspector should say so for mp4.
- **Engine reasons use three-letter codes** ("kept eng, deu, tur") while the settings use two-letter ones (en, de, tr).
- **The job page of a queued job says "Waiting for a lane" while the whole queue is paused**; only the Jobs page shows the queue banner.
- **The highlighted node while encoding is the last traced node** (the Run sub-workflow on the else branch), not "Encode video": the engine's `currentNode` follows the walk, and the encode runs after it.
- **Trace canvas clips wide graphs** at its 50 % minimum fit zoom (the trigger card is cut at the left edge).
- **"Inside Run sub-workflow"** heads the expanded child canvas with the node's title; the sub-workflow's name ("Inside Language cleanup") would read better when the Run node has no label.
- **The "c" connect search does not offer named sub-workflows** (the palette's Sub-workflows section does), so a Run node added that way needs its sub-workflow picked in the inspector.
- **Edge accessible names use node ids** ("Edge from NgsLkEM5dz to AczfBl4_R0") instead of node titles.
- **The Run inspector's sub-workflow select** is announced by its placeholder ("Choose a sub-workflow") rather than its "Sub-workflow" label.
- **A sub-workflow's editor banner says "Can't enable: …"** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: banner wording says activated, not enabled.)* although sub-workflows have no mode.
- **Already-processed rows have no size** in the run summary (`beforeBytes` null for jobs that never ran).
- **Fresh copies are "Skipped · still growing"** until the settle window passes (engine rule, expected; worth a hint in the copy-run guide).
- Browser automation only (not app bugs): shift-click multi-select needed a synthetic Shift keydown; a REST `POST` from the page console was refused (403) by the same-origin check.

## M2 gate

- **Date:** 2026-10-17. **Fixture:** `tigger-pooh.mp4` from `pnpm fixtures` (HEVC 180p, 20 AAC tracks, 21 mov_text subtitles with a Turkish default, one untagged subtitle, PNG cover; 379,411 bytes), copied into a temporary library folder; `h264-big.mkv` added later for the else branch and the live view.
- **Outcome: passed.** From an empty instance, in the UI only: library created, sub-workflow and workflow built in the editor, dry run planned (nothing on disk changed), enabled through the dialog, a real "Scan now" replaced the file (379 kB → 133 kB, original in `.transcoda-recycle`, run summary 1 queued · 1 accounted for, Replaced 1), and a second scan ended "Nothing to do" (a third "Already processed"). The full run is in [`docs/m2-gate.md`](m2-gate.md).

## M2 fix round (Phase B review + M2 gate findings)

- **"Scan now" asks first.** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: dialog speaks of active versions, not modes.)* The Libraries row's "Scan now" opens "Scan <library> now?" naming the default workflow, the root and the mode: enabled "It is enabled: it rewrites files that need changes."; dry-run "Dry run: it plans each file and changes nothing on disk."; unknown mode (workflow list failed) "If it is enabled, it rewrites files that need changes." Focus starts on Cancel. A library without a default workflow skips the dialog (nothing to confirm; the server's NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW is shown). The scan itself is unchanged: a real run (`dryRun: false`) of the root; a workflow in dry-run mode still makes it a dry run (server rule). *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
- **Job page keyed by id.** `/jobs/:id` renders `JobView key={id}`: moving to another job drops live fields, selection, expanded sub-workflows, sub-graphs and the log.
- **Job log.** Read on first open; "Reload log" always once read, "Try again" after a failed read; read again automatically when the job turns final while the log is open or was read.
- **Transient errors keep data.** `useAsync` keeps the last data when a `reload()` fails (same deps) and drops it when a call for new deps fails. The job page then shows "Could not refresh: … — showing the last state." above the last detail (as the run summary does).
- **Event stream.** The first connect fires the reconnect listeners too (a view fetched before the stream was up may have missed events), so every page refetches once when the stream comes up; listeners registered after it is live are not called. Every listener call is wrapped: one that throws is logged (`console.error`) and the others still run; the stream stays up.
- **Queue state.** `useQueue()` (queue.status + `queue.changed`) drops its live snapshot on reconnect/resync and refetches, so the server's answer wins. The Jobs page shares it between the queue bar and the rows; a queued job reads "Queue paused" (not "Waiting for a lane") while the whole queue is paused, on the Jobs page, the phone list and the job page.
- **Lit node while ffmpeg runs.** The engine's `currentNode` during encoding/verifying/replacing is the last *built* node (on the else branch the Run sub-workflow behind Encode video; null while replacing). The job page lights the walk's encoding node instead: Encode video, else Encode audio, else Remux (last of that type on the walk, not bypassed); without one, the engine's node. The header has "At: <node>" ("At: Keep languages in Language cleanup" inside a sub-workflow). A paused job's node stays lit without the moving border and reads "paused"/"Paused".
- **Names.** The expanded child canvas is "Inside <Run label | sub-workflow name | type title>"; decisions inside a sub-workflow read "<title> in <same> · <reason>"; edges are named "Edge from <title>[, <port label>,] to <title>" (the port only when the source has several outputs; a collapsed group by its name).
- **Trace canvas fit.** Fitted once the cards are measured and the pane has a size, at 100 % max and 40 % min (the compact level: names stay readable); a workflow still wider is shown from its left edge (the trigger) instead of cut on both sides, and panned. On a phone (< 640 px) the canvas neither pans nor pinch-zooms (`touch-action: pan-y`), so a drag scrolls the page; the controls zoom and "fit" shows the whole graph.
- **Sub-workflow fetches.** NOT_FOUND is final ("can no longer be read"); any other failure is asked again on the job's next update.
- **"c" connect search** lists the named sub-workflows (not the one being edited) after the node types whenever a Run node fits; picking one adds a Run node pinned to its current version, connected like any other pick.
- **Sub-workflow banner.** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: banner wording says activated, not enabled.)* A sub-workflow has no mode: its error banner reads "Needs a fix: <error>. Workflows that run this version can't be enabled." (workflows keep "Can't enable: … Dry runs still work.").
- **Run inspector select.** Verified in the browser: the select's `labels` is "Sub-workflow" (`<label for>`); the automation tool lists comboboxes by their value (the Group select shows the same), so nothing changed.
- **Nav pill.** Verified: the shell (and its version and job-count fetches) stays mounted across navigation (wouter's Switch keeps the same Route/Shell instance); a test guards it.
- **Small copy/a11y.** Dry runs: "ffmpeg command (not run)". Per-row controls named per file ("Pause <file>", "Cancel <file>", "Details for <file>"). Filter chips 44 px; the run link and crumb links have 44 px targets. Queued jobs are listed on a phone (QUEUED, with Pause/Cancel). Job cancel dialog focuses "Keep running". The file picker shows a refusal of a file picked from the list under the list ("Can't use <file>: <reason>"), a typed path's under the input. The run summary says "The list shows N of M files." when the file list and the total differ. The dry-run plan section (and its workflow fetch) mounts only for a dry run with a plan.
- **Server (engine track, to port to m1-engine):**
  - Migration 4 `0004_job_probe`: `jobs.probe` (JSON). The pipeline stores the probe with the job when it probes (each attempt overwrites); `jobDetail.probe` is the job's own probe (null for jobs from before the migration), never `files.last_probe`.
  - Already-processed jobs record their file (`upsertFile`, probe kept) and the run summary shows the file's recorded size as `beforeBytes` for them (`afterBytes` stays null; run totals still sum results only).
  - Reasons print languages as the settings spell them: two-letter when the language has one (`eng`/`ger` → `en`/`de`), else the three-letter code; untagged `und` (`reasonTag`). Track titles written into files are unchanged.
  - "Set default track" subtitle setting: "…; empty means no default subtitle (an mp4 output still flags its first subtitle: the mp4 muxer always marks one)".

## M2 final fix wave

- **Browser Back/Forward asks too (I1).** While the draft is dirty, `useLeaveGuard` listens to `popstate`: it puts the editor's URL back (`history.pushState`, a new entry) and holds the route Back/Forward went to, so "Leave without saving?" opens as for an in-app link. "Leave" and "Save and leave" then navigate to that route (a new history entry, not a history step). This replaces the Phase A limitation "wouter has no history blocker; the back button is covered only by `beforeunload`".
- **No automatic note on "Save over it" (I2).** A save with a `baseVersion` override (saving over someone else's version) sends only the user's own note; "Layout only" compared against the version the draft was loaded from would describe the wrong change. "Restored from vN" on a restore is unchanged.
- **A sub-workflow has no mode on the server either (I3).** *(Superseded by Activation, 2026-10-17: setMode removed; activate refuses sub-workflows.)* `workflows.setMode` on a sub-workflow is refused (`KIND_MISMATCH`, 400) for both modes; the contract lists `KIND_MISMATCH` on setMode.
- **Redacted webhook URLs never stored as a literal (I4).** A notify `url` or URL-valued Run-node override sent back in its redacted form (`scheme://host/…`, "an invalid URL") is the stored value of the same node (id and type) and key whose redacted form it equals, looked up in `restoredFrom` (new optional save input: the past version a restore comes from), then `baseVersion`, then every other version newest first (read lazily, unreadable versions skipped). A redacted value that matches nothing refuses the save with the new `HIDDEN_URL_UNKNOWN` (400, `data.nodeIds`) instead of storing the literal; an unknown workflow is still 404. "Restore as new version" sends `restoredFrom`. The editor shows the refusal inline: a danger banner on the canvas "A hidden webhook URL could not be matched to a saved one. Enter the URL again in <node>." with a "Show <node>" button per node (inside the conflict dialog for "Save over it", inside the leave dialog for "Save and leave"); the draft stays dirty; the next successful save clears it.
- **Past versions have no Save button**; the conflict dialog for a restore reads "Someone saved vN since you opened vM. Restore vM on top of it as a new version, or open the latest version." with "Open the latest version" / "Restore over it".
- **One versions list.** `useVersionList` (in `VersionsPanel.tsx`) fetches `workflows.versions` once per shown/current version for both the pill's time and the panel; opening the panel reuses it (asks again only after a failed load; "Try again" in the panel reloads).
- **"Save, then try" waits for a running save** and goes on when that save stored the draft as it is now (instead of closing the picker silently).
- **"Scan now" re-checks a library listed without a default workflow** (`libraries.get`): one set since the list loaded (another tab) opens the confirmation like any other; still none → the server's NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW as before; a failed check asks first. Supersedes "a library without a default workflow skips the dialog" above. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*
- **Small fixes.** Toast regions `aria-atomic="false"`; the phone Jobs "Menu" button has `aria-controls` (the shell's main nav, id `main-nav`); after a successful workflow delete focus moves to the list heading; NodeSearch ignores Enter/arrows during an IME composition; the Run inspector shows "Loading settings…" while another pin loads (answers are tagged with their `<subworkflowId>@<version>`); the multi-select inspector offers "Group N nodes" next to "Delete N nodes"; the job page's plan says "Could not check whether <workflow> is enabled: … — Try again" when the workflow fetch fails, and "Enable …" appears once it loads; `fromEdgeChanges` takes the flow edges as a required argument; the `IssueCode` casts and the unused `ModeBadge` (and its `.badge-enabled` style) are gone. The mode-switch radios already had the 44 px `::before` hit area (`inset: -3px 0` on 38 px); nothing changed there.
- **Tests.** The 1 s refetch coalescing and the 400 ms validation debounce are advanced with fake timers (`shouldAdvanceTime`, `advanceTimersByTimeAsync`) instead of 2–2.5 s real waits and a 1 s sleep; two 20 ms sleeps that waited for nothing became a promise flush.

## Activation

Per-version activation of workflows (design "Transcoda — per-version activation of workflows", approved by Jan 2026-10-17). Every save only adds a version; a person activates one; real runs use that version only. The mode switch, `needsReview`, `reviewNote` and `workflow_review_acks` are gone. Rulings the earlier sections made about them carry a "Superseded by Activation" marker.

### Jan's decisions (binding)

- **Activate before it runs.** A new version never runs for real until a person activates it; an older active version keeps running meanwhile.
- **Dry runs** use the newest version by default; a version can be picked (`jobs.queue` `workflowVersion`, MCP `dry_run` `version`). A real request with `workflowVersion` is a 400.
- **Activation is a human UI action**, inheriting the MCP milestone decision. No token scope, no MCP tool.
- **Queued real jobs follow the activation.** A real job that has not started runs on the version active when it starts; running jobs finish on theirs; dry-run jobs keep the version they were queued with. Nothing active at start: the job is skipped.
- **Scan now on a blocked active version refuses** and offers "Dry run vN instead".
- **Deactivating is person-only** too.
- **No acknowledgement checkbox** in the Activate dialog; the change summary and the button are enough.

### Model and migration

- **`active_version` on the `workflows` row** (nullable) with `activated_at`/`activated_by`; the three move together (CHECK). A composite foreign key `(id, active_version)` references `workflow_versions(workflow_id, version)`; a CHECK keeps `active_version` NULL for sub-workflows (kind CHECK). `mode`, `needs_review`, `review_note` are dropped. `current_version` stays the newest version.
- **Only `setActiveVersion`/`clearActiveVersion` (repo) and migration 0005 write the three columns.** `appendVersion` has no flags parameter. `save`, `create`, `duplicate`, `restore`, `jobs.queue` and `upgradeAll` never write `active_version`.
- **Migration 0005 is frozen SQL** (no application constants): rebuilds `workflows`, adds `runs.note`, deletes the `workflow_review_acks` setting. Enabled, unflagged workflows with a live current version row become active on that version (`activated_by` `system:migration`, `activated_at` the old `updated_at`); dry-run, flagged, sub-workflow and missing-version-row workflows become not active (the workflow and its versions are kept).
- **`migrate()` takes `foreignKeys: 'off'` per migration.** The table rebuild runs with the pragma off (it must be set outside a transaction); with it on, the `DROP` either fails or cascade-deletes every version. Inside the transaction `foreign_key_check` must be empty, else the migration throws and rolls back; the pragma is restored afterwards.
- **A hard `DELETE` of an active workflow row is not refused by the foreign key** (the child row is the one deleted, so the versions cascade); only deleting the active version row is refused. Production only soft-deletes, as before.
- **`setActiveVersion`** checks the workflow (missing or deleted: `false`), then the version (missing: `NotFoundError`) inside an immediate transaction, so a concurrent version delete is a not-found, not a foreign-key error.
- **`updated_at` is untouched** by activate and deactivate.
- **Upgrade day (decision 4 applied to in-flight jobs):** jobs M2 stored as dry runs (a real request on a dry-run-mode or flagged workflow became a dry run at queue time, `dry_run = 1`) keep running dry on their pinned version. The jobs that end `skipped: workflow is not active` are real (`dry_run = 0`) jobs queued while the workflow was enabled and unflagged, whose workflow was switched to dry run or flagged before they started (M2 ran them as effective dry runs); after the migration such a workflow is not active.
- **Known issue (follow-up):** backup files are stamped per second, so a failed migration followed by a restart within the same second fails on `VACUUM INTO` ("output file already exists") instead of on the migration. *(Fixed 2026-10-21: a taken name gets `-2`, `-3`, …; see Backup on build change.)*
- **No activation history**: only `activated_at`/`activated_by` of the current activation.

### Service and API

- **Needs review is derived**: `active !== null && !active.runnable`, where `runnable` is `loadForRun` ok on the active version. One extra validation per active workflow in `list()`/`get()` (shared cache for pinned children; no reuse when active equals current). `activeIssues` on `get()` carries the load errors. The pointer alone decides "active" (null `activated_at`/`activated_by` fall back defensively to `updated_at`/`system:migration`).
- **`setMode` is removed, not deprecated.** `workflows.activate` (`confirm: true` literal) and `workflows.deactivate` replace it; activating an older version is the rollback.
- **`activate` runs `loadForRun`** (the real-run check): errors give `WORKFLOW_INVALID` (422) with the issues. Re-activating the already active version is an `ok` no-op that leaves `activatedAt`/`By` untouched when it runs; when the active version cannot run it is 422, not a no-op. A missing version is `NOT_FOUND` ("Workflow version not found"); a sub-workflow is `KIND_MISMATCH`.
- **`deactivate` is ok when nothing was active.**
- **Session-only (`requireHumanSession`)**: no auth 401; any API token, whatever its scopes, 403 `FORBIDDEN` with message `Activation is a UI action`; a session needs the CSRF token. A valid bearer token plus a valid cookie on the same request is `FORBIDDEN` (the bearer is authoritative); an invalid bearer is 401. No new scopes, no new error codes.
- **`WORKFLOW_INVALID` also means** "the version to run cannot run" on `jobs.queue` and "the version cannot be activated" on `workflows.activate`.
- **Save output** carries `active` (unchanged by the save, echoed for "Saved v6 · v3 stays active") and `changes` (the old review-note texts, informational, not stored). `UpgradeSummary` counts `behaviour` instead of `flagged`.
- **"In use" counts active versions**: `usedBy` is the union of current-version and active-version users; `delete()` refuses a sub-workflow pinned by any live parent's active version, and also counts a parent whose active row is unreadable but mentions the id. Deleting the active workflow itself stays allowed (soft delete); real and dry queues then give `workflow-missing`.
- **Restore upgrades** the restored graph (safe and behaviour migrations, notes in the version note; `restoredFrom` on save). A migration that throws or yields an invalid graph is stored as sent, with `Could not upgrade: migration failed` / `invalid migration output` in `changes` (a throw is logged as a warning).
- **`upgradeAll`** upgrades each workflow's newest version only, never touches `active_version`, and writes nothing for an older active version. `flagStaleParents` and the acks setting are deleted.
- **Contract**: `JobsQueueInput` refines `workflowVersion` to dry runs only, so it is no longer a plain object (`.shape` is unavailable); `jobs.queue` output is `{ runId, jobIds, dryRun, note, workflowVersion }` from the run row; `RunSchema.note`; `RunDetail.files[].workflowVersion`.
- **MCP**: workflow briefs carry `active {version, runnable}` (no mode/needsReview/reviewNote); `get_workflow` prints "Active: vN …" and "Active version can't run: …"; no tool wraps `activate`/`deactivate`. `dry_run` takes an optional `version` (always sends `dryRun: true`, forwards `workflowVersion` only when given) and reports the queued `version`; an unknown version is `NOT_FOUND`. `docs/mcp.md` says activation is human-only.

### Engine

- **Queue resolution** (`resolveWorkflow`): a real request pins the active version; with nothing active it runs as a dry run of the newest version with run note `no active version`; with a blocked active version it is `workflow-invalid`, never a silent dry run. A dry request pins the chosen version or the newest. A version of another workflow, or an unknown number, is `version-missing` (REST: `NOT_FOUND`); a sub-workflow id is `workflow-missing`. Resolution runs again inside the insert transaction (the early call stays as the fast refusal), so an activation during a folder listing is honoured.
- **Real jobs re-pin at start** (`runPrepare`): the job's `workflow_version` is updated to the version active then (publishing `job.changed`) and the walk runs on it. Nothing active: `skipped` with reason `workflow is not active` (a skip at the engine's gate, like `still growing`, not a failure). A retried or crash-requeued job re-pins at its next start the same way.
- **History re-check on re-pin**: when the version changed and the run is not forced, a file the new version already finished for real ends `already_processed`, and the `job_results` row carries the new version. `force` is honoured (no re-check). A `failed` result counts as final for the check.
- **Not re-pinned**: dry-run jobs keep their version; an activation during an encode does not move a running job.
- **The dry-run finish has `reason: null`**; the "workflow is in dry-run mode" reason is gone. The run row keeps its queue-time version and `note`; the run summary shows each job's own version (`files[].workflowVersion`).
- **`loadWorkflowForJob`** returns `ok`, `invalid`, `missing` or `not-active` (real job: active version, dry job: its pinned version).

### UI

- **Words**: `Activate`, `Active: v3`, `Not active`, `Roll back to v2`, `Needs review` (derived), `can't run`. "Enable", "enabled" and "dry-run mode" leave the UI, README and `docs/copy-run.md`; `docs/m2-gate.md` stays as a record.
- **Activate dialog** (`ActivateDialog`) has no checkbox. It always fetches the version list (needed for the version note when nothing is active) and the graphs only when another version is active; the change summary names nodes by label, then catalogue title, then type, computed in render so a late catalogue still names them; identical graphs list "No changes". The waiting-jobs lookup (`jobs.list`) is a lower bound past 200 jobs ("At least N waiting jobs …"); when it fails the line is absent and activation is not blocked. Focus returns to the "Active" pill (`returnFocusTo`/`pillRef`). `WORKFLOW_INVALID` with no issues is the generic failure (primary stays usable). Deactivate has no confirmation.
- **`ActivationControl`** replaces the mode switch: a pill ("Active: v3", with an aria-label that adds "· can't run: <issue>"), Activate/Deactivate buttons; a blocked Activate explains why ("Save first", "Fix N errors before activating") through `aria-describedby`.
- **Editor**: the save toast reads "Saved vN · vM stays active"; the server's `changes` show once in an info banner ("This version: …", not live, cleared on apply/load). A can't-run danger banner outranks the draft-error banner and the pin notice, with an Activate button for another saved, error-free version. A past version's banner carries Activate / "Viewing vN — active"; its header control is read-only so the button is not duplicated. The versions panel marks the active row. Past versions can be tried on a file (`useTryOnFile` sends `workflowVersion`).
- **Workflows list**: an ACTIVE column; the chip is a button (`Activate vN of X (not active)` / `(active: v3)` / `(active: v3 · can't run)`) that opens the dialog for the newest version only when it is valid and not already active, and reloads the list after. Needs review shows the can't-run reason as screen-reader text. New workflows: "New workflows never touch files until you activate a version."
- **Scan now's four texts** (active and runnable, not active, active but can't run, list failed). On a blocked active version the primary is disabled and "Dry run vN instead" queues a dry run of the newest and opens the run; the reason comes from `workflows.get().activeIssues` (fallback "it has errors"; a failed lookup never blocks). The `WORKFLOW_INVALID` toast differs for dry and real runs.
- **Job page and jobs list**: the dry-run plan offers "Activate vN of <workflow>…" for the job's own version while it is not active; a failed workflow load says "Could not check which version of <workflow> is active: …" with Try again. A queued real job's version says "runs on the version active when it starts" (accessible description; visible on phone cards). The run page shows "Dry run · no active version" for the noted dry run and a per-file `v<N>` ("ran on vN") when it differs from the run's.

## MCP server

Built 2026-10-17 on branch `mcp-server`: an MCP endpoint at `/mcp` through which an agent can diagnose jobs, read and change workflows, duplicate them and start dry runs. User docs are in [`mcp.md`](mcp.md).

### SDK and transport

- **SDK:** `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.32.0 (2026-10-02; 1.32.1 was too young on 2026-10-17), pinned exactly. `WebStandardStreamableHTTPServerTransport` on Hono, stateless (`sessionIdGenerator` omitted; `undefined` fails `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`), `enableJsonResponse: true`. One `McpServer` and one transport per POST (the SDK refuses to reuse a stateless transport); GET and DELETE are 405. The SDK itself answers two cases as resolved `isError` results with text only and no `structuredContent`: an unknown or hidden tool (`MCP error -32602: Tool <name> not found`) and arguments that fail a tool's zod shape (`Input validation error: …`); tests assert on resolved results. The lockfile grew by the SDK's transitive dependencies (express 5, cors, jose, ajv, …); no other direct dependency, `.npmrc`/`allowBuilds` untouched. Why not a hand-written JSON-RPC handler: the SDK already speaks the fetch API, zod 4 and structured content (spike test `sdk.test.ts`: zod 4 shapes of the big graph and node schemas convert to JSON Schema).
- **Client typing:** the SDK's `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` does not satisfy its own `Transport` interface under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`; `test-client.ts` casts it with a comment. The tool callback receives `args: unknown`, cast to a record after the SDK validated it.

### Auth and guards

- **Bearer API tokens only**, resolved per request through `TokenService.authenticate`; nothing is cached, so a revoked or expired token fails the next call. Cookies are never read on `/mcp`; a bearer plus a cookie uses the bearer's scopes. 401 carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="transcoda"`. `Origin`, when present, is checked with `checkOrigin` (403). The host check and the 2 MiB body limit (413, also for chunked bodies without a length) cover `/mcp`. HTTP-level refusals are JSON-RPC error bodies with `id: null`. `app.onError` logs the error name only for `/mcp`.

### Tools call the router

- `createRouterClient(router, { context })` with a token `ServerContext`: `requireScope` is what refuses, and validation, path checks, redaction and error codes are the REST ones. Tools add only input shapes, a short text and structured output. Tool visibility is scope (`toolsFor`): a hidden tool is absent from `tools/list` and answers "not found". 24 tools; scopes stay exact and do not imply each other (a writing token also needs `read`); a `jobs:create`-only token sees only `dry_run`, and `dry_run` accepts `plan` or `jobs:create`.
- **Annotations:** read tools `readOnlyHint`; `delete_workflow` and `cancel_job` `destructiveHint`; `pause_*`, `resume_*`, `rename_workflow` `idempotentHint`; every tool `openWorldHint: false`.

### Never

- No tool wraps `workflows.activate`/`deactivate`; no tool name matches `/enable|mode|activ|real/`. `jobs.queue` is only reached with a literal `dryRun: true` (a `dryRun: false` or `mode` argument passed to `dry_run` is ignored or rejected and the run is still a dry run). No library, token, auth or events tool. A duplicate of an active workflow has no active version. `never.test.ts` holds the tests (exact 24 names, hidden tools never run their handler, the active map unchanged after every write tool, no secret in any output, `tools/list` or log line, corrupt graph answers `INTERNAL`, stale base answers `VERSION_CONFLICT`); mutation-checked (leaking the token or the error text into a log, an extra `activate_workflow` tool, `toolsFor` returning everything, create activating v1 each fail a test).
- **B4 resolved by version activation** (see "Activation"): a save through MCP creates a new version and never changes which version runs for real; the previously activated version keeps running, and activation is a human, session-only action (API tokens get 403 `Activation is a UI action`). Tested through the real queue (an MCP save leaves the real job pinned to the active version; an unactivated workflow's real request becomes a dry run with note `no active version`).

### Contract additions (REST and MCP)

- `workflows.create` takes `graph` (same kind, else `KIND_MISMATCH`, checked before insert; a redacted URL in it that matches nothing is `HIDDEN_URL_UNKNOWN`) and `note` (`''` is no note); its output is redacted like `get`. `workflows.duplicate` (`POST /workflows/{id}/duplicate`) copies the current version under a new name, same kind, dropping webhook URLs (notify `url` becomes `''`, URL-valued Run-node overrides are removed; `droppedWebhookUrls` lists the node ids that lost one) and returns `sourceVersion`; the REST response is redacted like `get`, so a dropped notify URL shows as `an invalid URL` there while the stored graph holds `''`. `insertWorkflow` stores a version-1 note. `dropWebhookUrls` and the redaction predicates (`isSecretOverride`) live in `workflows/webhook-urls.ts`; a notify node whose `url` is not a string is left alone.
- **Webhook hint in tool descriptions:** `an invalid URL` or `scheme://host/…` is hidden or empty; to set a URL an agent sends a full real URL, never the placeholder (a create with the placeholder is `HIDDEN_URL_UNKNOWN`).

### Version notes

- A save, create or duplicate through MCP stores `via MCP` or `via MCP: <note>`; the tool's `note` is at most 111 characters so the stored note stays within the contract's 120.

### Agent output

- Text summary plus `structuredContent`. Errors are text-only `isError` results: `CODE: message`, a hint per code (omitted when the data field is absent) and a `Details: {…}` line with the data; there is no `structuredContent` on a failure, because the SDK client validates it against the tool's output schema even with `isError` set and turns the failure into a -32602 error (found in the final review; a regression test lists tools first, then fails eight codes on one client). `INTERNAL` carries a generic message, the detail is not in the result or the log. `dry_run` takes a strict input (unknown keys are an input-validation error naming the key); the server advertises `tools.listChanged: false`.
- `get_job` reports the failing node only when a trace entry records the failure (the walk marks it with `port: null` and reason `failed: <reason>`); an ffmpeg failure happens after the walk, writes no trace entry and reports no node, with text `Failed during the ffmpeg encode, not at a workflow node: <reason>`. The engine keeps no persisted "current node"; making it do so would be an engine change. `path` titles come from the pinned graph's labels, then the catalogue.
- `get_job_log` returns the last `maxBytes` (default 16384, 256 to 65536) characters of the API's 64 KiB tail; `maxBytes` counts characters, a cut never starts on a lone low surrogate; `bytes` is the UTF-8 length of what is returned; a job that has not started is `log: ''`, `truncated: false`.
- `list_jobs` / `list_runs` default `limit` 20 (max 200); `list_jobs` returns `nextCursor` (the last job id when a full page came back, else `null`). `pause_job`/`resume_job`/`cancel_job` return `{ ok: true, id }`.
- `list_nodes` returns briefs; with `type`, the one node with its settings schema. `validate_workflow` text is `Valid.` only when there are no issues, else `n error(s), m warning(s):` and one line per issue; `workflowId` only resolves sub-workflow references. `save_workflow` text names the version, validity and the active version (`Saved … as vN … vM stays active`), then the `changes`.
- `browse_library` returns absolute paths under the library's stored real path (so `dry_run` accepts them); an absolute `path` argument is `PATH_REJECTED`; text lists at most 200 entries, structured output all. `dry_run` of a folder without media files says nothing was queued; it takes an optional `version`, reports the queued version, and an unknown version is `NOT_FOUND`.
- Workflow briefs carry `active {version, runnable}` (no mode or review flags), as the activation work defined.

### Logging

- One `log.info` per tool call: `msg: 'mcp tool call'`, `tool`, `tokenId`, `ok`, and `code` on failure. Never arguments, results, headers or messages that could carry a URL. An unexpected throw adds one `mcp tool failed` line with the error name only.

### Not done

- Rate limiting beyond the API's, OAuth, MCP resources and prompts (instructions text only), SSE and resumable streams, an environment switch to turn `/mcp` off (it is as reachable as `/api/v1`), persisting the failing node of an ffmpeg failure for `get_job`.

### Smoke test (2026-10-17)

- **Step 1, scripted (done):** a throwaway instance (`TRANSCODA_CONFIG_DIR`/`TRANSCODA_TRANSCODE_DIR` in a temporary folder, `127.0.0.1`, an unused port), a test admin through `/auth/setup`, a token with scopes `read`, `workflows:write`, `plan` created over REST. The three curl calls of `mcp.md` gave: `initialize` protocol 2025-06-18, `serverInfo` `transcoda` 0.0.0-dev, capability `tools`, instructions text present; `tools/list` returned exactly the 19 tools those scopes unlock (all read tools, the five write tools, `dry_run`; no `pause_*`, `resume_*`, `cancel_job`); `queue_status` gave `structuredContent.paused: false`. `pause_queue` and `pause_job` with that token were `isError` "Tool … not found". The session cookie alone was 401, with `Host: nope.example` 403, with `Origin: http://evil.example` 403, GET 405, no bearer 401 with the `WWW-Authenticate` header. `create_workflow` then `duplicate_workflow` worked, both v1 "not active", and `list_workflow_versions` showed the note `via MCP: smoke`; `dry_run` of a path outside every library was `PATH_REJECTED`; `get_job` of an unknown id was `NOT_FOUND`. The server was stopped by its pid. The token value is not recorded anywhere.
- **Step 2, Claude Code connection: not run** (a person adds it; the controller notes forbid changing Claude Code or Claude Desktop configuration). To do: `claude mcp add --transport http transcoda http://localhost:8080/mcp --header "Authorization: Bearer <token>"`, `claude mcp list` should show `✓ Connected`; in a session ask it to list the workflows and say why the last job failed (expects `list_workflows`, `list_jobs`, `get_job`), then to duplicate a workflow as "Copy" and dry-run it on a file (`duplicate_workflow`, `dry_run`, `get_job`); the copy should show in the UI's workflow list with version note `via MCP…`, nothing active, no real run; then `claude mcp remove transcoda`. Record any wording the agent misread here.
- **Docs recheck:** the Errors table in `mcp.md` was corrected against the procedures (`JOB_NOT_ACTIVE` also means "not paused" for resume; `NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW` is `dry_run` without a workflow; `IN_USE` is a sub-workflow; `WORKFLOW_INVALID` is the version a dry run would use, a save stores invalid graphs; `BAD_REQUEST` is rare because the SDK validates first), the `Tool … not found` text carries the SDK's `MCP error -32602:` prefix, and the one-liners for `get_job`, the job controls, `delete_workflow` and `dry_run` were made to match the tools. *(Superseded by Library trigger, 2026-10-21.)*

## M3 plugins (phase 1)

Built 2026-10-17 on branch `m3-plugins` (core) and `m3-plugins` in `transcoda-plugins`: the plugin SDK 1.0 release candidate, the sandboxed per-plugin process, the installer and catalog client, secrets and the fetch proxy, the engine's plugin finish steps, the API, MCP and Plugins page. Author docs are in [`plugin-sdk.md`](plugin-sdk.md); the gate record is [`m3-gate.md`](m3-gate.md). Phase 2 (the six official plugins) and phase 3 (inbound triggers) follow.

### Security promise (binding; do not soften by accident)

- Plugins are trusted code. The sandbox is **accident protection, not a wall**: each plugin runs in its own process under Node's permission model (read only its own bundle, no file writes, no child processes, no native addons, no worker threads, no code generation from strings, an environment of just `PATH` and `NODE_ENV`), and it never touches media or the database; Transcoda does all file work and gives plugins data only. It cannot stop a malicious plugin from using the network freely (raw sockets), signalling same-uid processes (including Transcoda), or exhausting memory the heap limit does not count. The wording the docs and the install dialog carry is in `plugin-sdk.md`, "What the sandbox does and does not do".
- The official catalog only lists builds made by CI from the public source, with a checksum Transcoda verifies before running a line, and a build attestation. Custom catalogs show a warning, and the install dialog says "Built by CI" only for the official one. *(Superseded in part by "M3 security hardening" below: the promise text now also says the plugin user, the signatures and the blocklist, and no longer says a plugin can signal Transcoda's own process in the image.)*
- Install, update, remove, enable, disable, settings, catalogs: admin session only. No token scope, no MCP tool. `list_plugins` is the only plugin MCP tool (read) and an MCP never-list is backed by the API refusing tokens. The REST refusal text is "Plugin management is a UI action".
- Sandbox proof: 17 probes *(19 since the hardening round)* (`SANDBOX_EXPECTATIONS`; the plan's "16" was stale) in `sandbox.test.ts` and, in the image, `--check-plugin … --expect-denials` (exit 1 on a bundle that is not denied, on a probe the table does not know). A Node upgrade that changes an answer fails the suite.

### Architecture

- **One process per plugin**, not one shared host: a crash, timeout or memory kill of one plugin leaves the others' in-flight calls and nodes untouched (tested with a real second plugin). Spawn flags are `--permission`, `--allow-fs-read=<host>`, `--allow-fs-read=<bundle>` (both dropped with `process.permission.drop` after the import), `--disallow-code-generation-from-strings`, `--max-old-space-size`; `env` is `PATH` and `NODE_ENV`; advanced IPC serialization, detached process group, killed as a group.
- **`process.binding` is not deleted** in the host: Node's permission model already answers it with `ERR_ACCESS_DENIED`; deleting it would change the probe to a `TypeError` and say less. fd 4 probe accepts `ENXIO` (kqueue) and `EINVAL` (epoll).
- **Supervisor** checks the bundle's checksum before **every** fork (boot, enable, restart, crash restart); a mismatch is `stopped: bundle changed on disk; reinstall`, no backoff. A node registration failure at boot (a core update took the type) stops the plugin instead of running it half-registered. A first start that fails (install, enable, restart) is `stopped` with no automatic retry; crash restarts back off 1 s doubling to 60 s (reset after 5 min stable) and the plugin stops after 3 crashes in 10 min; failed restart attempts count as crashes.
- **Remove signal:** `remove()` publishes `plugins.changed` with `state: 'disabled'`, `stateReason: 'removed'` rather than a `removed` state (the state enum feeds the contract, the SSE schema and the web; clients refetch on any `plugins.changed` and the plugin is gone from the list).
- **Update** swaps through `startCandidate` and `commit()`; everything that can refuse (node type collisions, duplicates, the `<plugin>:` prefix) is checked before commit; in-flight calls on the old process get the retryable "restarted" rejection. Install with no row for the name first removes `<name>-<version>.*` leftovers of a crash. `install`/`update` refresh the cached catalog once when the version is not in it.
- **Dev plugins** (`TRANSCODA_DEV_PLUGINS`): never persisted, never from a catalog, refused in production. Their settings (and enabled state) live in memory only: a private in-memory table without the foreign key, closed on stop. A dev bundle shadowing an installed plugin, a second bundle of one name, or one that does not start is refused and logged as an error.
- **Bundle path** is `realpath`ed (a symlinked parent such as macOS `/var` otherwise breaks the read grant). `PluginProcess.start` accepts `manifest: null` ("any") for `--check-plugin` and dev bundles.
- **Config:** `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_URL` replaces the official catalog when set (that is what "official, cannot be removed" means then); `http:`/`file:` catalog urls and `http:` bundle urls are accepted outside production only.
- **Limits** are settings rows `plugins.limits.<key>` (no UI); a malformed or out-of-bound row falls back to the default.
- **Logging:** plugin stdout/stderr lines are forwarded once (as `{ msg: 'plugin log', plugin, level, message, jobId }`, through the same path as `ctx.log`), at most 100 lines/s per process, then dropped with a counter line. Outside production the logger prints `msg key=value …`; production writes the same fields as JSON lines.

### Secrets and the fetch proxy

- **Ruling (fetch-secret tokens).** Nothing secret crosses the process boundary after `init`/`settings`, **except inside a `fetch` message, which the server treats as secret material.** The host replaces each secret occurrence in a fetch input with `__TRANSCODA_SECRET__<fieldPath>__<raw|url|form|b64|b64url>__`; the server's fetch proxy substitutes only **that plugin's own** decrypted secret fields, after the origin check, only into the url, headers and body of that request and its same-origin redirect re-sends, and never into a fetch result, log, problem, trace, finding, API response or MCP answer. A token for an unknown field refuses the request (`secret "<field>" is not a secret field of this integration`, the name cut at 100 characters). Text between tokens still goes through the clear redactor; a request still containing `[redacted]` is refused with a pointer to `auth`.
- **Structured `auth`** on a fetch (`{ basic }`, `{ bearer }`, `{ header }`, `{ query }`) is encoded server-side after substitution, because a prefixed base64 secret (Basic) cannot be tokenised. A `:` in a Basic username is refused; `auth.header` may not name `host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, `connection`, `proxy-authorization`, `expect` and similar; query auth is appended, not de-duplicated.
- **`test()` with unsaved settings** substitutes from the candidate (merged over stored: a field absent from the candidate falls back to stored) for that call only, through a per-call fetch context (`callId`, `AsyncLocalStorage` in the host, a call map in the process); their secrets stay in the host's bounded redactor set (32, oldest dropped). A fetch from a call that already settled follows the per-process signal and stored policy, and only fetches in flight at settle are aborted.
- **Ambiguous secret names:** two secret fields that sanitise to the same token path (`api-key`, `api_key`), or a path containing `__`, make the plugin `stopped` with the field names, never a value.
- **Known limits (documented in `plugin-sdk.md`):** responses are not scanned for echoed secrets; non-ASCII secrets in header values must use `b64`/`url`/`auth`; a secret used as a header name or JSON key is not protected (only string values are tokenised); secrets shorter than 4 characters are not redacted; overlapping secrets can leave a tail of the shorter one; a plugin's own error text is shown as written (only secrets are redacted).
- **Private addresses are not filtered** (no private-IP or DNS-rebinding filter in the fetch proxy): allowed origins come from admin settings and LAN integrations (Sonarr, Radarr, Jellyfin) need private addresses; the origin allow-list is the control. Redirects are same-origin only (at most 5; 303 and 301/302 on non-GET/HEAD become GET without a body, 307/308 keep it); network errors say only the error code (`request failed: ECONNREFUSED`), never the URL.
- **Fetch limits:** the timeout applies per hop; refusal happens before queueing (no slot, no socket); the token bucket's burst equals the rate and the retry after a 429 takes a token; 429 without a readable `Retry-After` is `rate-limited` at once, `Retry-After` is capped at 60 s (HTTP dates supported); plugin-supplied `host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding` and `connection` headers are dropped; non-2xx answers are `ok: true` with the status; the 16 MB body cap also applies to `accept: none`. Before `setPolicy` everything is refused.
- **Settings crypto:** AES-256-GCM with a key derived with HKDF from the instance secret; stored settings hold `{ $enc }` for secret fields only (asserted on the whole table). A secret key with a value that is neither a string nor `{ set: true }` is dropped. `{ set: true }` or an absent secret keeps the stored value; the API never returns a stored secret. No logging in the crypto; errors carry no key, plaintext or ciphertext.
- **Key loss:** losing `secret.key` (or `TRANSCODA_SECRET_KEY`) also loses every plugin's integration secrets (documented in `env.md`). A plugin whose secrets cannot be opened starts with its plain settings only, and the card says `cannot decrypt <plugin>.<field>`.
- **Settings schema changes:** an old key the new schema no longer knows stays stored, encrypted, until the next save drops it; `plugins.settings.get` still answers; `needsReview` is `needs review: <path>: <message>; …` (paths and zod messages only, never values) and the process keeps running. A plugin with no settings row never says needs review. Validation while running uses the plugin's own zod, else the persisted JSON Schema (`z.fromJSONSchema`, without patterns or formats since 2026-10-21); settings schemas are not recursive.
- **Test results:** `lastTest` keeps the failure `kind`, has its message and warnings redacted by value (form plus stored secrets) and cut to 500 characters, and is persisted only when a settings row exists.

### Install, catalog and compatibility

- **Compatibility** everywhere is `semver.satisfies(SDK_VERSION, range, { includePrerelease: true })` (without the option `1.0.0-rc.1` does not satisfy `^1`); `SDK_VERSION` is `1.0.0-rc.1` until the phase-2 gate. `semver` 7.8.5 is the only new dependency (server; the plugins repo takes the same pin for `validRange`).
- **Catalog index:** `{ schema: 1, generated, plugins[{ name, title, description, latest, versions[{ version, sdk, coreTested[], url, manifestUrl, sha256, size, attestation|null, releasedAt }] }] }`; versions and `latest` must be valid semver; entry urls are https (http outside production). Cached on disk for an hour (stale cache served on an error; a 304 refreshes the time); an invalid index with no cache gives an error and no entries; cache write failures are swallowed; the index body is capped at 4 MB. The catalog token goes only over https to `github.com`, `api.github.com`, `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `objects.githubusercontent.com`, decided on the original URL and re-checked on every redirect hop; the installer applies the same rule to bundle and manifest downloads.
- **Installer:** the manifest is fetched and validated before the bundle; bundles are streamed to a `.tmp-*` file, hashed, and refused on a checksum mismatch, a declared size over 32 MB or a body over `min(32 MB, size)`; the manifest is renamed first (a crash leaves an orphan manifest, which `audit` reports and which never loads); targets are checked absent before and right before the rename; names and versions are validated (traversal such as `1.0.0+../../x` is refused; version at most 64 characters); the directory is created 0700 and a symlink or non-directory is refused. One in-process lock per `name@version`.
- **Failure texts:** `checksum mismatch` (plain, no hashes shown); `sdk ^2 is not supported by this server (sdk 1.0.0-rc.1)` (index entry first, then the manifest); `manifest in bundle says <name> <version>, catalog says <version>`; a start failure is `plugin crashed on start: <exit description>`, so a bundle without a plugin default export reads `plugin crashed on start: crashed: bundle has no plugin default export` and nothing is registered. Oversize manifest (over 1 MB) is `manifest`; fetch errors and non-2xx are `network`.
- **Provenance:** `attestation` and `coreTested` are kept in memory from install/update and the last catalog fetch (no column); `updateAvailable` is null until the first `catalog()` call. The catalog output is `{ catalogs[{ catalogUrl, official, error, fetchedAt, plugins }] }` with `installed` (string or null) per plugin.
- **Plugin names** `catalog` and `catalogs` are reserved; node types are `<plugin>:<node>`; a type already provided by core or another plugin is refused at install. The manifest schema is left non-strict (forward compatible).
- **API:** `plugins.list` needs the `read` scope and never includes settings; the other procedures are session only with CSRF. `catalogsAdd`/`catalogsRemove` take a body (DELETE with JSON) and answer the refreshed catalog output from cache; a service `invalid` error maps to `PLUGIN_INSTALL_FAILED` (422 with the reason), `no-integration` to NOT_FOUND, `settings-invalid` to `PLUGIN_SETTINGS_INVALID`, a plugin that is not running to `PLUGIN_NOT_RUNNING` (409). `ready.integration` also carries `id`, `title`, `requires` so the detail is complete.

### Engine

- **Finish phases and `FINISH_ORDER`:** finish nodes run `gate → write → library-manager → media-server → notify`; plugin finish nodes may use only the last three. The order is enforced per path in validation. **Consequence:** a workflow that notifies before the write (the old `core:notify` before `core:replace-original`) is now invalid; an existing one has an active version that cannot run until the notify node is moved after the write. The MCP never-test graphs were reordered accordingly. The error names the node holding the highest phase seen first.
- **`PLUGIN_UNAVAILABLE`:** a plugin node whose plugin is disabled, stopped, restarting or being removed makes the version invalid (`active.runnable` false, `workflows.activate` and `jobs.queue` refuse `WORKFLOW_INVALID`, a queued job fails at start with `workflow invalid: …plugin <name> is not running…`); the node is never treated as unknown. Core and plugin trace entries carry `source`; the web shows a plug icon for `plugin`.
- **Plugin finish steps** run after the write in phase order and never undo it. A failed `required` step is a **problem** (stored in `job_results.problems_json`, listed on the job and in `jobs.stats`, which makes the Jobs page show "needs attention" for a `done` job); an optional one is a warning; `warnings` still also contain the problems (unchanged behaviour). A step whose plugin is down is a retryable failure: one retry after `PLUGIN_RETRY_MS` (10 s), then it fails the same way. A stop during the steps ends the job `done` with the problem `the server stopped before the remaining finish actions` (or the cancel variant) and the next start does not re-run the write.
- **Hooks:** `afterReplace` is awaited right after the write (bounded, shutdown- and cancel-aware); `onJobDone`/`onJobFailed` are fire-and-forget after the result row (they must not hold a lane). Hooks are skipped on shutdown and during dry runs, and are not replayed on the next start; `onJobFailed` fires only for failures the pipeline records (not the scheduler's unexpected failure or the startup sweep). Hook contexts carry `dryRun` and `job { id, attempt }`.
- **Prepare can requeue:** a retryable plugin failure on attempt 1 becomes attempt 2 (same rule as the encode); a non-retryable one fails the job with `<nodeId>: <message>`. Remote decide naming an undeclared port throws `plugin N chose port P, which T does not declare`. Finish phase and hook kind are enforced server-side in `remote.ts`.
- **Cancel:** the pipeline's abort signal reaches the plugin as a protocol `cancel` for that call (the host drops its answer and aborts its fetches); a cancelled call that still does not answer is killed at its timeout (the process is restarted).
- **Registry:** entries are `{ meta, source, parseSettings, hooks }`; plugin node metadata is persisted so a restarting plugin's nodes are still listed (with `availability`); `listNodes` is cached per registry generation; `workflowsUsingNodeTypes` lists every live workflow (including sub-workflows) whose current or active graph uses the type and does not follow pinned children into parents.
- **Problems columns:** `upsertPluginSettings` keeps the stored `last_test_json`; `updatePlugin` maps patch keys to columns (`integration: null` clears); a sweep-completed job keeps earlier problems.
- **Language names:** `normalizeLanguageValue` treats `xx` as unknown.

### Web

- Plugins page: install dialog states provenance ("Built by CI" for the official catalog, "From a custom catalog" otherwise; an attestation is a link only when it parses as http(s)); installed catalog entries show "installed X" and updates go through the card; remove confirmation names the workflows that use the plugin's nodes and says which versions are active; an untouched stored secret is omitted from the save/test payload; a client-side "Required" check precedes save/test.
- `verdictOf(job)` takes the job; only `done` with problems becomes `attention`; the Jobs page has a needs-attention filter before "Paused".

### Known gaps and findings from the gate (see `m3-gate.md`)

- **A plugin that crashes while a job is in `decide` or `build` no longer fails the job (fixed):** `prepare`, when the workflow is invalid only because a plugin is unavailable, first waits (`PluginRestartGate`, polling the supervisor) while a plugin is `starting` or `restarting`, at most until its next restart plus the start timeout, then validates again; a plugin that is `stopped` (gave up), disabled or removed is not waited for and fails the job at once with `PLUGIN_UNAVAILABLE`. A call to a starting or restarting plugin is now a retryable `PluginCallError`. Still down after the wait: requeued once as attempt 2, failed at the last attempt (attempt counting unchanged). Validation is not relaxed (review focus 4). Tested with the real host fixture (kill -9 by `PluginService.pid` during decide: the job ends `done`, attempt 2).
- **Installer and catalog downloads no longer use the global `fetch` (fixed):** the Node 24.21 undici crash (`assert(!this.paused)`, HTTP/1.0 close-delimited host plus a slow reader) is avoided by `plugins/http-get.ts`, a `fetch`-shaped GET on `node:http(s)` (manual redirects, so the GitHub-token host re-check in `fetchGuarded` is unchanged; streamed bodies, size caps and timeouts stay with the callers). A malformed or HTTP/1.0 server is an ordinary `network` failure (tested against a raw TCP HTTP/1.0 server and a garbage answer). The installer removes its `.tmp-*` files on every failure path, and the startup audit removes stale `.tmp-*` files from `/config/plugins` (reported as removed).
- Not a defect but worth knowing: the image-level sandbox job runs after the image push (a failure is a red workflow on a published tag, accepted for now); `@transcoda/sdk` is not published, so the plugins repo links it from a core checkout (`CORE_REPO_TOKEN` in CI while the repo is private); the plugins repo's example test still patches `now` onto `fakeHookContext()` (the SDK now supplies it).

## M3 security hardening

Built 2026-10-17 on `m3-plugins` (core) and `m3-plugins` (plugins repo) from the sandbox architecture review's findings H1 (plugin hosts shared the server's uid), H2 (nothing hardened the container), M1/M2 and the blocklist idea, before phase 2. Jan approved the review's recommendations the same day ("go with recs"). What a plugin can and cannot do now is in [`plugin-sdk.md`](plugin-sdk.md) ("What the sandbox does and does not do"), the variables in [`env.md`](env.md), and the signing and blocklist workflow in the plugins repo's README. The security promise text is shared verbatim by `plugin-sdk.md` and `SECURITY_PROMISE` in `apps/web/src/plugins/pluginText.ts`; `pluginText.test.ts` pins the two equal (and `CUSTOM_CATALOG_WARNING`).

### Rulings from Jan (2026-10-17, "go with recs")

1. Plugin user `65001:65001`; the entrypoint `chmod o-rwx` of `/config` and `/transcode` (persists on the host bind mounts; accepted). Works with `PUID=0` (the CT 111 setup; a uid-0 server plus a setpriv host was tested).
2. No `useradd`/`groupadd` (numeric ids everywhere; read-only rootfs). `docker exec … whoami` prints `unknown uid` (the measured text is `whoami: cannot find name for user ID <id>`); `os.userInfo()` throws for such a uid (nothing in Transcoda calls it; `HOME` is exported).
3. The blocklist lives on the plugins repo's `catalog` branch beside `index.json` (same key, same CI, one secret).
4. A plugin installed from a signed custom catalog that is later removed stops at its next start with `signed with an unknown key <id>` (verified against an empty key list, never skipped).
5. `pids_limit: 1024` in the repo compose; `mem_limit` stays commented there with the formula, and is set to `mem_limit: 3g` in the deploy plan's CT 111 compose.
6. Dev-mode plugins (`TRANSCODA_DEV_PLUGINS`) are **not isolated**: they run as the server's user, documented; refused in production anyway.
7. Jan generates the production signing key later; until the public key is pinned, a production build refuses the official catalog (`catalog signature missing` / `signed with an unknown key`) and logs one warning at start. Local and dev flows use `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY=none` or `dev:<hex>`.

Controller rulings during the round (also "go with recs"): `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY=<id>:<hex>` may replace the pinned set in production (whoever sets the environment controls the server); a custom catalog's key is entered by the admin as `<keyId>:<hex>[,…]`, the same format (the first draft hard-coded the key id `custom`); production on Linux without isolation refuses to start unless `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_ISOLATION=off`.

### Plugin user and container
