- **Mechanism: ambient capabilities on the server plus `setpriv` per host.** The entrypoint runs as root only to prepare ids and directories, then execs the server as `PUID:PGID` through `setpriv` with `CAP_SETUID`, `CAP_SETGID` and `CAP_KILL` as bounding, inheritable and ambient capabilities (`00000000000000e0`, effective too) and no-new-privileges; the server starts each host as `PLUGIN_UID:PLUGIN_GID` through `setpriv --reuid --regid --clear-groups --inh-caps=-all --ambient-caps=-all --no-new-privs`. Rejected: (a) a root launcher process that forks hosts on request (an IPC relay for every host message, and a root Node process alive for the container's life); (b) a setuid helper (defeated by `no-new-privileges`, which we want); (c) user namespaces (`unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` is blocked by Docker's default seccomp profile without `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`). The cost of the chosen one: the server could `setuid(0)` inside the container; that root has no capabilities (bounding set) and a read-only rootfs, so it is "a user that owns root's files", and the server already holds every secret the container has.
- **`CAP_KILL`** because `kill(2)` across uids needs it: the supervisor SIGKILLs a spinning host and its process group.
- **`CAP_FSETID` for the entrypoint only** (compose `cap_add` has it; the server's bounding set drops it): `chmod 2710` on a directory whose group the caller is not in silently loses the setgid bit without it (measured in the preflight), and staged bundles would then be `644 PUID:PGID`, unreadable for the plugin user.
- **No `--bounding-set` on hosts.** Dropping bounding-set capabilities needs `CAP_SETPCAP` in the effective set, which the server deliberately lacks: with util-linux 2.41.5 (the preflight) a host spawned with `--bounding-set=-all` does not start (`setpriv: apply bounding set: Operation not permitted`); with the image's 2.38.1 the flag happened to be a silent no-op. Either way the host argv never carries it. A host's `CapBnd` stays `e0`; with zero permitted, effective, inheritable and ambient capabilities and `NoNewPrivs 1` it cannot be exercised, so it is harmless. The identity check and the image CI never look at `CapBnd`.
- **Engine children are wrapped too (deviation from the plan).** The plan accepted that ffmpeg and ffprobe inherit the server's three ambient capabilities as "not a regression". The review of Task 7 asked for more and `engine/child-caps.ts` now starts every ffmpeg and ffprobe through `setpriv --inh-caps=-all --ambient-caps=-all --no-new-privs --` when isolation is configured (a missing ffmpeg then shows as setpriv's non-zero exit rather than `ENOENT`; transcoding is unavailable either way). The image CI's hardening job proves such a child has zero capability sets.
- **No `useradd`/`groupadd`:** `setpriv --reuid/--regid/--groups` take numbers; a passwd entry was cosmetic and wrote `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/group`, which a read-only rootfs forbids. `HOME` is exported (`/config`). Consequences: `whoami` fails with `cannot find name for user ID <id>`, `os.userInfo()` throws (nothing in `main.js` or `plugin-host.js` calls it), and a `PUID` change no longer needs a recreated container (it applies on restart; `TRANSCODA_CHOWN_CONFIG=1` re-owns existing files once). The entrypoint validates `PUID`, `PGID`, `PLUGIN_UID` and `PLUGIN_GID` as plain decimals up to 2147483647 and the plugin ids as at least 1 (4294967295 is setpriv's "leave unchanged" sentinel and must never reach it; 0 would give a host that regains capabilities), `PLUGIN_UID`/`PLUGIN_GID` must differ from `PUID`/`PGID`, and `PLUGIN_GID` must not be a passed-in device group; all exit 2 with the offending name.
- **`chmod o-rwx` on `/config` and `/transcode`:** the plugin user must not reach the database, `secret.key` or the manifests. On a typical `755` volume the result is `750` (group bits stay; "no other bits" is the rule, not `700`). On a host bind mount the change persists. Docker Desktop for macOS (virtiofs) does not enforce it: the check passes only on Linux or with a named volume. Bind-mounted media (`/data`) is the user's and is not touched; there Node's permission model is the wall.
- **The staging copy, and why not `/config/plugins` group-readable.** A host needs to read its bundle, but giving the plugin group traversal on `/config` would expose every `0644` file under it. Instead the server writes the verified bytes to `/run/transcoda/plugins/<name>-<version>.js` (tmpfs; directory `PUID:PLUGIN_GID`, mode `2710`; file `0640`, exclusive temporary file then rename, real path used for the sandbox read grant) before every start, and clears the directory at start. An update stages the **candidate** and verifies it before the commit; the old version's copy goes after the commit; a disabled plugin updated in place loses its staged copy at once and stages again on enable; install rollback, discard, disable and remove unstage. A staging failure is `stopped: cannot stage bundle: <reason>`, no restart loop.
- **Identity check after spawn.** Once the host reports ready, the supervisor reads `/proc/<pid>/status` and stops the plugin (`plugin host is not isolated: <field> expected <x>, got <y>`, no restart) when the real uid or gid differs, any of the effective, permitted or ambient capability sets is not zero, or `NoNewPrivs` is not 1; an unreadable identity fails closed. It runs after the host evaluated the bundle's module code (so it proves the steady state, not the first lines); it reads the real uid and gid only. `--check-plugin` reports the same fields (`isolation`) and the image CI asserts them. Known small gap: the `--check-plugin` command does not compare the host's gid with the server's own gid when parsing its isolation.
- **`--user`, an unpreparable staging directory and the "same-uid with a log line" idea.** The plan's first draft let a container started with `--user`, or a staging directory that could not be created, quietly fall back to same-uid hosts. That is not what shipped. The entrypoint then exports no plugin variables and logs `plugin isolation is off`; the server, in production on Linux, **refuses to start** unless `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_ISOLATION=off` (controller ruling), and with it logs `plugin hosts run as the server's user`. Outside production same-uid runs without a requirement. **What same-uid means precisely** (final review): the plugin host runs as the server's uid and group, with no capabilities. When isolation is off, whether by `--user`, an unpreparable `/run/transcoda`, or `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_ISOLATION=off`, the entrypoint (if root) execs the server with `--inh-caps=-all --ambient-caps=-all --bounding-set=-all --no-new-privs` (the server needs `CAP_SETUID`/`CAP_SETGID`/`CAP_KILL` only to start a plugin user), and, as a second line, the server wraps plugin hosts and ffmpeg/ffprobe in `setpriv --inh-caps=-all --ambient-caps=-all --no-new-privs --` whenever it is Linux, `setpriv` exists and `/proc/self/status` shows a nonzero `CapAmb`. `entrypoint.test.sh` checks both off paths and CI runs it. `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_ISOLATION=off` is honoured by the entrypoint (final review: it exports no plugin user and starts the server without capabilities); the server itself reads it only when no plugin user is configured, so a server run directly with a plugin user ignores it. Configured isolation that cannot work is a startup error, never same-uid: `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_UID` equal to the server's uid or `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_GID` to its gid, set on a non-Linux platform, set without `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_GID`, outside 1..2147483647, or set while the server lacks `CAP_SETUID`/`CAP_SETGID`/`CAP_KILL` (read from `/proc/self/status`), without an executable `setpriv`, or with a staging directory that is not writable (`IsolationError`, exit 2).
- **Dev plugins are not isolated** (ruling 6): `isolated: false`, they run from their own path as the server's user and are never staged; `TRANSCODA_DEV_PLUGINS` is refused in production.
- **Cross-plugin isolation is uid-level only.** All hosts share `PLUGIN_UID:PLUGIN_GID`: a plugin can signal a sibling host and, through the shared group, read the staged bundles at the operating-system level (bundles are public code; the permission model's read grant is what separates them). Per-plugin uids were not worth the id management; per-plugin network and process namespaces mean the separate-container design below.
- **Two facts measured in the preflight** that shaped the code: setpriv orders its operations itself, so the entrypoint's option order does not matter (the line gives the server exactly `e0`/`NoNewPrivs 1`, also with `PUID=0`); and `spawn(setpriv, …, { stdio: [..., 'ipc'], serialization: 'advanced', detached: true })` carries the IPC channel (the child's pid is setpriv's, which execs node, so the pid and `kill(-pid)` keep working).
- **Compose and CI.** `docker/docker-compose.yml`: `security_opt: no-new-privileges:true`, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `cap_add: [CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, FOWNER, FSETID, SETUID, SETGID, SETPCAP, KILL]`, `read_only: true`, tmpfs `/tmp` and `/run/transcoda` (`mode=0755`, 256 MB), `pids_limit: 1024`, `mem_limit` commented with the formula (set to 3g in the CT 111 compose of the deploy plan), and a comment never to use `privileged`, `seccomp=unconfined` or `docker.sock`. Two image CI jobs: `sandbox` (per architecture, the compose flags, asserts `probes == 19`, uid `65001`, all capability sets zero, `NoNewPrivs 1`) and `hardening` (`docker/entrypoint.test.sh` in the base image with the real `setpriv`, then the shipped compose file against the pushed image: the server's five capability lines are exactly `e0` with `NoNewPrivs 1`, the plugin user cannot signal the server nor list `/config`, the root filesystem is read-only, ffmpeg works, no isolation warning in the log, a server child has no capabilities). Neither job has run on GitHub yet.

### Signing and the catalog

- **Format.** A detached file beside the document, `<url>.sig`: `{ "schema": 1, "alg": "ed25519", "keyId": "<id>", "signature": "<base64 of the 64-byte signature over the exact bytes of the signed file>" }`, strict (no extra keys, canonical base64, `keyId` matches `/^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$/`, at most 16 KB). Public keys are the raw 32 bytes as 64 lowercase hex characters; the private key is PKCS8 PEM. `node:crypto` only, no new dependency in either repo. The signature covers the bytes, not the checksum: the installer reads the stored bundle back and verifies it, and the catalog and blocklist caches keep the exact verified bytes and their `.sig`.
- **Keys are pinned in `apps/server/src/plugins/official-keys.ts`** (`OFFICIAL_SIGNING_KEYS`, empty until Jan generates the production key). One key signs bundles, the index and the blocklist. `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY` replaces the list (also in production, see the rulings); `none` is refused when `NODE_ENV=production`; an empty list means official installs are refused (`catalog signature missing` or `signed with an unknown key <id>`), with one warning at start. A whitespace-only value counts as unset. A self-hosted catalog in the official slot is verified with the pinned keys.
- **Missing is never "unsigned".** A catalog whose index loads but whose `.sig` answers 404 is `catalog signature missing`; a 5xx on the `.sig` is a fetch error (the last verified cache may serve); a signature disagreement is retried once past the CDN cache (`raw.githubusercontent.com` caches the document and its `.sig` separately), then falls back to the last verified index only; a missing, malformed or unknown-key signature never falls back. A signed plugin whose `.js.sig` was deleted is `bundle signature missing; reinstall` before the start, never started on the checksum alone; a changed one `bundle signature invalid; reinstall`; a removed key `signed with an unknown key <id>; update the plugin`. An install records `signed_by` (migration 0007, plus `signing_keys` on `plugin_catalogs`).
- **Custom catalogs pin a key when they are added**: `<keyId>:<hex>[,…]` in the same format (ruling above; the signature files must name one of those ids), refused on the official url (`the official catalog's key is set by TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY`) and on a url with a query string. Re-adding a known url without a key keeps the pin (`COALESCE`); to unpin, remove the catalog and add it again. Stored keys that no longer parse verify nothing (never "unsigned"). Removing a signed catalog makes its plugins stop at their next start with `signed with an unknown key <id>` (ruling 4: verified against an empty key list, never skipped).
- **Installs recorded unsigned are never checked later.** A plugin installed from an unsigned catalog or with `none` has `signed_by` null and is not signature-checked at any later start, even after a key is pinned or the catalog gets signed; reinstall it. After switching a signed install to `none`, the start fails with the unknown-key message and its `; update the plugin` hint, which is then misleading.
- **No freshness.** Signed documents carry no expiry: a validly signed older index can be replayed to someone who controls the network path, hiding an update or offering an older signed build. *(Partly superseded 2026-10-21: a verified index older than the cached verified one is now refused, see "Signed catalog index is monotonic". A server with no cache, or after the cache is cleared, still accepts any validly signed index.)* The blocklist has this protection (below).
- **Rotation.** Add the new public key to `official-keys.ts` and release core; then switch the CI secret; remove the old key two core releases later (a bundle signed with a removed key then stops at its next start with `signed with an unknown key <id>`). **Compromise runbook, in this order** (final review; the first draft said "remove the key now and blocklist", which cannot work because the blocklist is signed with the same key): (1) generate a new key; (2) pin it and drop the compromised one in a core release; (3) re-sign the index and the blocklist with the new key; (4) only then add the sha256 entries for the affected bundles. A server on an older core has no protection until it updates: it still trusts the compromised key and cannot verify the new blocklist. The plugins repo README part is a separate change.
- **A custom `TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY` makes the official blocklist inert.** The blocklist is verified with the same keys as the catalog, so overriding them in the official slot leaves the official blocklist unverifiable. The server logs one warning at start (`the official plugin blocklist is configured but TRANSCODA_PLUGIN_CATALOG_KEY replaces the official signing keys: the blocklist is inert and blocks nothing`) when the blocklist url is the official one and the keys differ from `OFFICIAL_SIGNING_KEYS`.
- **Production signing key and CI secrets are Jan's hand-off:** generate with `node scripts/sign.mjs keygen --out <dir>`, store the PEM as `PLUGIN_SIGNING_KEY` and the id as `PLUGIN_SIGNING_KEY_ID`, pin the printed public key, and (later, noted in the controller ledger) put the secret in a GitHub Environment restricted to `main` and the release tags with required reviewers and pin the actions by SHA.

### Blocklist

- **Location.** `blocklist.json` and `blocklist.json.sig` on the plugins repo's `catalog` branch beside `index.json` (ruling 3: same key, same CI, one secret; a second repository would add a secret and a workflow for no isolation gain, since whoever controls the signing key controls both anyway). The hand-edited source is `catalog/blocklist.json` on `main`; `catalog.yml` validates it with `scripts/check-blocklist.mjs`, stamps `generated`, signs and pushes it, even when the index build fails.
- **Semantics (from the review): fail-open on a fetch error, fail-closed on a positive match.** Checked at install and update, and before every start; an installed match is stopped with `blocklisted: <reason>` (disabled plugins stay disabled and are refused at their next start). Entries match by `sha256`, or by `name` with an optional semver `versions` range (`versions` needs `name`); an entry with both `sha256` and `name` matches **either** (OR), so a name-and-range entry with a sha256 also blocks a renamed copy of that exact build and blocks every build of that name in the range, not only the hashed one; `reason` is 1-200 characters, `url` an optional https advisory link. One malformed entry makes the whole document invalid: it is reported as a fetch problem and the previously cached list keeps being enforced. `generated` is monotonic: an older signed list never replaces a newer one, so replaying an old document cannot lift a block. A list that cannot be read blocks nothing.
- **Core mirrors the plugins repo's schema** (strict objects, non-blank range, trimmed reason). The two content checks `check-blocklist.mjs` adds (unique ids, `added` not after `generated`) stay CI-only in core: a list that is merely untidy must still be enforced. A follow-up for the plugins repo: warn when a `versions` range normalises to `*`.
- **Timing and cache.** Fetched at start in the background (3 s timeout, nothing waits for the network; the cached list, verified again, is in force before any plugin boots), then hourly, and when a catalog refresh is asked for (`catalog(refresh=true)` runs the full recheck, so a running plugin newly listed is stopped at once, not at the next hourly recheck); cached at `<config>/plugins/.catalog/blocklist.json` with its ETag and `.sig` (a 304 only answers for a cache that still verifies). A dev plugin is never blocklisted. The blocklist uses the official keys, so it is inert until a production key is pinned (hand-off).
- **Where it shows:** the plugin card (`Blocklisted: <reason>`, with an advisory link for http(s) urls), the catalog and install dialog (`blocklisted: <reason>`, install disabled), `list_plugins` (`BLOCKLISTED: <reason>`), the catalog output's `blocklist` status, and `plugins.list`'s `blocked`.

### Known, documented, not fixable in-process

- **`--disallow-code-generation-from-strings` is not a boundary** (the M2 sandbox review): `vm.runInNewContext` and `vm.compileFunction` run strings regardless. No privilege is gained (the permission model and the plugin user still apply); documented as "do not rely on the flag".
- **Raw sockets (H3).** The network is outside Node's permission model and the fetch proxy has no private-address filter: a plugin can reach the LAN, the server's own API on `127.0.0.1` and anything else the container can reach. Hence never mount `docker.sock`. Documented in the promise and in `plugin-sdk.md`.
- Memory outside the V8 heap is caught only by the 1 s RSS poll and the container limit.

### What stays open

- A per-plugin network namespace needs plugins in a separate container (the design alternative); not done.
- The attestation link in the install dialog is only a link: server-side verification of build attestations is phase 2's item.
- A GitHub Actions run of the new image jobs, and CT 111's checks (cgroup memory limit, NVENC under `cap_drop: ALL`), are still to be done (Task 11).

## 2026-10-18 Integrations (Bazarr, Seerr, sidecar subtitles, deep health)

Built on `integrations`: extra input files in the build model, the embed-sidecar and recycle steps, the deep health check, and the Bazarr and Seerr plugins. Gate outcomes are in `integrations-gate.md`.

### Extra input files and sidecars

- **`ExtraInput.codec` is required:** `fitToContainer` rewrites a stream's codec to `mov_text`, so the original is kept on the input. The diff (`convert subrip→mov_text`) and the "container can't hold it" check need it. `renderArgs` adds `-f <demuxer>` before each extra `-i` (subrip `srt`, ass/ssa `ass`, `webvtt`, `hdmv_pgs_subtitle` `sup`); any other codec is a `RenderError`.
- **The walker guard fails closed:** it applies to anything that is not a core node, including a missing registry entry. It compares `JSON.stringify` of `extraInputs` and the extra-stream pairs `(sourceIndex, input)` sorted by `sourceIndex`; source streams only must keep `input` 0. A plugin may edit an extra stream's flags but never add, remove or re-point an input. Why: a plugin must not be able to make ffmpeg read an arbitrary path.
- **Sidecars are copied before ffmpeg sees them:** `FsOps.copyRegular` opens with `O_NOFOLLOW`, checks the handle (regular file, at most 8 MiB, recounted while streaming) and writes `<tempDir>/sidecar-<n><ext>` exclusively with mode 0600. The original path never reaches ffmpeg. Why: no symlink or swap between check and use (TOCTOU). The 8 MiB cap lives only in `engine/paths.ts`; discovery skips larger files for every format.
- **Discovery is just as strict:** the head read opens with `O_NONBLOCK` and refuses anything that is not a regular file (a FIFO would block). The video's own base name is always a candidate owner. `ExtraInput.path` is the realpath.
- **Embed reason format:** `embed A (deu, forced), B (eng)`, each skip appended as `; skipped <file>: <reason>`. Nothing embedded but something skipped starts `no subtitle file embedded`; no candidates reads `no subtitle files next to the video`. `already has <lang>[ forced][ sdh]` uses the 639-2/T code; an untagged file reads `language und not selected`.
- **Nothing embedded returns the incoming model unchanged** (no `extraInputs: []` key), so a re-run is `nothing_to_do`.
- **Order and defaults:** embedded streams go after the highest existing `order` (kept or dropped), in sidecar name order; a `default` token sets the flag and `normalizeDefaults` keeps the first default per type. The same language twice (`en.eng`) is one language, not a skip. A later embed node counts kept extra streams as existing and skips a path that is already an input, whatever `skipExisting` says.
- **Names, not indexes:** an extra stream is named by file name (`subtitle Show.fr.sup`) in core node reasons and target warnings, never `#-1`. Source streams keep `#n`.
- **Dropped sidecar:** diff line `drop subtitle from <file> (container can't hold it)`, so the plan note is exactly `container can't hold it`. Plan table "Subtitle files", header `N of M embedded`. The verify check counts kept extra streams whose output position holds a subtitle of the same language; a missing sidecar also fails stream-count.
- **Recycle after the replace:** sidecars go to the recycle folder right after the replace installs, before later finish actions (the recovered-replace path too), with `renameNoClobber`. The path must be inside the library root and equal its realpath (the same check as the copy), else a problem and the file stays. A sidecar is recorded before the folder sync; a failed sync is a problem, not a failed recycle. Problem text uses the error code, so no full path appears in a job reason. If the unlink after the link fails the file stays under both names; that is reported and not recorded.
- **mp4 output always has a default video:** when none is flagged, the first kept non-cover video is, with the plan note `mp4 cannot have no default video`. Why: the mp4 muxer flags it anyway, so verify failed on `default, expected not default` after a remux from an mkv without one. mkv is unchanged.

### Deep health check

- **`GET /system/health?deep=1` (or `deep=true`):** any other value is a 400. Failure is a 503 with the failing checks named; the result is cached 10 s, stamped when the check finished. A check that throws unexpectedly is not cached.
- **Checks:** ffmpeg available, scratch space against `MIN_FREE_GIB` (plain decimals, no sign or exponent), and no stalled job (`STALL_HOURS`; `deciding` counts as running). Paused work is ignored: a paused queue is ok. Each check is raced against 5 s, so a hung statfs is a failure, not a hung monitor.
- **Logging:** a failed check logs a warning; a thrown one adds `error`. The message may carry a path, so it goes to the server log only, never the response.

### Bazarr plugin

- **`get-subtitles-now` waits at most 25 s (default 25), not the design's 120 s:** a plugin call is limited to 30 s (`plugins.limits.callTimeoutMs`), and the lookups and the last check count against the wait. `waitSeconds` has `.max(25)` and build clamps again. The reason names the clamped value. Each request gets the remaining budget (at least 1 s), so the last request may end about 1 s past the budget, inside the 30 s.
- **Polling:** the PATCHes run together (`Promise.allSettled`); a PATCH that times out is ignored, any other fetch failure ends with `Bazarr unreachable: <kind>; continuing without new subtitles`, and HTTP errors on the PATCH are ignored since the listing is the truth. One reload right after the PATCHes, then polls at 0, 3 and 6 s steps; the last poll is at the deadline, never after. A dry run makes no calls at all.
- **B5 (differs from the assumption): the subtitle PATCH is queued, not synchronous.** Bazarr answers 204 at once, even for an unknown id, and the result shows up in a later listing. Why polling stays: it was already right; only comments and a test name said "synchronous".
- **B4 (differs): embedded tracks are listed only for languages in the item's Bazarr language profile** (default `embedded_subs_show_desired`), and not at all for an item without a profile. The API cannot reveal the rest. Setting and node descriptions now say so (`EMBEDDED_CAVEAT`); an item with no profile gives no languages, not an error.
- **Lookup:** movies cache only `{id,title,path}` (10 min); the subtitle list is always read fresh by `radarrid[]` after the path match. Episodes are fetched per series, uncached. The title fallback is only for series entries without a `path`. Movie `title` has no year (B3). Episode item title is `<series> S01E03`.
- **Languages:** `subtitles-present` compares through `lang.toIso3` (`ger` equals `deu`). An unknown-language subtitle stays `unknown` and is left out of the summary. The `test()` status call has a 10 s timeout.
- **Version check:** numeric, 1.4.0 or newer; an unparsable version is `wrong-product`; `data.bazarr_version` (no leading `v`), else `data.version`. `rescan`: 4xx is `ok: false`, 5xx adds `retryable: true`; an item gone after the scan says so.
- **Secrets:** the key travels only in `auth` (`X-API-KEY`); a sentinel test covers every hook on the happy and failing paths, movies included.

### Seerr plugin

- **S4 (differs): the 400 was the plugin's own doing.** Seerr rejects an unescaped `' ( ) ! *` in `query` ("must be url encoded"), and `encodeURIComponent` leaves those alone. `strictEncode` escapes them (`%27 %28 %29 %21 %2A`) for every query value. The cleaned-title retry stays as a safety net, only when the cleaned title differs.
- **S3 (differs): an unknown TMDB id answers 500, not 404** ("Unable to retrieve movie."). The plugin maps a 500 from `/movie/` or `/tv/` to unknown with `Seerr could not load TMDB id N (unknown id, or TMDB unreachable)`. A known but never requested title has `mediaInfo.requests: []` and reads as not requested. `requestedBy` carries no `email` (Seerr filters it).
- **Search hits carry no `requests`:** the matched hit's own list is used only when non-empty; otherwise the lookup goes by id (S3), so an empty list is re-verified.
- **Ids:** tmdb and tvdb together mean tv; tmdb only means a movie guess, checked against a connected title when there is one; tvdb only searches by title (kind tv) or, without a title, is unknown (`Seerr cannot look up a TVDB id; connect a title`). Title-only matches without a year say `(matched by title, no year)`.
- **Requests:** declined (status 3) is ignored; failed (4) still counts as requested; earliest by `createdAt`, a missing one sorts last, an invalid one reads `unknown` (`date unknown`).
- **Privacy:** a `displayName` containing `@` (Seerr falls back to the email) is shown as `user #<id>`, or `unknown`; an address is never output.
- **Statuses:** 401/403 `Seerr rejected the API key`; 404 `Seerr does not know TMDB id N`; others `Seerr answered HTTP n for ...`. A 5xx on `/status` is `other`. No `instanceof`; fetch failures go through `fetchErrorKind`, HTTP statuses are data.
- **Notify-requester is dropped**; Task 5's docs say so.

### Plugin conventions and gate

- **Shared helpers:** Bazarr and Seerr use `@transcoda/plugin-arr-common` (a workspace devDependency inlined in the bundle), like Sonarr and Radarr. Query keys are written literally (`seriesid[]=1`), values percent-encoded.
- **Gate run (2026-10-18, real Sonarr 4.0.20, Radarr, Bazarr 1.6.2 and Seerr 3.5.0):** 10 of 14 assumptions confirmed as written; B4, B5, S3 and S4 differ and are all fixed (above). Seerr was finished against a live instance with a temporary Jellyfin. Not tested: B5 with a real subtitle provider (none configured).

### Size gate: webhook body

- **A kept original is visible in the webhook body.** `core:notify-webhook` wired to the size gate's No output sends `outcome: "kept_original"` plus `reason` (for example `saved only 3.2%, below 10%`), with `replacedTo: null` and `afterBytes` the size of the discarded result. On every other path `outcome` is `"done"` and there is no `reason` key. The other fields are unchanged (`jobId`, `runId`, `path`, `workflowId`, `workflowVersion`, `beforeBytes`, `afterBytes`, `replacedTo`). Why: the job ends `kept_original`, so a receiver should not read a kept file as a success.

## 2026-10-18 Editor patterns

Built on `editor-patterns`: the handoff's node and handle patterns (N2, N4–N11) checked against the M2 editor and the gaps closed. Rulings from Jan on the plan's four questions come first.

### Rulings from Jan (2026-10-18)

- **Keep N9 as built.** The compact and icon zoom levels stay (see "M2 Task 8"); N12's "collapse below 75 %" relies on them.
- **"plugin missing" and "needs a newer version".** An unknown node type whose plugin is not installed reads "plugin missing" (red, plug-off); a core or un-namespaced unknown type still reads "unknown node". `UNKNOWN_NODE_VERSION` reads "needs a newer version" (red, plug-off). This supersedes the M3 ruling in `plugins/pluginText.ts`; the plugin-remove confirmation now says the workflows "will show "plugin missing"".
- **The M2 keyboard model stays.** Tab moves to the next node; Enter or "." moves into the node's toolbar, Escape back (see "M2 Task 11"). The canvas key now lists Enter "node actions".
- **Arrowheads on every edge**, data edges included (N7 "Direction").

### Node states (N6)

- **"plugin missing" only when the plugin is really absent:** the node's type counts as a plugin's only when no catalogue node shares its namespace (`stateTypeFor`). A plugin that is installed but no longer ships the node reads "unknown node". No catalogue yet counts as absent. The canvas and the Inspector header use the same rule.
- **`UNKNOWN_NODE_VERSION` badge:** `plugin-missing` for a plugin type, `unknown-node` otherwise; with the rule above an installed plugin's node gets `unknown-node`. Both draw the plug-off glyph, so only the class differs.

### Connection feedback (N5)

- **Fits are worked out per card,** in one `useStore` selector that returns a string, and passed to each `TcHandle` as `fit`. `dragFromOf` lives in `TcHandle.tsx`; `connectionFit.ts` stays free of React and React Flow and mirrors `checkConnection`.
- **An unknown node's ports have no type** (`HandleRef.portType: null`): never compatible, but still the dragged handle. A card with no handles never fades (`nodeFaded([])` is false). The dragged handle gets no class of its own (`is-source` dropped: nothing styled it).
- **Faded once, never multiplied:** a faded card is `opacity: 0.4`; while faded it drops the inline 60 % of "not on this server", and its incompatible handles stay at full strength inside it (`.tc-node.is-faded .tc-handle.is-incompatible { opacity: 1 }`).
- **Collapsed groups are not faded while dragging:** they are no drop target (their handles are not connectable; `checkConnection` finds no node by a group's id).
- **The dragged line over a valid target:** a file line turns lime (`--accent`, the compatible handle's ring); a data line keeps its data yellow, as the data port's halo does, and thickens to 3 px. The glowing handle carries the meaning.
- **Click-to-connect** does not set React Flow's `connection.inProgress`, so it shows no glow or fade (unchanged from M2). Handle and fade transitions are off under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.

### Edges and the key (N7, N8)

- **Every edge style draws an arrowhead at the target,** filled with the edge's own colour token. The marker is sized in user space (`markerUnits="userSpaceOnUse"`, 12 px), so a 2 px dotted data edge gets the same arrow as a 3.5 px path edge.
- **An edge's accessible name ends with its meaning word** (`, leads to a broken node` / `, data` / `, path taken` / `, not taken`; plain file flow none). The word follows the drawn style, first match wins, so a broken data edge reads "broken". `edgeStyle`, `edgeWord` and `ARROWED` live in the pure `editor/flow/edge-style.ts`; `toFlow`, `TcEdge` and the key read them.
- **The key** follows `ARROWED`, lists "file flow" first, shows "running (moving border)" as a still lime ring, and lists Enter "node actions".
- **Bypass is a toggle button** with one stable name "Bypass" and `aria-pressed`; "Un-bypass" is retired. The card's "SKIPPED HERE" stays the visible state.

### Node search (N4)

- **Heading "Add the next node · N matches"** (spec N4's button name) for a filtered search. A line under the list says what is listed and is the search box's accessible description: "Only nodes that accept a file are listed", or for data "Only nodes with a/an <name> input are listed", where the name is the dragged port's label when it has one (first letter lower-cased unless it starts with an acronym), else the type id (`data:audio-track` → "an audio-track input"); "a" or "an" by the first letter.
- **The hint shows for every filtered search** (the "+" handle, "C", a connection dropped on empty canvas), and still under "No nodes match".

### Branch rows (N2)

- **Yes reads lime, No faint with a grey dot:** the `pass` row's word is `--accent`, the `else` row's word `--faint` with a filled `--edge-untaken` dot; the words carry the meaning. The trace's taken / dimmed colours and the progress fill win over them.
- **The colour follows the port id** (`branchWord`), so a plugin's own words for its pass / else ports get the same colours. Data ports are never branch-coloured.

### Groups and the minimap (N10, N11)

- **A collapsed group shows its hidden members' worst state:** border red > amber > dashed, the worst member's badge (`generic-error` if it has none), its word for one member or "<n> nodes need attention" for several. Expanded, the tile shows nothing; the members show their own.
- **Tile looks:** red takes the card's broken background (`--node-broken-bg`) and a 2 px ring; dashed recolours the tile's own dashed border to `--faint` (no second outline; selection's accent still wins). The badge has `pointer-events: none`, so it never eats the 44 px collapse toggle; the focus ring wins over a state ring.
- **Minimap:** a collapsed group is filled `--line-strong` (not `--raised`, the minimap's own background). Its accessible name counts every card with a state word, hidden members included: "Minimap, 1 node needs attention" / "Minimap, N nodes need attention".
- **The minimap follows the light theme:** React Flow runs with `colorMode="dark"` fixed, so its minimap paints from `--xy-minimap-*-props`, which beat a plain background. `.tc-minimap` sets `--xy-minimap-background-color-props: var(--raised)` and `--xy-minimap-mask-background-color-props: var(--backdrop)`.

## 2026-10-19 Several finish steps on one output (gate finding C1)

- **The bug:** the walk refused any file output with more than one edge, so `examples/workflows/track-cleanup-plugins.json` (Sonarr rescan, Jellyfin refresh and ntfy all off Replace original) failed every job at run time (`replace: output file has 3 connections; a file follows one`) although validation accepted it and `plugins.md` promised it. Plugin finish nodes have no outputs, so they could not be chained instead.
- **Decision (option (a) of the finding):** a file output may feed several nodes when **every** node it feeds is a finish node. The walk collects every finish node reachable from that output (each followed by the output a file leaves it by: the size gate's `pass`, a bypassed node's first) and runs them as if they were chained in phase order: a node runs after the finish nodes wired before it; among the nodes free to run, the lower phase (`gate → write → library-manager → media-server → notify`) goes first, then the node further left on the canvas (`position.x`), then the higher one (`position.y`), then the node earlier in `graph.nodes` (last resort, for nodes on the same spot). Canvas position was chosen (Jan, via the controller) because people can see it; the order nodes were added cannot be seen. A finish node without a phase (none in core or plugins) runs last. The path ends with them. Everything downstream is unchanged because it only sees the order: the actions list is in phase order, so the write runs before the plugin steps, `problemsSoFar` carries earlier problems into later steps, `afterReplace` runs right after the write, and the pipeline's phase checks never fire.
- **Size gate:** a gate among the shared finish nodes still has its `else` walked apart. The `else` output may itself feed several finish nodes (the same ordering; anything else fails the walk with `only finish steps can follow the size gate's No branch`, and a writing step there still fails the build); `pass` may too.
- **Refusals:** an output shared with any non-finish node fails the walk at the node it leaves (`output <port> has <n> connections; only finish steps can share an output`); a non-finish node behind one of the shared finish nodes fails at that node (`<node> comes after a finish node; only finish nodes may follow one`, as `FINISH_ORDER` says at save).
- **Validation `OUTPUT_MULTIPLE_EDGES` (error, on the node whose output is shared):** `<node>: output <label> has <n> connections; only finish steps can share an output (a file follows one path)`, for a file output with two or more edges where a target of known type is not a finish node. Data outputs are exempt. Added to `ISSUE_CODES` (so the API, the contract and the MCP tools carry it) and shown in the editor like `FINISH_ORDER` ("not allowed here").
- **Existing workflows:** a saved workflow that fans out to non-finish nodes now shows an error (`OUTPUT_MULTIPLE_EDGES`), its active version is `runnable: false`, and queueing a run returns 422 `WORKFLOW_INVALID` instead of every job failing at run time.
- **Follow-ups (after review):**
  - Shared-output issues are now one per offending edge, with both `edgeId` and `nodeId`, and their codes are in the web's `EDGE_CODES`. So the editor draws those lines broken and badges the node. The message names the target (`…has 2 connections, one to Keep languages; …`), so two issues on one node read differently in the issues panel and over the MCP.
  - **`SIZE_GATE_SHARED_OUTPUT` (error, on the gate and its edge):** "The size gate must be the only step on this output; put the other steps after the gate's Yes or No." A gate among siblings would decide only for the steps wired after it, while the others ran whatever it decided. The walk refuses it too, at the gate. A bypassed gate decides nothing and may share.
  - **`OUTPUT_MULTIPLE_WRITES` (error, on each writing step and its edge):** two or more running write steps (Replace original, Move or copy) on one shared output.
  - **`NO_WRITE_PATH`:** finish nodes sharing an output count as one set, so a sibling of Replace original (a notify off the same Run node output, say) is no longer warned as "never writes the result".
- **Sub-workflows** cannot hold finish nodes, so a shared output inside one is always an error (validation and walk). A Run node's output in the parent may feed several finish nodes like any other.
- **Tests:** walk (three off Replace original in reverse wiring order; chains mixed with fan-out; same-phase nodes added in one order and placed in the other; wiring beats position; the gate's pass and else; Run node output; refusals), pipeline (run order and `problemsSoFar` after the write; two plugin steps on a failing gate's No), validation, and `engine/examples-walk.test.ts`, which walks every file in `examples/workflows` through `walkDecide`/`walkBuild` with fake plugin nodes (it reproduced the gate failure before the fix) and checks that no two same-phase finish nodes on one output share a canvas spot.
- **Same-phase order:** Sonarr or Radarr before Bazarr (both `library-manager`): `plugins.md` says to place Sonarr's rescan left of Bazarr's (or above it in one column).

## 2026-10-19 UI round 3, core part (C4, C2 / #37, #36, #35)

- **A replace waiting for free space says so (gate finding C4).** `replaceOriginal` still waits (cancellable) until the original's filesystem has the replacement's size plus `FREE_SPACE_MARGIN_BYTES` (5 GiB) free; nothing about whether or how long it waits changed. New: on every check while short it tells the pipeline (`ReplaceDeps.onSpaceWait`), which sets the job's `reason` to `waiting for 5.4 GB free on /media/shows (5.1 GB free)` (decimal units; the path is the library root, the place people know, not the mount point: finding the mount point would add disk calls to the replace's pinned, fault-injected sequence) and clears it to `null` when the wait is over. The server log gets one `replace waits for free space` line when the wait starts and one `replace stopped waiting for free space` (`outcome: space free` or `stopped`, `waitedSeconds`) when it ends. `job.changed` now carries `reason` (contract: required, nullable), so the Jobs list, the phone card and the job page show the sentence live in place of "Replacing the original".
- **Deep health: the `queue` check reports it, not `scratchSpace`.** `scratchSpace` is about `TRANSCODA_TRANSCODE_DIR`; the wait is on the media filesystem and it stops the run, which is what `queue` stands for. While any replace waits (the engine's in-memory `SpaceWaits`; a restart ends every wait and the sweep settles the jobs), `queue` is `fail` and the `deep health check failed` log line names every waiting job with its wait and since when. The body stays names and ok/fail only. This fires at once, without the stall window: a space wait never ends by itself.
- **Open (for Jan):** a job waiting for space keeps its lane, so the other jobs of that lane stay planned (`Planned · waiting for the encoder` / `quick lane`) until space is free. Unchanged on purpose. Releasing the lane would let the others encode meanwhile, but each would add an output to the transcode dir and then wait for the same space.
- **A plugin finish step's own answer is on the job (gate finding C2, #37).** After a plugin finish step runs, the pipeline appends a `finish` trace entry for that node (`source: plugin`, `port: null`, no findings) with the step's message: `Radarr lists …`, `asked Jellyfin to rescan …`, or `failed: <message>` when it failed (still a problem or a warning as before). A best-effort step that failed shows its trace card as failed while the job ends `done` with a warning: the card says what that step did, the job's state says what happened to the file. The message is the one the plugin process already redacted with that plugin's secrets (`redactResult` in `plugins/process.ts`, the same text the `plugin finish step` log line prints); nothing new is redacted or added. The host checks a plugin's `run` answer with zod (`ok` true or false, `message` a string of at most 2000 characters, `retryable` an optional boolean); anything else is a non-retryable failure (`plugin <name> sent an invalid run answer: …`), and a finish entry's reason is capped at 2000 characters. The job page's Decisions list shows the answer in place of the planned text ("Finished: Radarr lists …"; the planned `rescan after the write` entry is hidden once an answer follows it in that attempt), and the trace card shows it as the node's reason. Size-gate decisions ignore plugin entries, so an answer is never read as a gate's branch. `get_job` (MCP) lists every trace entry under `decisions` as before, so the answer is there, and its text adds a `Finish steps:` line with each finish step's last entry.
- **Trace canvas: cards grown by their trace rows no longer cover each other (#36).** `spreadCards` (`editor/trace/traceLayout.ts`) runs on the measured cards of the job page's flow view: a main card covered by a grown card above it in its column moves below it (24 px gap); a data card is a side input and takes its own offset instead, moving up clear of the card below it (or down when it sits below the card it collides with), so the main row stays aligned. Group members keep their place; nothing moves until the cards are measured, and the first fit waits for the spread. The editor is unchanged.
- **Node-kind words in sentence case (#35).** `KIND_WORD` is `Trigger`, `Condition`, `Action`, `Finish`, `Data`, `Sub-workflow`, `Workflow`; `KindEyebrow` wraps only the kind words (`Action`, and `plugin` in "Finish · plugin jellyfin 1.2.0") in `.eyebrow-kind-word`, which uppercases them, so a plugin name and version keep their case. The palette headings and the node search's kind column uppercase by CSS too; the cards' eyebrow already did. The source-copy test has no exceptions left; accessible names now read "Action Keep languages".

## 2026-10-19 A restored original and its stale recycle row (gate finding C5)

- **The bug:** after an original is moved back from the recycle folder by hand (`mv -f .transcoda-recycle/<rel> <rel>`, the documented one-file rollback), its `recycled` row stays. A forced replace of that file moves the new original to the same recycle path, then fails to record it (`UNIQUE constraint failed: recycled.path`), recovers, and the job ends *done with a problem*. The row also kept the old job, size and time, so retention and the recycled bytes were off.
- **Decision: the new row replaces the stale one.** `insertRecycled` upserts on `path` and returns the row it replaced. A row for the same path can only be stale: the replace never moves an original onto a taken path, so a row there whose file is gone or changed was left by a manual move. Pruning stale rows up front was rejected: it would need a stat per row before every replace, and the upsert covers the only path where it matters. `recordRecycledRow` (`engine/recycle.ts`) logs one warning when it replaced another job's row: `replaced a stale recycle row; its original had left the recycle folder` with `path`, `job`, `staleJob`, `staleRecycledAt`. The replace (`durableReplaceWrites`, which now takes the logger), sidecar recycling and the startup sweep all use it; the sweep no longer skips recording when a row of an *earlier* job sits at the copy path (it did, leaving the stale row).
- **Decision: a restored original stays `already_processed`.** A `done` result's fingerprint is the original's (the replacement has its own and is walked again on the next run, ending `nothing_to_do`), so a match on a `done` result means the original is back. Making it eligible again was rejected: moving an original back is a rollback, and the next scheduled or folder-wide run would redo the change the person just undid. "A second run has nothing to do" holds either way. The job's reason now says what happened and what to do: `already done on <ISO>; this file is the original from before then (moved back from the recycle folder?); run with force to process it again` (`alreadyProcessedReason` in `db/repos/jobs.ts`, used by the queue and by the pipeline's re-pin check). Other outcomes keep `already <outcome> on <ISO>`. The web's `reasonOf` keeps the text after `; `: "Processed on 1 Oct 2026: replaced; this file is the original …".
- **Docs:** `copy-run.md`'s rollback note said a restored file has a new fingerprint and is processed again; it does not, and now says so.
- **Open (for Jan):** the web's Run dialog has no force option, so "run with force" means the API (`force: true`) or the MCP tool.

## 2026-10-21 Plugin settings patterns never run on the server (sandbox review H2)

- **The problem:** the server built zod copies of a plugin's settings JSON Schema with `z.fromJSONSchema` (`plugins/settings.ts` for save/review, `nodes/remote.ts` for node settings, used by workflow validation, upgrade and the walk). A plugin-authored `pattern` became a `RegExp` run on the event loop: `^([a-f0-9]+)*X$` against 26 characters took ~12 s.
- **Decision: the server copy drops every regex-bearing keyword.** `serverSettingsSchema` (`nodes/plugin-settings-schema.ts`) removes `pattern`, `format` and `patternProperties` at every depth (a dropped `patternProperties` also drops an `additionalProperties: false` beside it, so keys it allowed stay allowed) before `fromJSONSchema`. Shape, type, required, enum, numeric and length bounds and defaults still apply. The host's validation with the plugin's own zod stays authoritative (it runs before every hook and on save while the plugin runs). Consequence: a save while the plugin is not running, and the server-side node settings check in the editor and validation, no longer report a pattern or format mismatch; the host's own zod checks them when the plugin next gets the settings. The schema reported to the web is unchanged (verbatim).
- **Backstop:** every string in the server copy is capped at 64 KiB (`MAX_SETTINGS_STRING`; a tighter plugin `maxLength` stays). A test fails if any other server file calls `fromJSONSchema`.

## 2026-10-21 A plugin cannot take the server down through its channel (sandbox review H1)

- **The problem:** the host shares an address space with the plugin bundle, and fd 3 (the IPC channel) is writable from plugin code (`--permission` does not gate writes to open fds). One malformed frame written there made Node's channel reader throw inside its own socket callback in the server: `Unable to deserialize cloned data` with `serialization: 'advanced'`. The server died, and died again on every boot while the plugin stayed enabled.
- **Not enough: `serialization: 'json'`.** Verified on Node 24.21: Node's json channel reader throws an uncatchable `SyntaxError` from the same callback for a non-JSON line, so switching serialization alone still crashes the server.
- **Decision: the server frames the channel itself.** `forkHost` spawns the host with a plain pipe on fd 3 (`stdio[3] = 'pipe'`, never `'ipc'`, no `fork`) and `NODE_CHANNEL_FD=3`, `NODE_CHANNEL_SERIALIZATION_MODE=json`, so the host keeps `process.send`/`on('message')` on a json channel (Node deletes both variables from the host's `process.env`). The server reads newline-delimited JSON in `plugins/channel.ts`; a line that is not JSON, or longer than `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` + 64 KiB characters, is a `bad-frame`. The protocol was JSON values already, so nothing is lost; the test harness (`testing/spawn.ts`) uses `json` too.
- **A bad frame stops that plugin for good:** new exit reason `protocol` (`broke the message protocol: malformed message frame: …`), the host is killed, a start fails with it, and the supervisor does not restart it (state `stopped`); the admin restarts or updates it.
- **Backstop in `main.ts`** (`process-guards.ts`): an unhandled rejection is logged and the server keeps running; an uncaught exception is logged with its stack and the server exits 1 after closing the database (its state is unknown).
- **Crash-loop guard** (migration 0012, `plugin_start_attempts`; numbered 0008 on its branch, renumbered after main's 0008–0011 when merging): each boot counts every enabled stored plugin it starts; a clean stop or `stableMs` (5 min) of uptime clears all counts, and enabling a plugin clears its own. A plugin counted `maxCrashes` (3) boots in a row is disabled in the database instead of started, with the state reason `disabled: the server stopped unexpectedly 3 times in a row while this plugin was starting or running; enable it to try again`. The server cannot tell which plugin killed it, so an unrelated crash loop within 5 min of boot disables every enabled plugin; that is the safe side. Dev plugins are not counted.

## 2026-10-21 The fetch proxy pins the address it checked (sandbox review M1)

- **The problem:** the proxy checked the origin as a hostname string and then let `http(s).request` resolve the name again on connect, and again on every redirect hop. A resolver that answers differently per lookup (DNS rebinding, or a compromised internal resolver) could point an allow-listed name at 127.0.0.1, 169.254.169.254 or the Docker network.
- **Decision: resolve once per hop, check every answer, connect to that address.** `FetchProxy.pin` resolves the hop's host (`dns.lookup`, `all: true`), refuses the request if any answer is forbidden, and answers the socket's `lookup` with the checked address; the url keeps the name, so Host, TLS SNI and certificate checks are unchanged. IP literals are checked without a lookup. A 429 retry and each redirect hop are pinned afresh. Classification lives in `plugins/address-policy.ts`.
- **Policy (behaviour change):** loopback (127/8, ::1, 0.0.0.0/8, ::, IPv4-mapped/NAT64 forms), link-local (169.254/16, fe80::/10), cloud metadata (169.254.169.254, 100.100.100.200, fd00:ec2::254) and multicast/reserved are always refused (`refused`, `<host> resolves to …`). Private ranges (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 100.64/10, fc00::/7) are allowed only when the allow-listed origin's host is itself private: a private IP literal, a single-label name (Docker service) or a LAN suffix (`.local`, `.lan`, `.home`, `.home.arpa`, `.internal`, `.intranet`, `.localdomain`, `.corp`, `.private`, `.ts.net`). The allow-list stays the boundary; this only narrows it. Consequences: an integration configured as `http://localhost:8989` or `http://127.0.0.1:…` no longer works (use the host's LAN address), and a public name served by split-horizon DNS to a LAN address is refused (use the LAN name or IP).
- Tests stand a loopback server in for a LAN or public one through `FetchProxyDeps.classify`/`resolve` (`PluginServiceDeps.fetchAddresses`); production never sets them.

## 2026-10-21 Plugin memory and slot caps (sandbox review M2, M4)

- **The problem:** nothing bounded how many plugin hosts ran at once (N enabled plugins = N processes of up to `rssMb` each), `plugins.limits.rssMb` accepted up to 8192, the shipped compose had `mem_limit` commented out, and a cancelled call whose plugin ignored `cancel` kept its host (and its memory) until the call's full timeout (30 s, 5 min for a finish `run`).
- **Decision: a cap on live hosts.** New limit `maxHosts` (default 16, settings row `plugins.limits.maxHosts`, at most 256). `PluginSupervisor.launch` counts other plugins with a live process (one in restart backoff does not count) and refuses the start past the cap: state `stopped`, reason `too many plugins running (at most 16); disable another plugin or raise plugins.limits.maxHosts`, returned as a `bad-manifest` start error like the other refusals. It is not retried by itself (a crash restart that finds the cap full stops too); enable or restart the plugin once a slot is free. A candidate (update) of a running plugin replaces its process and is allowed at the cap; one for a stopped plugin needs a free slot.
- **Decision: `rssMb` is bounded at 2048** (was 8192); a stored value above it falls back to the 256 MB default. `heapMb` keeps its bound of 4096 (an RSS kill comes first anyway).
- **Decision: a cancelled call gets a short grace.** New limit `cancelGraceMs` (default 5 s, at most 60 s). On cancel, `PluginProcess` replaces the call's timeout with a reaper at `min(cancelGraceMs, time left on the call)`; an answer in that window disarms it, otherwise the host is killed (exit reason `timed out after 5 s in <hook>`, warning `cancelled plugin call did not stop within its grace period`) and the supervisor restarts it as after any timeout. Behaviour change: a plugin that keeps working after its job is cancelled for more than 5 s is now restarted instead of being given the full call timeout.
- **Compose:** `docker/docker-compose.yml` ships `mem_limit: 4g` with the sizing formula (server ~512 MB + 256 MB per enabled plugin + ffmpeg); the README snippet and `docs/env.md` say so. The CT 111 deploy compose keeps its own 3g.

## 2026-10-21 Signed catalog index is monotonic (sandbox review L1)

- **The problem:** the catalog verified the signature over the index bytes but not its age, so whoever controls the network path could replay an older validly signed `index.json` to hide an update or offer an older signed build.
- **Decision: mirror the blocklist.** For a catalog with signing keys, a freshly fetched and verified index whose `generated` is older than the cached verified index's is refused: the cache is not overwritten, the cached index keeps being served, the listing carries the error `catalog index older than the cached one (generated <new>, cached <old>)`, and the server warns `refused a catalog index older than the cached one`. An equal `generated` is accepted. Unsigned catalogs are unchanged (they have no integrity to protect). Limit: the first fetch, or one after the cache is cleared or tampered with, has nothing to compare against.

## 2026-10-21 Library trigger

Built on branch `library-trigger`. Jan's answers (2026-10-21) took every recommended default; the spec is `docs/plans/2026-10-21-library-trigger-design.md` (local).

### The binding

- **The active version's Library input is the one binding between a library and a workflow.** `libraries.default_workflow_id` is gone. The binding is derived from the active graph and cached as `workflows.active_library_id`, unique among live rows (partial index `workflows_active_library`, `deleted_at IS NULL`). Only migration 0008, `setActiveVersion` and `clearActiveVersion` write it; save, create, duplicate, restore, the upgrades and the queue never do.
- **One active workflow per library, no take-over.** Activating a version whose Library input names a library another live active workflow feeds is `LIBRARY_TAKEN` (409): "<Library> is fed by <Workflow> v<N>. Deactivate it or point this Library input elsewhere." The feeder's own row never counts (re-activation, rollback to an older version on the same library). A concurrent activation that loses the unique index answers the same 409. Activating a Manual or Webhook version clears the binding.
- **`LIBRARY_MISSING`** is a warning issue ("Library input: no library with this id", `settingPath: libraryId`) on a Library input naming no live library (a soft-deleted one counts as missing). It blocks only activation (400 `LIBRARY_MISSING`); saving, validating and dry runs go on. An empty `libraryId` stays the `INVALID_SETTINGS` error.
- **Read fields:** `Library.feeds` (`{ workflowId, name, version }` or null), `WorkflowSummary.feedsLibrary` (the live library the active version takes files from; null for a deleted library or a sub-workflow) and `WorkflowSummary.libraryInput` (the newest version's Library input id and live library name, `name: null` when none; a plan addition, because Dry run… needs the newest version's library). `summary()` builds the library lookups once per `list()`.
- **Queue:** `jobs.queue` without `workflowId` uses the library's feeder; none → `NO_FEEDING_WORKFLOW` (400, replaces `NO_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW`). A real run uses the feeder's active version, a dry run the newest (or `workflowVersion`). An explicit `workflowId` is unchanged, including the real-to-dry note for an inactive one.
- **`defaultWorkflowId` is removed from the API.** Sending it to library create or update is a 400 whose issue says "defaultWorkflowId was removed: a workflow's Library input chooses its library" (`REMOVED_DEFAULT_WORKFLOW`); `DEFAULT_WORKFLOW_INVALID` is gone.
- **Scopes unchanged.** `libraries.dismissNotices` needs `jobs:manage`; activation stays session-only. `TRIGGER_COUNT` stays at exactly one trigger; Manual input is unchanged.

### Migration 0008

- Frozen SQL: every name is literal, graphs are read with `JSON.parse` only. `Migration.up` gains an optional `log` argument; existing migrations keep `up(db)`.
- **Phase 1, defaults** (workflows by name, id; a default's libraries by name, id): an active default whose Library input already names one of its libraries is bound there with no new version (this prefers the library the node names over the first by name, a plan decision). Any other active default (Manual, Webhook, empty or missing library) gets a new version with the trigger swapped to a Library input on its first library, activated by `system:migration` and bound; a newer draft gets a swapped copy too, so it stays newest. The swap keeps the node's id, position, label, group, bypass and every edge. New versions carry the note "Library input set to <library> (from its default workflow)". An inactive default gets one swapped copy of its newest version and is neither activated nor bound.
- **Phase 2, node claims** (by name, id; the tie-break between two active workflows naming the same free library is the name, a plan decision): an active workflow whose Library input names a library not yet bound takes it; one naming a bound library is deactivated.
- **Notices**, stored in the settings row `libraries.migrationNotices` and logged as `Migration 0008: <notice>`:
  - N1 "<L> has no workflow now: its default, <W>, takes files from <T> instead. Add a Library input to a workflow for <L>." (a shared default binds the first library by name).
  - N2 "<L> has no workflow now: its default, <W>, has a Library input that takes files from <L2>." (the node wins).
  - N3 "<W> was deactivated: its Library input takes files from <L>, which <X> already feeds."
  - N4 "<L> has no workflow now: its default, <W>, could not be switched to a Library input. Add one to <W> by hand." (corrupt JSON, zero or two triggers, a plugin trigger: the workflow is left as it is; a plan addition).
  - N5 "<L> has no workflow now: its default, <W>, has no active version. Activate <W> v<N> to feed <L>." (a plan addition).
- Soft-deleted libraries and soft-deleted default workflows are skipped without a notice. `libraries` is rebuilt without the column (the 0005 recipe).
- **Notices API:** `libraries.list` carries `notices`; `POST /libraries/notices/dismiss` clears them (a plan addition).

### Web

- **Editor:** the Library field's hint is "Files from this library go to this workflow once a version is active." Activate is blocked by a `LIBRARY_MISSING` issue ("Pick a library that exists first"). A draft whose Library input differs from the fed library shows the info banner "Real runs still take files from <from> (v<N> active). Activate this version to switch to <to>." or, with no library in the draft, "… Activating this version stops that." (the second sentence is a plan decision).
- **Activate dialog:** "From now on, Scan now on <Library> runs v<N>. …" with a library; "No library sends files to v<N>; it runs only when you use Run on a file." without one (a plan decision). A library fed by another workflow shows the `LIBRARY_TAKEN` text and disables Activate; the server's `LIBRARY_TAKEN` and `LIBRARY_MISSING` show their texts. When the extra fetches fail, the library sentence is left out and the server still decides.
- **Libraries page:** a `Feeds` column ("<Workflow> v<N>, active", else "—" and "Add a Library input to a workflow"); no workflow select in the library dialog; Scan now disabled without a feeder ("No active workflow takes files from this library") and queued without `workflowId`; `NO_FEEDING_WORKFLOW` toasts "No active workflow takes files from <Library> any more" and reloads; a "Dry run…" row action lists the workflows whose newest Library input names the library; deleting a fed library adds "<Workflow> v<N> will stop receiving files."; migration notices show under "Libraries changed when Transcoda was updated" with Dismiss.
- **Run on a file** (editor header after "Dry run on a file", and the Workflows row menu): a real run of the active version on one file in any library; that file's library supplies the recycle folder named in the confirm ("Applies <Workflow> v<N> (active) to <path>. The original moves to <folder>."). Disabled without an active version ("Activate a version to run for real") or with one that can't run. It never forces, never sends a version and never saves the draft (a plan decision: a real run uses the active version).

### MCP and docs

- `list_libraries` names the feeder (`fed by <Workflow> v<N>` / `no workflow takes files from it`) and returns `feeds`; `list_workflows` and `get_workflow` briefs carry `feedsLibrary` and the line ends with `, feeds <Library>`; `dry_run` without `workflowId` uses the feeder and answers `NO_FEEDING_WORKFLOW` when there is none. The instructions gain one sentence on the binding; the never-sentences are unchanged and MCP still never activates or runs for real.
- `core:input-library`'s Revisit note keeps only the export/import remap of `libraryId`.
- `copy-run.md` no longer sets a default workflow; it says to add a Library input naming the library and activate it. `mcp.md`, `env.md` and the README follow.

## 2026-10-21 Process again from the UI (#38)

- **Job page:** every finished job (any final state, dry runs too) has "Process again" and "Dry run again" under its header. Both call `jobs.queue` with the job's `filePath`, `workflowId` and `force: true`, then open the new job. "Process again" first loads `workflows.get` and the library together (the button is `aria-busy` with a status text meanwhile) and opens the dialog only once both are back, so a screen reader hears the final text. The dialog asks `Process <file> again?` and names the recycle folder the current file goes to if it is rewritten (the library's `recycleFolder`, or `<root>/.transcoda-recycle`; "of the library" when the library cannot be read). With no active version it reads "<workflow> has no active version, so this is a dry run of v<current>; nothing on disk changes", is titled "Dry run <file> again?" and its button is "Start dry run" (sent as a dry run). When the active version cannot run it says so and the primary button is disabled. A real run sends no `workflowVersion` (it always runs the active version). "Dry run again" does not ask and plans the workflow's active version (`workflowVersion` from `workflows.get`), or the newest when none is active or that read fails.
- **Scan dialog (Libraries → Scan now):** a force checkbox sends `force: true` with the scan (real or "Dry run instead"). For a real scan it reads "Also re-run files v<N> already finished (they may be rewritten)" (N is the active version); for a dry scan "Also plan files v<N> already finished" (N is the newest). It is hidden when the scan is blocked (the active version cannot run). It starts unchecked every time the dialog opens and is not remembered. Without it the request is unchanged (`force` omitted). Focus still starts on Cancel (now `data-autofocus`). The checkbox label uses `.lib-check` (the radios keep `.lib-radio`).
- Force is per request only: nothing is stored beyond the run's existing `force` column. This closes the C5 open point above.
- **With the library trigger (merged into `library-trigger`):** Scan now is always a real run of the feeder's active version, so its checkbox always reads "Also re-run files v<N> already finished (they may be rewritten)" with N from `Library.feeds`; it is still hidden when blocked, and "Dry run v<N> instead" sends no `force`. The "Dry run…" dialog has its own checkbox above the workflow list, "Also plan files the newest version already finished" (the dialog plans each workflow's newest version), unchecked each time it opens; picking a workflow sends `force: true` with the dry run when it is checked.

## 2026-10-21 Side nav divider runs the full height (#42)

- The line between the side nav and the page is now the main column's left border (`.shell-main`), not the nav's right border. The nav is sticky and only as tall as its items, so its border stopped under the last item; the main column stretches to the shell body, which fills the window, so the line reaches the bottom on short and long pages, in the editor's rail too. No divider at phone width (the nav is a drawer there), as before.

## 2026-10-21 Percent fields move in whole steps (UI list, size gate "Saved at least")

- **The bug:** the size gate's `minSavingPercent` is `z.number().min(0).max(100)`, so its slider had `step="any"` and dragging stored values like `11.581583`.
- **Decision: whole percent in the editor, no `.int()` in the schema.** Saved graphs can already hold fractions (the schema has always allowed them and the slider wrote them), so `.int()` would turn those graphs invalid and need a node version bump with an upgrade. Instead the editor snaps: a number field whose unit is `%` steps 1 (input and slider), a typed or slid value rounds to a whole percent, and a stored fraction is shown rounded (inspector, on-node input and summary). The stored value changes only when someone edits it; the engine still compares against the stored number.
- **Other fields:** integer fields already stepped 1 (Quality CRF, bitrates, heights, days, version). A fractional field with a slider now moves in a hundredth of its range (`schema-fields.ts` `numberSpec`); the unbounded MB sizes of "File matches" keep any value.

## 2026-10-21 Grouped codec picker (UI list #40)

- **The field:** "Video is → Codecs" was free-text chips. It is now a picker: chips show the plain name and the ffmpeg id, the stored value stays the list of ffmpeg ids, and a typed name the table does not have is still accepted (`Add "…"`).
- **Decision: a schema marker picks the control.** `core:video-is` marks `codecs` with `x-transcoda: 'video-codecs'` (as `secret()`/`url()` mark plugin fields); the editor maps `video-codecs` / `audio-codecs` to the `codec-list` field. Matching on the key name was rejected (a plugin's `codecs` may mean anything). The marker only adds a JSON Schema keyword: validation, the node version and saved graphs are unchanged.
- **Groups** (`apps/web/src/data/codecs.ts`): video "Efficient (target)" HEVC, AV1; "Common" H.264, VP9; "Older (worth re-encoding)" the rest. Presets "Efficient (HEVC, AV1)" and "Everything else" (Common + Older), plus "Add all" per group. Audio "Lossless", "Surround (lossy)", "Stereo-friendly"; DTS-HD MA shares `dts` with DTS (the profile is not told apart by codec), so both options read selected.
- **No audio codec list exists yet** (`core:has-stream` has one free `codec` string; the encoders use enums), so the audio groups wait for the first field marked `audio-codecs`.
- **Accessibility:** the search box is the combobox of one multi-select listbox with labelled option groups; arrows, Home/End and Enter work from the search box, a click or Enter toggles and the picker stays open until Done.

## 2026-10-21 Backup on build change

- **The gap:** a database was backed up only before pending migrations. An update without a migration still changes data at start (workflow upgrades run right after `migrate`), with no copy to go back to.
- **Decision: back up when the build changes.** In `createDeps`, right after `migrate` and before anything else writes (plugin start, workflow upgrades, the engine's sweep), `backupOnBuildChange` (`db/build-backup.ts`) compares the build id with the `app.lastBuildId` settings row. Different, or no row while the database already existed (`migrate`'s `from > 0`: an install from before this check): a `VACUUM INTO` backup into the same `backups/` folder, pruned with the others (`KEEP_BACKUPS`, see the review fixes below), one log line `Build changed (<old|none> → <new>); backed up database to <file>`. Then the new id is stored. No backup when `migrate` took one this start, or for a brand-new database. A failed backup stops the start, as a failed migration backup does. The id is stored after the backup, so the backup holds the old id: restoring it and starting the same build backs up once more, which is harmless.
- **Build id:** `TRANSCODA_BUILD_ID` when set (the image has an optional `TRANSCODA_BUILD_ID` build-arg, empty by default; 1-64 of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, else a `ConfigError`), else the first 12 hex characters of the sha256 of the running server file, resolved from `import.meta.url` in `bootstrap.ts`, which in the image is the single bundle `/app/server/main.js`. The version is `0.0.0` until releases and the image is built from `git archive` (no `.git`), so neither can tell builds apart; the hash changes with every server code change and needs nothing from CI. A web-only or plugin-host-only change keeps the id (neither writes the database). From source (`pnpm dev`) the hashed file is `bootstrap.ts` itself, so dev starts rarely back up; set `TRANSCODA_BUILD_ID` to force one. An unreadable file logs a warning and skips the check.
- **Names:** the format stays `transcoda-<YYYYMMDD-HHmmss>-v<schema>.sqlite`; build-change backups add `-build`, manual ones `-manual` (`v` is the current schema for these, the schema before the migration for migration backups). A name already taken gets `-2`, `-3`, …, so a second backup within the same second never fails on `VACUUM INTO` (fixes the per-second known issue). `listBackups` sorts by the stamp, then the counter (no counter counts as 1), then the name, so pruning stays oldest-first across reasons; names it cannot parse sort by their text as before.
- **API and UI:** `system.backups.list` (`GET /system/backups`, `read` scope) returns `{ backups: [{ name, size, createdAt, reason }], keep }`, newest first, file names only (never a server path). `system.backups.create` (`POST /system/backups`) takes a `-manual` backup; `requireHumanSessionWith('Backups are taken in the UI')` refuses every API token, and there is no MCP tool. `createdAt` is read from the stamp in the name (local time), not the file's mtime. Settings → About shows a Database backups panel: each file's name, reason (Before migration, Build change, Manual), size and time, **Back up now**, and the restore steps (stop the container, copy the backup over `transcoda.sqlite`, delete `-wal` and `-shm`, start). `docs/env.md` has a Database backups section with the same steps.
- **Review fixes (same day):** pruning keeps 5 *per reason* (migration, build, manual), so build-change or manual backups never evict the pre-migration copy that `SchemaTooNewError` points to. Backups are atomic: `VACUUM INTO ?` (a bound parameter, no interpolated path) writes `<name>.part` (which `BACKUP_PATTERN` does not match), the file is fsynced and renamed into place; on failure the `.part` is removed and the error rethrown, and a stale `.part` from a crash is removed before the next attempt. `system.backups.create` refuses a second manual backup within 30 s (`RATE_LIMITED`, "A backup was taken moments ago; try again in N s"; per app instance, in memory). The panel's button uses `aria-disabled` while a backup runs (focus stays), the "Backed up to <name>" line is visible, and a rate-limit refusal shows the server's message.
- **Download (same day, Jan's request):** `GET /api/v1/system/backups/<name>/download` is a plain Hono route beside the contract (`http/backup-download.ts`; it streams a file, which the contract's JSON procedures do not), so it is not in the OpenAPI spec or the RPC client. A backup is treated like the database: session only (token → 403 "Backups are downloaded in the UI", no MCP tool), no CSRF header for the GET but the cookie is `SameSite=Lax` and `sameOriginOk` refuses `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or a foreign `Origin`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. `findBackupFile` serves a name only when it matches `BACKUP_PATTERN`, is made of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` without `..` (also keeps `Content-Disposition` safe), is in `listBackups` (so never a `.part`), resolves directly inside `backups/` and is a regular file; anything else is a 404 with no detail. Hardening after review: the file is opened with `O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW` and checked with `fstat` on that fd (regular file, size for `Content-Length`), then streamed with `createReadStream('', { fd, autoClose: true })`, so a symlink or other file swapped in after the name checks is refused at open (no check-then-read race). HEAD (which Hono answers with the GET handler, dropping the body uncancelled) closes the fd at once and returns headers only, so it never leaks a descriptor. The panel has a Download link per row, a note that a backup holds the password and token hashes and the plugin secrets (keep it private; plugin secrets are AES-256-GCM under a key derived from `secret.key`, not in the backup, so unreadable without it), and the restore note now says to restore next to the same `secret.key`.
- **`SchemaTooNewError` hint:** names the newest *migration* backup (the copy from before the upgrade that made the schema too new); only when there is none, the newest backup of any kind, since a build-change or manual backup may already be at the newer schema.
- **Not done:** no delete or restore from the UI; restoring stays a manual step with Transcoda stopped.

## 2026-10-21 Plan-only dry runs say when the size gate decides (UI list #41)

Approved plan: `docs/plans/2026-10-21-dryrun-depends-on-size.md` (local).

- **Server, one source.** In a plan-only dry run whose walked finish actions hold a size gate before the first write (replace, move or copy), the stored plan carries `dependsOnSize: { minSavingPercent }` (`sizeGateDecides`; with several gates the strictest). The job still ends `dry_run`; no new state. No gate → plain "would replace"; no write → the path never writes (as before). A gate on a branch the file did not take, or a bypassed gate, adds no action and never counts. Test-encode dry runs never carry it (they measure). Plans stored before it read as before.
- **Read fields.** `Job.dependsOnSize` and the run file's `dependsOnSize` (`{ minSavingPercent }` or null, only for `dry_run` jobs of a dry run), `RunCounts.dependsOnSize` (the part of `dry_run`, not an outcome of its own, so the outcome keys still sum to `total`), `jobs.list` `dependsOnSize: true` filter with `dependsOnSizeCount` (scope only, like `problemCount`).
- **Wording.** The gate's trace reason in a plan-only dry run is `Size gate: decides after encoding: keep if it saves less than N%` (was `depends on the result (kept original below N%)`). Job page: the result and chip read `Would replace if it saves ≥ N%` (dry-run tone and eye icon, no estimate percent); the size tile `315 MB → ? · decided after encoding` with "Test-encode this file" (a forced test-encode dry run of the same file and workflow version, then its job opens); the Replace original step `would replace the original if the size gate passes; otherwise keeps it`, and a Move or copy step `would move|copy the result to <folder> if the size gate passes; otherwise keeps it`.
- **Jobs page:** a `Depends on size` chip after `Dry run`. **Run summary** (plan-only dry runs): a `Depends on size` tile after `Would replace` (which no longer counts those files) and the line `N would replace · N depend on size · N nothing to do`; each such file's chip reads `Would replace if it saves ≥ N%`.
- **MCP:** `get_job`/`list_jobs` carry `dependsOnSize` and say `would replace if it saves ≥ N% (decided after encoding)`; `get_job`'s text adds `Depends on size: …`; `get_job`/`list_jobs` give no estimate for them; `list_runs`/`get_run` lines of a dry run count `would replace`, `depend on size` (left out when 0), `would keep original` and `nothing to do`. A size gate inside a sub-workflow cannot happen (validate refuses finish nodes there); a Run node that encodes before a top-level gate counts like any encode.
