## 2026-10-21 Test-encode dry runs

- **What stops where.** A test-encode dry run (`testEncode` on the run and job) runs probe → decide → build → encode → verify → measure for real, with the same encoder and the same verify checks as a real run, into the job's temp dir under `TRANSCODA_TRANSCODE_DIR`. It then walks the workflow's actions up to the first write (Replace original or Move or copy): every size gate before it decides on the measured size, and the first failing one ends the job `kept_original` with its own numbers (`saved only 3.0%, below 10%`; the trace takes its `else` branch). Reaching the first write, or the end of the list, ends it `dry_run` ("would replace"). Nothing after that point runs: no replace, move/copy, `.orig`, library temp file, recycle row, sidecar recycle, notify, plugin finish step, plugin job hook or webhook, whatever the workflow holds. An encode or verify failure is the dry run's failure (`failed`). Every writing step (replace, move-copy, sidecar recycle, notify, `afterReplace`) now throws if a dry run ever reaches it, so a future walk bug fails the job instead of writing. Plan-only dry runs (`testEncode` false) behave exactly as before; `execute` refuses one outright.
- **When it encodes.** `jobs.queue` takes `testEncode` only with `dryRun: true` (contract refusal: `only a dry run can test-encode`). Omitted on a dry run, the server uses `'if-single-file'`: a single file test-encodes, a folder stays plan-only. So "Dry run on a file", "Dry run another file" and the job page test-encode by default; folder and library dry runs only when "Test-encode" is ticked (Libraries → Dry run…, or beside "Dry run v<N> instead" in a blocked Scan now; unticked each time the dialog opens; a real scan never sends it). MCP `dry_run` takes `testEncode`, default `false`, and passes it explicitly, so an agent never test-encodes unless it asks. A REST caller that omits it on a single file gets the server default (test-encode), like the UI.
- **Lane.** A test-encode job takes the lane `laneFor` gives, like a real job (no priority boost); it queues behind real jobs in the encode lane.
- **Kept output.** After a passing verify the output is moved to `<transcodeDir>/test-outputs/<jobId>.<mkv|mp4>` and registered in the new `test_outputs` table (one row per job, `ON DELETE CASCADE` with the job) with `kept_until`; it survives restarts until then. The job detail carries `testOutput` (`keptUntil`, `sizeBytes`, `container`) and `testOutputNote`. The startup temp sweep skips `test-outputs/`; the store's own sweep runs at every engine start and hourly (`TEST_OUTPUT_SWEEP_MS = 3_600_000`, an unref'd timer) and removes expired outputs (note `expired`), rows whose file is gone (note `deleted: file missing`), and anything in the dir that is not a registered `<jobId>.<ext>` regular file (a symlink, a stray file or folder). Every deletion is logged (`test output deleted` with `why`, `stray test output removed`). Opening an output past its `kept_until` (play, download) is a 404 and removes it as expired at once, without waiting for the sweep.
- **Retention.** Setting "Test encodes kept for" (Settings → Queue and engine; `queue.testEncodeKeepHours`, whole hours, default 2, 0–72, `TEST_ENCODE_KEEP_HOURS_MAX = 72`). It is read when an output is kept, so a change applies to new outputs only; existing ones keep their `kept_until`. 0 deletes the output right after measuring (note `not kept: test encodes are kept for 0 hours`). An invalid stored value falls back to the default.
- **Space guard.** The margin is `max(5, TRANSCODA_HEALTH_MIN_FREE_GIB)` GiB free in the transcode dir, one function (`scratchMinFreeBytes`) shared with the deep health's `scratchSpace` check. An output that would leave less free is deleted at once (note `not kept: scratch space low`). Before a real job encodes, the oldest kept outputs are deleted while less is free (note `deleted: scratch space was needed`); this never throws, so kept outputs never refuse or delay a real job. Keeping never throws either: a failure is logged, keeps nothing and notes why (`not kept: <error>`).
- **Access, and why a plain route.** `GET`/`HEAD /api/v1/jobs/:id/test-output` streams a file, so it is a plain Hono route beside the contract (like the backup download), not an oRPC procedure. A person's browser session only: no session 401, every API token 403 whatever its scopes, cross-site or a foreign Origin 403 `CSRF_FAILED`. Addressed by job id only: the path comes from the registered row, opened `O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and checked with `fstat` on that fd (a symlink, folder or FIFO in its place is a 404); `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Range is honoured for downloads and for an mp4 played inline (416 when unsatisfiable). The download name is `<file name, safe characters only>.test-encode.<ext>`. Delete is the contract's `jobs.deleteTestOutput` (`DELETE /jobs/{id}/test-output`), session only too (note `deleted`). No MCP tool plays, downloads or deletes a test output.
- **Preview.** `?play=1` on an mkv serves `<jobId>.preview.mp4` beside the output, built by ffmpeg on the first play (through a `.part` file, then renamed) and reused after. It was a fragmented-mp4 stream first, which browsers could not seek and whose duration grew while it played. ffmpeg reads the checked fd on stdin and writes a normal mp4 (`-movflags +faststart`; no subtitles or data), copying only, never encoding: the first video track, HEVC tagged `hvc1` (Safari plays no `hev1`), and the output's first audio track when its codec in the stored plan's `output` (the output's tracks in output order, a new optional plan field) is one ffmpeg 9.0.2 copies into mp4 without `-strict` (aac, mp3, opus, flac, alac, ac3, eac3). Other audio (DTS, TrueHD, PCM, anything else) is left out: the preview plays without sound. A plan stored before `output` existed leaves the audio out too (its codec is unknown, and one mp4 cannot carry would fail the build). The preview is served like an mp4 output (Range, `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, the fd opened `O_NOFOLLOW` and checked). Plays of one job share one build; at most `MAX_PREVIEW_BUILDS = 2` build at once and another answers 503 `BUSY` with `Retry-After: 5`; a build needs the scratch margin plus the output's size free, else 503 `SCRATCH_LOW`; a failed build is logged at warn, leaves nothing behind and answers 500 `PREVIEW_FAILED`. A HEAD never builds; on `?play=1` it says in `x-preview` where the preview stands (`ready`, `building`, `busy`, `scratchLow`, `none`). A client gone while the build ran gets no body (the fd is closed), and a file stream is destroyed when the request aborts (the node server never cancels a body it stopped writing). A read error on the output fails the build even when ffmpeg exits 0. The preview goes with its output (delete, expiry, eviction, a newer output for the job); the sweep keeps a registered mkv's preview and the `.part` of a build in flight, and removes any other. The plan's output tracks carry `bitDepth` (video: the encode's pixel format, else the source's) and `channels` (audio: the encode's, else the source's) when known, for the job page.
- **Job page.** The kept-output panel shows "Test output kept until HH:MM" with Play (inline player), Download and Delete now; Play and Download go at `keptUntil` without a reload. Delete now is described as `Removes the kept output; the job result stays`. A player error is checked with a HEAD: 404 shows `This test output is gone.`, anything else `Could not play the preview. Use Download to get the file instead.` While the player has no metadata yet it shows `Preparing preview…` (the first play of a long file may take a minute). Above the player it names, from the plan's output tracks, what this browser cannot play (`canPlayType` with mp4 codec strings: `This browser can't play HEVC 10-bit video.`, `This browser can't play E-AC-3 5.1 audio, so the preview plays without sound. Download the file to check the audio.`) and audio an mkv's preview leaves out (`The preview can't carry DTS 7.1 audio, so it plays without sound.`; `The preview plays without sound.` when the plan does not list the tracks). A player error names an unplayable video codec; it HEADs the play URL, and `x-preview` `busy`/`building`, `scratchLow` or `none` show `The server is busy preparing other previews. Try again in a few seconds.`, `Not enough scratch space to prepare the preview.` or `Could not prepare the preview. Use Download to get the file instead.` with a Try again button. The plan's size tile reads `Now 2.41 GB → …` while a test encode measures and `Now 2.41 GB` when it ended unmeasured. Outcome lines read `Would replace · 315 MB → 190 MB (−40%)` and `Would keep the original · saved only 3% (gate ≥ 10%)`; a test-encode run shows measured sizes instead of estimates, and its Jobs list line is the measured outcome, never `Plan ready`.
- **MCP.** `dry_run` gains `testEncode` (strict input; `test_encode` is refused), its text says `Test-encode dry run … queued` and that nothing in the library is written; `get_job`/`list_jobs` tag `[test encode]` and report `measured <n> bytes` instead of the estimate. The instructions mention that it is slow and queues behind real jobs. `never.test.ts` pins that a test-encode `dry_run` on a workflow that replaces and notifies leaves the library byte-identical, fetches nothing and records no recycle row.
- **Migration 0009** (`0009_test_encode`, frozen SQL, additive; written as 0008 and renumbered when `library-trigger`, whose 0008 merged first, was merged in): `runs.test_encode` and `jobs.test_encode` (`INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, 0 or 1), `jobs.test_output_note`, and table `test_outputs` (`job_id` primary key, `file_name`, `container` mkv or mp4, `size_bytes`, `created_at`, `kept_until`, indexed).
- **Out of scope (later):** side-by-side compare with the original; a preview while encoding.
- **With the library trigger (merged in):** the Libraries page's dry runs moved to "Dry run…" (pick a workflow); its dialog has the Test-encode box beside the force box. The job page's "Dry run again" omits `testEncode`, so a single file test-encodes by the server default.

## 2026-10-22 NVENC encodes decode on the GPU

- **Why.** On CT 111 (4 cores, RTX 3060) the GPU looked idle: Beszel shows the shader load, which NVENC is not part of, and NVENC waited on the CPU's H.264 decode. Measured with jellyfin-ffmpeg 8.1.3 on a 1080p AVC remux, 60 s, NVENC `-cq 20`: CPU decode 10.5x at about 3 cores; `-hwaccel cuda` 15.7x at 0.1 core (frames on the GPU) and, for the whole job with two DTS-HD → AAC tracks, 7x → 11.8x with 2.7 → 1.6 cores.
- **Rule.** `renderArgs` puts `-hwaccel cuda` before the source's `-i` when a converted video stream uses an `*_nvenc` encoder, unless the model's `globalArgs`/`inputArgs` already name a `-hwaccel` (then that one stands). Without `-hwaccel_output_format cuda` the decoded frames come back to system memory, so `-pix_fmt` and filters render as before; ffmpeg decodes on the CPU when the GPU has no decoder for the codec (checked with an MPEG-4 Part 2 source). QSV and VAAPI are unchanged.
- **Not done.** Keeping frames on the GPU (`-hwaccel_output_format cuda`, `scale_cuda` for pixel formats) saves the copies too but breaks CPU filters and has no CPU fallback; worth it only if the copy shows up as the limit.

## 2026-10-22 Encoding device setting

- **Where.** One global setting, Settings → Queue and engine → "Encoding device" (`engine.encodeDevice`): `auto` (default, as before), `cpu`, or `nvidia:<index>`. Not per workflow: a GPU index belongs to the machine, and workflows are exported and shared. A node that must stay on the CPU already can (its encoder setting, e.g. `libx265`).
- **Effect.** The setting filters the capabilities Encode video and validation see (`effectiveCapabilities`): `cpu` drops every hardware encoder and hwaccel, so `auto` picks software and a node that names `hevc_nvenc` is ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE; `nvidia:<n>` keeps NVENC and CUDA only and renders `-hwaccel cuda -hwaccel_device n` plus NVENC `-gpu:<i> n`. `auto` renders no device index, so a one-GPU or no-GPU machine gets the argv it had. The device is read when a job is prepared.
- **Decoding follows encoding.** No separate setting: a job decodes on the device it encodes on (with the CPU fallback of 2026-10-22 "NVENC encodes decode on the GPU").
- **Detection.** `nvidia-smi -L` at startup (`capabilities.gpus`); none detected but NVENC present counts as an unnamed GPU 0. The server sets `CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER=PCI_BUS_ID` (unless set) so ffmpeg's CUDA index is nvidia-smi's. A stored GPU that is no longer found acts as CPU only, and Settings says so.
- **Not run on a GPU.** The `-hwaccel_device`/`-gpu` flags are covered by render and pipeline tests only.

## 2026-10-23 Several GPUs encode in parallel

- **Slots.** The encode lane has encode slots (`encodeSlots` in capabilities/devices.ts, asked at every grant): with "Encoding device" `auto`, two or more GPUs from `nvidia-smi -L` and NVENC in ffmpeg, one slot per GPU (`gpu:<index>`); otherwise the one slot of before, on the pinned GPU if the setting names one. The quick lane is unchanged. Jobs keep `lane = 'encode'` on their row.
- **Device per job.** Build renders the argv before a slot is known; `execute` gets the slot's device and renders it again (`-hwaccel cuda -hwaccel_device n`, NVENC `-gpu:<i> n`), so the stored command names the GPU. A software encode takes any free slot: its argv gets no device flags, and the number of encodes at once stays the number of GPUs.
- **Pause.** A job the user paused gets its own slot back (its ffmpeg runs on that GPU); other slots go on to other jobs meanwhile. If its slot is no longer offered (the setting changed), it takes any free one. A slot no longer offered stays in the status while a job runs in it.
- **Status.** `queue.status` and `queue.changed` carry `encodeSlots: [{ id, label, jobId }]`; `lanes.encode` stays, as the first busy slot, for older clients. The queue bar names each GPU when there are several ("GPU 0 · RTX 3060: movie.mkv · GPU 1 · RTX 3060: idle · Quick lane: idle"); Settings → Lanes reads "Encode ×2 (one per GPU)".
- **CPU only and a named NVENC encoder** stay an error (ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE), as of 2026-10-22.
- **Not done.** QSV/VAAPI devices are neither listed nor scheduled per device: the render passes no VAAPI device or `hwupload`, so `*_vaapi` likely does not run at all yet. "Jobs per GPU" (more than one NVENC session per card) is left for later.
- **Not run on hardware.** No multi-GPU machine was available; the scheduler and argv are covered by tests only, and CUDA's PCI-bus order matching nvidia-smi is from NVIDIA's documentation, not observed.

## 2026-10-22 Remove duplicate audio

- **Node.** `core:remove-duplicate-audio` ("Remove duplicate audio", version 1, action, icon `copy`, no settings) works on the build model as planned so far. Meant to follow Encode audio: a film with TrueHD 5.1 and AC-3 5.1 640k in English, both encoded to AAC 2.0 160k, keeps one AAC track.
- **Duplicates.** Two kept audio tracks are duplicates when what they write has the same language (normalised; untagged and `und` are one value of their own, never a match for a real language), the same codec and the same known channel count (a converted track's target codec and channels, else the source's; a copied track's probe codec and channels; an unknown count matches nothing), the same forced flag, the same title words (below), and, for copied tracks, the same sample rate and bit depth (unknown is a value of its own; a conversion's are what the encode makes, so they only rank the sources); and neither is special. Special: a `comment`, `visual_impaired`, `hearing_impaired`, `karaoke` or `dub` disposition, or a title word starting with comment, descri or narrat, or the word AD. Bitrate is not compared. All of this is one key per track, so "duplicate" is an equivalence and the groups never depend on track order.
- **Titles (review fix, same day).** Two titles match only when their meaningful words are equal; there is no wildcard, so an untitled track matches only a title that strips to nothing. Stripped: whole format names (`Dolby Digital Plus`, `Dolby Digital`, `TrueHD`, `DTS-HD MA`, `DTS:X`, `DD+`, `E-AC-3`, `AC-3`, `HE-AAC`, `640 kbps`, `48 kHz`, `24-bit`, …), single codec and layout words (Dolby, DTS, AAC, AC3, Atmos, FLAC, PCM, stereo, mono, surround, …), plain numbers, and the track's language name and codes. Ordinary words that also occur inside format names (master, true, plus, digital, audio, x, es) are kept when they stand alone: stripping makes tracks more alike, so a doubtful word stays. So "Theatrical (DD+ 5.1)" = "Theatrical", "English DTS 5.1 1509 kbps" = no title, while "Audiodeskription", "Hörfilm", "Kommentar", "Isolated score" or "Music only" never go for an untitled main track (the first version treated an empty title as matching any title, which dropped those).
- **Added copies (review fix).** A kept plan that copies the same source stream as an earlier kept plan (Encode audio's add-track copy) is left out entirely: it exists so a target can play the default, it is never dropped and never makes another track a duplicate.
- **Which one stays.** The one made from the best source: lossless (truehd, mlp, flac, alac, `pcm_*`; dts only with a DTS-HD MA profile) before lossy, then more source channels, then higher bit depth, then higher sample rate, then higher bitrate (unknown lowest in each), then the earlier track in output order. When a dropped track was default and no kept audio track is default after the drop, the kept track of its group becomes default (the reason ends `; default audio now audio #1`); otherwise flags stay as they are and the usual normalisation applies.
- **Words.** Tracks are named `audio #N`, N their place among the source's audio tracks (as the job page names them). Reason: `dropped audio #2: same as audio #1 (AAC 2.0 English)`, joined by `; `; with nothing to drop `no duplicate audio` (a no-change step). The plan's note for the dropped stream is `same as audio #1 (AAC 2.0 English)`: `StreamPlan` gains an optional `dropReason`, which `diffFromProbe` uses in place of the language on a drop line. `findings.dropped` lists `{ index, sameAs, codec, channels }` per dropped track (source indices and what it would have written). Job page: `Drops audio #2 (AC-3 5.1 → AAC 2.0): same as audio #1` (several tracks with the same keeper in one clause, `; ` between keepers, `; English audio #1 becomes the default` when the flag moved), `No duplicate audio tracks` when nothing goes.

## 2026-10-22 Verify progress

- **What verify does.** It is not one ffmpeg pass: an ffprobe of the output, bounded packet reads at each output media stream's end (against the source's), decode of the first second, of the end from a keyframe, and of ~10 one-second spots, then (library `verifyFullDecode`) a decode of the whole output. After a decode failure the source is decoded at the same spots. Measured on a 10 min 1080p HEVC file (M-series Mac): each spot decode ~0.2 s, each read ~0.05 s, the checks ~2 s in all; the whole-file decode ~8 s, so minutes on a film or a busy box.
- **What is shown.** The checks are counted, not given as a percent (a probe read and a decode are different amounts of work): `job.progress.verify` = `{ stage: 'checks', done, total }`, total = 1 probe + one per output media stream end + decode start and end + the sampled spots (a spot that cannot be opened counts as done). The bar and the node fill follow `done / total`; the words say `Verifying · 3 of 14 checks` (node, header) and `Checking duration, streams and decode · 3 of 14 done` (detail). The whole-file decode has real progress: ffmpeg `-progress pipe:1` (through `Exec`'s new `onStdout`), its out time against the output's duration, as an encode (`Verifying · whole file 43%`, `Decoding the whole file · 43% · 412 fps · ETA 4 min`), written at most once a second. Re-decoding the source has no measure: `stage: 'source'`, words only (`Verifying · checking the source`), no fill. Replacing starts with no progress.
- `JobProgress.verify` is optional, so encode progress and rows written before it parse as they are. The Jobs list's row bar shows for verifying too; the phone card and the activity ring stay encode only (their head reads the percent as a bare number).

## 2026-10-22 Tidy up the canvas, version banner links

- **Tidy up** (`editor/model/layout.ts` `tidyUp`, button in the canvas controls beside zoom and fit): a small layered layout written here, no library. dagre and elkjs were weighed and left out: the editor needs columns by graph order and branch rows, which take about 200 lines, and a layout library would add a dependency (and, for elkjs, a large bundle) for that.
- **Columns:** a node sits one column after its longest path in, so the trigger (or a sub-workflow's start) comes first and finishes last. A link back to a node still on the depth-first path closes a cycle and is left out of the layering (a draft may hold one until validate refuses it).
- **Rows:** a node continues its first parent's row; a parent's outputs are taken in the catalogue's port order (a condition's "Yes" before its "No", then edge order), and every further branch opens a row below the rows of the branches before it, so a nested branch stays next to its parent. Each chain's columns rise, so no two boxes meet. Unconnected nodes get a row each.
- **Spacing:** 90 px between columns (the TV cleanup example's step minus a card) and 80 px between rows, measured from the cards' real sizes; a size smaller than `NODE_SIZE` (a zoomed-out card measures as a tile) counts as `NODE_SIZE`. Cards in a row are top-aligned, so their file ports line up and straight chains draw straight edges.
- **Groups stay intact:** a group's members are laid out among themselves (edges inside the group only), the box is resized to them plus `GROUP_PADDING`, and the group is placed as one block; its first row of cards lines up with the row it sits in. A collapsed group takes its tile's room (`COLLAPSED_GROUP_SIZE`) and its members move with it (its stored size is the expanded box). Membership, edges, settings and ids never change.
- **Where it lands:** the layout keeps the graph's old top-left corner. It is one plain `apply` (no coalescing), so it is its own undo step and marks the draft unsaved like any move; the button is disabled read-only (a past version) and on an empty canvas. The view fits afterwards (`fitView` with `FIT_VIEW` once the new positions are on the canvas).
- **Version banner:** "Edit it" is a link, and a link's text sat at the top of the 44 px action target while the buttons centred theirs. `.canvas-banner-action` is now an inline flex box centred on both axes, and a past version's two actions sit in one `.canvas-banner-actions` box, so at narrow widths they wrap together, on one line, instead of one by one.

## 2026-10-22 Encode bitrate control

- **Settings.** `core:encode-video` and `core:encode-audio` are at version 2. Encode video gains `rateControl` (`quality`, the default and the old behaviour, or `bitrate`) and `bitrateKbps` (100–200 000, default 6000, used only in bitrate mode); both nodes gain `neverIncreaseBitrate` (default `true`). Migration 1→2 (risk `safe`) sets `neverIncreaseBitrate: false` on a saved node that does not say, so existing workflows encode exactly as before; new nodes, the examples and the TV cleanup example get the cap.
- **Cap, not skip.** The engine plans the whole ffmpeg run before it encodes and writes every stream in that one pass, so "keep the original stream when the encode came out bigger" would need a second encode (or a whole-file keep-original, which the Size gate already is). So the cap is planned: a target bitrate above the source is lowered to it, and quality mode gives the encoder the source bitrate as a ceiling. Audio always knows both numbers, so a track is encoded at `min(set bitrate, source bitrate)`.
- **Source bitrate.** The probed stream's `bit_rate`, else mkvmerge's `BPS`/`BPS-<lang>` statistics tag (probe now reads it), floored to whole kbps. Video falls back to the file's bitrate (an upper bound, as in Video is); the reason says `the whole file's bitrate`. Unknown → no cap, and Encode video's reason says `source bitrate unknown, not capped`. Audio without a known source bitrate is not capped and says nothing (lossless sources mostly).
- **Model.** No new `StreamPlan` field: on video, `bitrateKbps` alone is a target bitrate and `quality` + `bitrateKbps` is capped quality.
- **Rendering (ffmpeg 7.1 sources checked).** Target bitrate: `-b` on every encoder, NVENC with `-rc vbr`, VAAPI without an rc_mode (ffmpeg tries AVBR, VBR, CBR). Capped quality: x264/x265/SVT-AV1 `-crf` + `-maxrate`/`-bufsize` (2× the rate; x264 ignores a maxrate without a buffer); libaom `-crf` with `-b` = the cap (constrained quality instead of `-b 0`); NVENC `-rc vbr -cq <q> -b 0 -maxrate -bufsize` (constqp ignores a maxrate); QSV `-global_quality` with `-b` 1k under `-maxrate` (QVBR; an equal pair selects CBR); VAAPI `-global_quality -b -maxrate` without rc_mode (QVBR where the driver has it, else ICQ/CQP at the quality, uncapped). Quality alone renders as before.
- **Measured (ffmpeg 9.0.2, 30 s 640×360 test source re-encoded from 300 kbps, cap 300k):** x265 crf 18 → 307 kbps, x264 crf 12 → 307 kbps (container overhead included), SVT-AV1 crf 12 → 365 kbps (uncapped 2.2 Mbps; SVT-AV1's max bitrate is a soft cap, a smaller buffer does not tighten it). Target bitrate x265 200k → 200 kbps. libaom was not available locally.
- **Not verified on hardware.** The NVENC/QSV/VAAPI flags follow ffmpeg's rate-control selection code; no GPU run was done.
- **Wording.** Reasons and the plan diff name a capped NVENC quality `cq` and a capped VAAPI one `q` (`qualityName(encoder, capped)`, shared by the diff and Encode video's reason); the diff writes the ceiling as `max 5000k`, a target bitrate as `3000k`. The job page says `in software (libx265, 3000 kb/s)` and `on the GPU (NVENC, CQ 24, at most 5000 kb/s)`.
- **Editor.** The schema fields carry the labels and hints ("Rate control", "Quality (CRF)", "Target bitrate (kbps)", "Never above the source bitrate"). A property may carry `x-transcoda-show-when: { <setting>: <value> }`: the inspector shows it only while that setting has that value (its default when unset), so Encode video shows Quality in quality mode and Target bitrate in bitrate mode (Jan's request). A hidden field keeps its value, and still shows when it has an issue to report. The card's on-node row is the first of `onNodeSettings` (`['quality', 'bitrateKbps']`) the inspector would show. An unset switch now shows the schema default (it read as off), so a node saved without the key (the TV cleanup example, an API- or MCP-made node) no longer shows a default-on setting as off; this applies to every boolean setting.

## 2026-10-22 Library run hours

- **What.** Each library can restrict when its automatic jobs start ("Run hours" card in the library dialog). Off by default (`libraries.run_hours` NULL), so existing libraries behave as before. The window is whole local hours, one strip for every day or one per weekday (Monday first); both strips are stored (`{ enabled, perDay, every, days }`, JSON) so switching mode or unticking Restrict loses nothing. Every day → Per day copies the every-day strip into each day.
- **Automatic vs manual (Decision).** A run is `library` (`runs.source`) when it is a real run that names no workflow, i.e. the library's feeder chose it: "Scan now" and any later automatic scan. Run on a file, Process again, the Dry run/Try/Test-encode actions and every dry run name a workflow or plan only, so they are `manual` and start at once. Existing runs are `manual`.
- **Scheduler.** Each tick reads which restricted libraries are closed now (`closedLibraries`). Their `library` jobs are passed over when picking queued jobs to prepare, and a `library` job already prepared (ready, waiting for its lane) gets no lane while closed; held ready jobs do not count toward the ready limit, so they never block manual jobs. A job holding its lane (running) is never stopped: it finishes after the window closes. One timer wakes the scheduler at the earliest opening among closed libraries, at most an hour ahead (a clock change is caught on the next wake); none when no closed library ever opens. Saving run hours ticks the scheduler.
- **Time.** The server's zone (`Intl` resolved zone: the container's `TZ`, else UTC). Hours are wall-clock: in autumn the repeated hour is open twice when painted; in spring the skipped hour never happens. `libraries.list` returns `clock { now, timeZone }`; the card shows "Now 14:32 (Europe/Berlin), closed until 22:00" and rings the current hour on the strip (today's row in Per day).
- **Held jobs say why.** Job rows carry `heldByRunHours { opensAt, timeZone }` (queued, or prepared and waiting for its lane, while its library is closed); the Jobs list and the job page say "Waiting for run hours · starts at 22:00" ("starts Sat 09:00" on a later day; "none are chosen for this library" when the window is empty). The Scan now dialog says the files are queued and when they start.
- **Migration 0011** (`0011_run_hours`, additive; 0010 is job-before-after's `0010_output_probe`, merged in first): `libraries.run_hours TEXT`, `runs.source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'manual' CHECK (source IN ('manual','library'))`. Another branch adding a migration renumbers on merge as before.

## 2026-10-23 Encode audio updates track titles

- **Why.** A re-encoded track kept its source title, so an AAC 2.0 track could be named `USA / DTS-HD Master Audio / 5.1 / 48 kHz / 2823 kbps / 24-bit` (seen on CT 111).
- **Setting.** `core:encode-audio` is at version 3 with `updateTitles` (default `true`). Migration 2→3 (risk `safe`) sets it `false` on a saved node that does not say, so existing workflows keep their titles.
- **Rule** (`retitle` in `nodes/core/audio-title.ts`). A title's format parts are found as whole words: codec names (longest first, `DTS-HD MA` before `DTS`; an `Audio` after one belongs to it), layouts (`5.1`, `7.1.4`, `stereo`, `mono`, `surround`, `6ch`), bitrates (`kbps`, `kb/s`), sample rates (`kHz`) and bit depths (`24-bit`). Plain numbers never count. The first part becomes the new format: codec name, layout from the channels the encode writes (left out when unknown), then the sample rate and bitrate only when the title named them (libopus always writes 48 kHz), never a bit depth (every Encode audio codec is lossy). The other parts go; separators and brackets left empty are tidied. The new parts are joined with ` / ` when the title joined its format parts that way, else with a space. A title without format parts stays as it is.
- **Scope.** Applies to tracks converted in place (re-encode mode and `whenUnsupported: convert`). The add-track copy keeps its own name (`eng (aac)`). The plan diff already shows the new title.
- **Shared names.** `CODEC_NAMES` moved to `audio-title.ts`; Remove duplicate audio imports it.

## 2026-10-25 The encoder is free once a file is verified

- **Why.** On CT 111 (one RTX 3060, NVENC-bound at about 23x real time) NVENC sat idle about 21% of the time: a job kept the encode lane while it copied its output from /transcode onto the library's disks (about 10–15 s per episode), with four planned jobs waiting.
- **Rule.** *(Superseded by "The encoder is free once a file is encoded": the release now comes before verify.)* `JobControl.releaseEncoder()` (optional): the pipeline calls it once verify passed, before the finish steps (replace, recycle, plugins) and before a test-encode's ending. The scheduler frees the job's encode slot and starts the next encode; the job runs on without a lane and never takes a slot back (a user pause and resume after that only holds its finish steps). Once per job; no effect for a quick job. The job row keeps `lane: 'encode'`.
- **Presets (measured, not changed).** 3-minute 1080p samples at CQ 20, HEVC NVENC, queue paused: p2 encoded 1.15–1.33x faster than medium and wrote 3–30% (mean 17%) more; p1 1.31–1.76x faster and about 22% more. The default stays medium.

## 2026-10-25 Jobs stats count the encoder's work and the server's days

- **Why.** The Jobs page only showed today's saving. "How fast is it going" is files and hours of video per day, not speed per job (that depends on the source); "how much has it saved" is per day for a week plus one all-time number.
- **Processed.** A kept-original file counts as processed (files done, hours of video): the encoder did the work even though the original stayed. Hours come from the source's probed duration.
- **Saved.** Only replaced files (`done`) count toward saved bytes, and a day never goes below 0. Saved all time is the sum over every replaced file; its "since" is the day of the first job result of any outcome.
- **Days.** The 7 days and "today" (the Saved today tile) are the server's local days, from its time zone (`TZ`), never the browser's: the tile and today's bar always agree. A DST day is 23 or 25 hours long.
- **GPU load.** Sampled in memory only, never stored: `nvidia-smi` every 5 s per NVIDIA GPU, the last hour kept. One GPU shows encoder and decoder; several show one encoder line each. No NVIDIA GPU: no graph.

## 2026-10-26 Versioned releases

- **Versions.** Semver from `0.1.0`. While `0.x`: minor releases carry features and anything needing action (listed under Breaking), patch releases only fixes. `1.0.0` once the workflow format, REST API and plugin API are stable. The plugin SDK and plugins are versioned on their own.
- **Cutting.** `pnpm release <version>` (the release-notes script) also sets the version in the root, server, web, shared and contract `package.json` files and refuses a version that is not newer. A release pull request, then a `v<version>` tag on `main` (or a `release/*` branch for a patch from an older release).
- **Publishing.** `release.yml` checks the tag against `apps/server/package.json` and publishes the GitHub Release from the `CHANGELOG.md` section. `docker.yml` adds `{{major}}.{{minor}}` and `latest` (releases only, never pre-releases) to the semver tag.
- **Schedule.** A minor release every four weeks with a milestone per minor version; patch releases when a fix matters; security fixes as soon as they are ready. Steps in `docs/releasing.md`.
## 2026-10-26 The empty list offers the three examples

- **Rule.** The empty Workflows page offers Save space, Play everywhere and Keep my languages (the bundled examples that need no plugin) as cards under "Add a trigger"; each creates a workflow whose version 1 is the example graph. The Examples menu lists the bundled examples and the import. This supersedes the TV cleanup example (M2 Task 16): its graphs remain only as a test fixture (`apps/web/src/test/fixtures/tv-cleanup.ts`, used by the layout tests).

## 2026-10-26 The encoder is free once a file is encoded

- **Why.** The Jobs page encoder graph still showed NVENC idle while a finished file ran its Verifying step (duration, streams and decode checks, and a whole-file decode when the library asks for one). Verify decodes; it does not need the encode slot.
- **Rule.** The pipeline calls `JobControl.releaseEncoder()` as soon as the encode ended well, before the job turns `verifying`. Everything after (verify, a test-encode's ending, the finish steps) runs without a lane. A user pause after that holds the job before verify and before the finish steps; it frees nothing, since the job holds nothing.
- **Retries.** A verify failure is retryable as before: the job is requeued, prepared again and waits for a free slot like any other job (it is a new in-memory job, so its earlier release does not carry over). `source-damaged` keeps the original as before.

## 2026-10-26 Node 24.22.1

- **Why.** Node 24.22.1 (2026-10-20) and 26.12.1 are security releases. The image used the floating `node:24.22-bookworm-slim` tag, so a cached build could still carry 24.22.0.
- **Rule.** Every Node pin names the exact patch: the image's `ARG NODE_IMAGE` and the entrypoint test image are `node:24.22.1-bookworm-slim`, `.node-version` is `24.22.1` (CI and the release-note check read it), and `engines` is `>=24.22.1`. Rebuild deployed images with `--pull`.
- **Not yet 26.** Node 26 is not LTS yet; moving to it is a separate change with a sandbox re-check (`sandbox-expectations.ts` is verified per Node release).

## 2026-10-26 Intel GPUs and GPU order

- **Why.** Jan added an Intel Arc A310 next to the RTX 3060. A new show drops 100+ episodes into the queue; both cards should work through them, NVIDIA first, Intel only when NVIDIA is busy. Design: `docs/plans/2026-10-26-gpu-priority-design.md` (git-ignored).
- **Detection.** NVIDIA GPUs come from `nvidia-smi -L` as before. Intel GPUs are the DRM render nodes (`/sys/class/drm/renderD*`) whose PCI vendor is `0x8086` and that the server can open, sorted by PCI address. Names come from a small device-id table (`56a6` = "Intel Arc A310"), else "Intel GPU <id>". No `/sys/class/drm` (macOS, some containers) means none. `Gpu.vendor` is `nvidia | intel`; Intel GPUs carry `renderNode` and `pci`.
- **Ids.** Setting ids: `auto`, `cpu`, `nvidia:<index>`, `intel:<pci address>` (`intel:0000:07:00.0`); "Encoding device" can pin an Intel GPU like an NVIDIA one (effective capabilities then keep QSV only). Slot ids: `gpu:<index>` (NVIDIA, unchanged) and `gpu:intel:<pci>`. Labels: `GPU 0 · NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060`, `Intel Arc A310 · 07:00.0`. Stored values from before stay valid; migration 0013 only adds the job's `encoded_on` and `failed_gpus`.
- **Order.** `engine.gpuOrder` (Settings → Queue → GPU order, Move up/down) lists GPU ids; the default (empty) is NVIDIA by index, then Intel by PCI address. Ids no longer detected stay in the list, marked "(not found)". Under Auto with two or more usable GPUs (NVIDIA with NVENC, Intel with a QSV encoder) there is one encode slot per GPU in that order (`Encode ×2 (one per GPU)`); otherwise the one slot of before. NVENC without nvidia-smi counts as an unnamed GPU 0, so that setup stays on NVIDIA by default.
- **Fit.** Prepare says what a job needs (`slotFitOf`): `gpu` (an auto-picked hardware encoder, any GPU), `nvidia` / `intel` (a named `*_nvenc` / `*_qsv` encoder, that vendor only), `software`. A waiting `gpu` job takes the first free slot in GPU order; a named job the first free slot of its vendor, and waits for one while a `gpu` job behind it takes the other GPU; a software job takes the last free slot, so it leaves the first GPU to GPU jobs. Look-ahead keeps preparing while a free slot has no ready job that fits it.
- **Auto-pick tracking.** Build still resolves Auto in the old order (NVENC → QSV → VAAPI → software), whatever the GPU order, so a preview may name NVENC for a job that then runs on the Arc. The walker records which streams an `encoder: auto` Encode video picked (server-side, per stream; no SDK change). At Execute, when the slot's vendor differs, those streams swap to the slot vendor's encoder for the same codec (`hevc_nvenc` ↔ `hevc_qsv`, `h264_nvenc` ↔ `h264_qsv`), presets map (`p1`–`p7` → QSV `veryfast`…`veryslow`; QSV `veryfast`/`faster` → NVENC `fast`, `slower`/`veryslow` → `slow`), pixel formats map to `nv12`/`p010le`, quality and bitrate stay, and the plan is recomputed so the job page names what ran. A named encoder never moves; a plugin hook that changes the encoder makes the stream not auto. QSV profiles: `hevc_qsv` main/main10, `h264_qsv` baseline/main/high.
- **Known limits of the inference.** A workflow with two Encode video nodes on one path, Auto and then a named encoder equal to Auto's pick, is treated as auto (the named stream may then move to the Arc). An Auto node that lands on the encoder an earlier named node already set records no pick, so that stream stays where it is (fails safe).
- **Encoded on.** The job records the GPU it encoded on (`encodedOn`, "Encoded on" on the job page): the slot's label, or for the one plain slot the pinned GPU's name (fixed on CT 126: it read "Encode"). Null for a software encode, a slot without a device, or the other vendor's encoder.
- **A failed GPU tries another GPU first** (Jan). A GPU failure (`looksLikeGpuFailure`; now also libvpl/MFX errors and a render node that cannot be opened: "No VA display found for device …", "Device creation failed") sends the job back to the queue with that GPU in `failedGpus` and a warning ("<GPU> failed (…); trying another GPU"); placement skips the failed GPUs. Only when no other GPU fits does it fall back to software in place, as before. A software fallback after the last GPU does not add that GPU to `failedGpus`, so an automatic attempt 2 gives each GPU one more try. "Retry failed" clears the list. A job whose failed GPUs cover every slot it may use (after a settings change) is placed ignoring the list, so it is never stranded.
- **QSV flags (checked on the A310, ffmpeg 8.1.3-Jellyfin).** Every QSV encode on an Intel slot opens that GPU: `-init_hw_device qsv=qsv:hw,child_device=/dev/dri/renderD128 -filter_hw_device qsv`. When every converted video stream is QSV-encoded from the source at a known 8- or 10-bit 4:2:0 format and the model brings no device or hwaccel of its own, the source also decodes on the Arc and stays in GPU memory: `-hwaccel qsv -hwaccel_output_format qsv`, and `-pix_fmt` becomes `-filter:<i> vpp_qsv=format=nv12|p010`. Otherwise the source decodes on the CPU as before. Checked on CT 126: H.264 8-bit → HEVC main and main10, HEVC 10-bit → HEVC main10 (stays 10-bit) and → H.264 high, MPEG-4 (no hardware decoder; ffmpeg decodes it on the CPU and vpp_qsv takes the frames). A 4:2:2 H.264 source fails in the QSV decoder ("Error querying IO surface"), which counts as a GPU failure: another GPU, then software. Rejected: `-hwaccel qsv` without an output format (ffmpeg then defaults to GPU frames and the CPU scaler fails), `-hwaccel_output_format nv12` (fails on 10-bit: "Error synchronizing the operation: -16"), and VAAPI decode with frames back in system memory (works, but on the A310 it cost more CPU than decoding on the CPU: 63–68 s user time against 53–56 s for a 24-min 484×360 episode, because frames go down and up again).
- **Measured (CT 126, 4 cores, 3060 + A310; CT 111 was encoding on the same host and the same 3060 throughout, host load 17–28, so job times vary by ±30 %).**
  - Encoders alone, two 4-min 1080p H.264 episodes (10 Mbit/s): the 3060 one after the other 55.6 s and 52.9 s; the 3060 and the Arc together 28.4 s and 23.5 s (the Arc's episode 15.2 s and 14.3 s); with the Arc decoding on the CPU, as first built, 52.9 s and 41.0 s. Jan's own measurement on a 1080p H.264 Blu-ray source under load: CPU decode then hevc_qsv 2.51× realtime, the GPU path 14.3×; with the CPU decode the Arc job took ~250 % CPU and the NVENC job's encoder load fell from ~100 % to ~50 %.
  - Whole jobs (test encodes through the queue), same pair: with the library's whole-file decode check, 3060 alone 142 s and 168 s, Auto 126 s and 187 s (the box is CPU-bound by the verify decodes: 2–8 % idle); without it, 3060 alone 58 s and 78 s, Auto 53 s and 55 s.
  - Low-resolution dev episodes (24-min 320×240 and 484×360), CPU decode on the Arc: 3060 alone 79, 66, 50 s, Auto 65, 65, 70 s; with the GPU path: 3060 alone 51, 64, 95 s, Auto 57, 72, 76 s. These jobs are verify- and CPU-bound, so a second GPU does not shorten them.
  - So the pass rule holds for the encode itself and for jobs without the whole-file check; with it, the CPU (verify decodes on 4 cores) is the limit. CPU during Auto pairs: 2–15 % idle in the CT.
- **Load graph.** Intel load comes from the i915 per-client counters in `/proc/<pid>/fdinfo/<fd>` (`drm-engine-video`, busy ns over wall ns, every 5 s), for the processes visible in Transcoda's own PID namespace: its own encodes only, not other users of the card. Encoder and decoder share that engine, so one "video" line. An idle Arc has no graph line until its first encode. No privileges and no `intel_gpu_top` (it needs perf events; the host has `perf_event_paranoid = 4`).
- **Queued jobs follow the activation** (checked on CT 126). Six real jobs with a version naming `hevc_nvenc`; while the first encoded, a version with `encoder: auto` was saved and activated. The five that had started preparing ran on the old version on the 3060; the one still queued re-pinned to the new version and ran with `hevc_qsv` on the Arc, at the same time as the 3060's. The pinned-vendor hint in Settings → Queue names active workflows whose Encode video names `*_nvenc`/`*_qsv`, so "the Arc never gets work" is never silent.
- **Passing the Arc in** (CT 126): `dev4: /dev/dri/renderD128,gid=993` in `/etc/pve/lxc/126.conf` (gid 993 = the host's `render` group), `devices: ["/dev/dri/renderD128:/dev/dri/renderD128"]` on the service; the entrypoint adds the node's group to the server. A render node can be passed to two containers at once.
- **Not done.** AV1 on QSV (`AUTO_ENCODERS.av1` stays software), VAAPI, AMD, jobs per GPU (more than one encode per card), per-workflow device choice, and scaling/tonemapping filters on the GPU (Transcoda has no video filters yet; when it gets them, a stream with one must keep the CPU path or use the `*_qsv` filters).

## 2026-10-28 Already processed

- **Why.** Jan wants a condition that answers "has this file already been through workflow X?" in the graph. The queue's own `already_processed` skip only knows the source fingerprint of the same workflow version, and after a replace the file on disk is the output, with a new fingerprint: nothing knew it as Transcoda's output. Jan chose fingerprint plus a tag.
- **Output fingerprint.** `job_results.output_fingerprint` (migration 0015, index `job_results_output_lookup` on `(output_fingerprint, workflow_id, workflow_version)`) holds the `fingerprintKey` of the file the job left at its final path, read once every finish step ran: the replaced original, or the last move-copy destination (the copy for copy, the moved file for move). Only `done` results carry it; null when nothing was written or the file is gone by then (a plugin step may move it). The startup sweep that finishes an installed replace records it too. A replace keeps the original's mtime, but the inode and size are the output's, so the key differs from the source's.
- **The tag (Matroska only).** Every Matroska encode (muxer `matroska` or `webm`, `muxerKeepsTranscodaTag`) carries the container tag `TRANSCODA`, a comma-separated list of `<workflowId>@<version>`, oldest first and newest last: the source's own list (read from the probe's format tags, key matched in any case because Matroska upper-cases names) with the job's entry appended; an identical earlier entry moves to the end; at most 16 entries (the oldest fall off). `-map_metadata 0` already copied the source's tags; `-metadata TRANSCODA=…` overrides the copied one.
- **No tag on MP4/MOV.** A custom key there needs `-movflags use_metadata_tags`, and that flag moves every global tag into `mdta` keys: checked with ffmpeg, the title leaves the iTunes `©nam` atom that Apple, Plex and Jellyfin read. So no mov-family output gets the tag or the flag, and their title stays where players read it. For MP4/MOV outputs the recorded output fingerprint is the only evidence: a file Transcoda wrote as MKV is recognised by its tag even after it is copied or downloaded again; any other file only while it is the exact file the job left. Reading stays case-insensitive and container-agnostic, so a tag already present in any container still counts.
- **Never a reason to write.** The tag lives in the render args, not in the build model, so `isIdentity` and the plan's `rewrite` never see it: a copy-only plan stays `nothing_to_do` and no file is written just to add the tag. Verify compares streams, not format tags, so a tagged output verifies as before (checked with real ffmpeg).
- **The node.** `core:already-processed`, "Already processed", a condition with Yes (`pass`) and No (`else`). Settings: `workflowId` (absent: "This workflow") and `version` (absent: any version). Yes when (a) a real job of that workflow (at that version, if set) ended `done`, `nothing_to_do` or `kept_original` and the file's current fingerprint is its source `fingerprint` or its `output_fingerprint`, or (b) the file's `TRANSCODA` tag lists that workflow (at that version, if set). `failed` never counts (a failed file should be able to run again), dry runs never count, and the asking job's own result is left out. A recorded result wins over the tag (it says more). Reasons: `done by Save space v3 on 2026-10-28 (this file is its output)`, `nothing to do for Save space v1 on …`, `original kept by …`, `done by … (this file is its original)` (the original came back from the recycle folder), `tagged by Save space v2`, `not processed by Save space v2`. The date is the server's local calendar day (`TZ`); findings carry the full time, the job id and which evidence matched.
- **"This workflow" in a sub-workflow.** Results are recorded for the workflow a job runs, never for a sub-workflow, so "This workflow" always means the job's own (top-level) workflow, and a version means that workflow's version, the one the job runs at. A node inside a sub-workflow used by two parents therefore asks each parent about itself. Picking a sub-workflow explicitly is not offered.
- **Editor.** Its own inspector, like Run sub-workflow's: a picker with "This workflow" and the other workflows by name (a deleted one stays selected as "(no longer exists)"), and "Any version" or v1…vN of the chosen workflow; inside a sub-workflow "This workflow" has no known version list, so the version is a number to type. MCP agents get the settings schema from `list_nodes` like any core node.
- **Not done.** No validation issue for a `workflowId` that no longer exists (the node answers No and names the id). The tag is not written by a job that writes nothing or writes MP4/MOV, so such files are known only by their fingerprint.

## Follow-ups (2026-10-17)

- **mp4 video default normalisation.** `normalizeDefaults` treats video like subtitle when the output container is mp4: extra default video flags are cleared and the first kept non-cover video is flagged when none is (note `mp4 cannot have no default video`). The mp4 muxer enables a video track that was told "not default", which verify would report as a stream mismatch. Matroska passes the flag through, so mkv output is unchanged; cover pictures are never touched.
- **Upgrade summary `flagged`** *(Superseded by Activation: `upgradeAll` no longer flags anything; the summary counts `behaviour`.)* counts only workflows that were actually flagged for review. A behaviour upgrade of a sub-workflow counts as `upgraded` but not `flagged` (sub-workflows are never flagged; their parents are, by `flagStaleParents`).
- **`nothing_to_do` has a reason: "already as planned".** The engine writes it on the job row; the UI shows the job's reason as it is. The UI's older wording "already complies with every rule" stays as the fallback for jobs finished before this change (they have no reason).
- **Jobs-row run link and folder pick → run summary** were already in place (`JobsPage` "run" link to `/runs/:runId`; `useTryOnFile` opens `/runs/:runId` when a folder queued several files), so nothing changed.
- **Stale recycle wording** was in the Task 20 "found and left for later" note about the enable dialog (14 days, "Empty means no recycle copy"); it is marked superseded in place.
